# Glossary

One rendering per controlled Latin term, so that what repeats in the Latin repeats in the Portuguese. It grows from the work: add an entry when a word turns out to recur or to collide with a neighbour; put your psalm number beside it. **Status:** `decided` (Gustavo) · `settled` (ruled by Claude overnight under Gustavo's delegation, with reasons in `DECISIONS.md` — use it; he reviews) · `working` (use it; nobody has ruled) · `open` (no settled rendering — propose, and make it a decision in your psalm).

Heavy-duty words get a study in `words/` (`python3.13 research/psalterium/words.py <name> '<regex>' --greek … --pt …`).

## Address and grammar

| | | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| God | *vós*, lowercase pronouns | decided |
| plural humans | *vós* | decided **Ps 10:2 — a cost of *vós* found:** *quómodo dícitis ánimæ meæ* (the advisers, plural) in a psalm that turns to God in 10:4 (*perfecísti*, singular): the Latin tells the two apart by number, Portuguese cannot. *como dizeis à minha alma* was heard by the blind reader FIRST as said to the Lord; *como dizeis vós* (the subject named, set against *o Senhor* of the first colon — D2 allows it) was heard as said to others. The same remedy may serve wherever plural men are addressed next to God (cf. D25 on 7:13). **Ps 13:6 *consílium ínopis confudístis* (the evildoers, plural, between two verses naming the Lord in the third person):** the Ps 10:2 remedy used again — ***vós envergonhastes*** (the subject named); the blind reader heard it as said to the evildoers, not to God. The remedy holds twice now. |
| a singular human addressee | *tu* | decided **Ps 20:9–13** (after 20:8 names the king in the third person, *Inveniátur manus tua … Pones eos*): the addressee is God or the king, and the Latin leaves it open (20:10 then names *Dóminus* in the third person). Kept **vós** — it holds both readings; the blind reader heard the Lord throughout, with the doubt the Latin itself raises at 20:10. *tu* (the king) is one touch away (decision `address`). For Gustavo. |
| God speaking to a single addressee — his Anointed / Son (2:7–9), the king, the just man (90:9–13) | *tu* — **address follows the speaker**: *vós* is the voice of the one who prays to God, not a mark of the addressee's rank; when God speaks he says *tu* (*Tu és meu Filho, eu hoje te gerei*) | settled (D17); was: open — ruled in Ps 2 (decision `address`), wants confirming; will govern 109:1, 109:4, 88:27–28. Does **not** settle the psalmist addressing the Messiah-king (Ps 44). The *vós* form is selectable in Ps 2 (*Vós sois meu Filho … Pedi-me*) |
| *servíre* at the *vós* imperative (*Servíte Dómino*, 2:11, 99:2) | *Sede servos do Senhor* — because the bare *Servi ao Senhor* is also "I served" | settled (D18); was: open — **wants a ruling like D13**: Latinist (twice, minor) and stylist both asked for *Servi ao Senhor*; the object is a noun, so the enclitic exception does not apply; the run of imperatives around it (*entendei … deixai-vos instruir … exultai*) makes the mood plain — if the ban may yield to context, this is the verse |
| possessives | with the article: *o vosso nome*, *a minha alma* | working |
| *vós* imperatives equal to a first-person past (*ouvi*, *abri*, *parti*) | avoid the **bare** form; with an enclitic pronoun the imperative stands (*Abri-me as portas da justiça*, 117:19) | decided (Gustavo); the enclitic exception settled (D13) |

## Terms

| Latin | Portuguese | Status | Note |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Dóminus | Senhor | working | vocative without *ó* unless the Latin has *o* **Ps 8:2a = 8:10 *Dómine, Dóminus noster* → *Senhor, nosso Senhor*:** one word twice, as the Latin and the Greek have it against the Hebrew's two names (MS1932 *nosso Dominador soberano* and DM1962 *nosso Deus* both avoid it). Possessive first, so that the second member is heard as the title *nosso Senhor* in apposition — Portuguese has no case to do what *Dómine / Dóminus* does; *Senhor, Senhor nosso* (the Latin's order) is the option, refused because *Senhor, Senhor* back to back is a doubled cry. The stylist's best line; the Latinist passed it twice; the blind reader did not stop at it. |
| Deus | Deus | working | |
| Altíssimus | Altíssimo | working | Ps 7:18 = 12:6b *nómini Dómini altíssimi* → *ao nome do altíssimo Senhor*: the adjective moved before the noun at the stylist's request, so that the verse closes on an oxytone (the word is a proparoxytone; Ps 90:1 lets it stand at a mediant). Ps 49:14 *redde Altíssimo* → *paga ao Altíssimo*. |
| exaudíre | escutar | settled (D3) | `words/exaudire.md`; Ps 4 moves to it in draft 3 Ps 19:2 *O Senhor te escute*, 19:7b *Ele o escutará*, 19:10 *e escutai-nos* (the versicle). Ps 21:3 *et non exáudies* → *e não escutareis*; 21:25b *exaudívit me* → *escutou-me*. |
| audíre | ouvir | settled (D3) | never at the *vós* imperative (*ouvi*) — recast or use *escutar* there and note it **Ps 118:149 *Vocem meam audi* → *Escutai a minha voz*, as this row directs** — four verses after *exáudi me* → *escutai-me* (118:145), so the two Latin verbs are one there (the Greek keeps them apart too: ἐπάκουσον / ἄκουσον). D13's form *Ouvi-me a voz* is the option; no reader remarked. Ps 33:12 *Veníte, fílii, audíte me* → *Vinde, filhos, ouvi-me* (D13: enclitic, allowed); 33:3 *áudiant* → *ouçam*. **Ps 48:2 *Audíte hæc, omnes gentes* → *Escutai isto, todas as nações*** — the bare *Ouvi isto* at the head of a psalm (rule 3), as this row directs; beside D4's *dai ouvidos*. No reader remarked. **Ps 65:16 *Veníte, audíte* → *Vinde, escutai*** — 48:2's recast of the banned bare *ouvi*; no reader remarked, though 65:18–19 has *escutar* for *exaudíre* twice. |
| inténdere | atender | settled (D3) | stands parallel to *exáudi* (16:1, 54:2, 60:2, 140:1) Ps 5:3 *Atendei à voz da minha oração* — the stylist's best line of the psalm; the blind reader heard "grant what I ask" before "listen" (the cost D3 named, in reverse). Ps 16:1a *Exáudi … inténde* → *Escutai … atendei* — the first of the four parallels this row names; the two verbs stood two; no reader remarked. **Ps 44:5a absolute *inténde, próspere procéde, et regna* → *atende, avança próspero, e reina*** — D3 kept where the Latin has no object (L&S: bow, course, mind; ἔντεινον 'bend'); the blind reader heard 'pay attention', odd before *avança* — the cost, flagged for Gustavo. *estende o arco* and *põe-te a caminho* are options (decision `intende`). **Ps 60:2 *Exáudi, Deus, deprecatiónem meam: * inténde oratióni meæ* → *Escutai, Deus, a minha prece: * atendei à minha oração*** — the third of the four parallels; the opening = 54:2 (rule 6); no reader remarked; the stylist's best line. **Ps 58:6b *Inténde ad visitándas omnes gentes* → *Atendei, para visitar todas as nações*** — absolute, the purpose with *para*; the stylist asked *Aplicai-vos a visitar* (refused, leaves D3; option); the ambiguity reader heard a kind visit rather than a judging one. |
| áuribus percípere | dar ouvidos a (*dai ouvidos às palavras da minha boca*) | settled (D4) | Ps 53:4 proposes it for all eight places (5:2, 16:1b, 38:13, 48:2, 53:4, 83:9, 85:6, 142:1): keeps the ears, stands apart from *escutar* / *ouvir* / *atender* (it is the second member after *exáudi* in four of them, and beside *inténde* in 85:6) and from *inclína aurem*; works with no object (83:9 *dai ouvidos, Deus de Jacó*) and at the *vós* imperative (*dai* ≠ *dei*); cost: in everyday speech it leans to "give credence to" — yet the stylist named 53:4 the psalm's best line and the blind reader found nothing to mishear; alternatives *prestai ouvidos a* (pt-PT 85:6, 142:1), *percebei com os ouvidos*; MS1932 *atender* refused (it is *inténdere*) **Ps 5:2, its first place:** *Dai ouvidos, Senhor, às minhas palavras* — holds; natural order (MS1932 has the same words); Latinist clean, stylist and blind reader silent. It stands there beside *intéllege* → *entendei* and *inténde* → *atendei*, three verbs kept three. **Ps 16:1b *Áuribus pércipe oratiónem meam* → *Dai ouvidos à minha oração*** — a place this row named; D4 held; no reader remarked. **Ps 38:13 *áuribus pércipe lácrimas meas* → *dai ouvidos às minhas lágrimas*** — a place this row named; D4 held; no reader remarked. |
| misericórdia | misericórdia | working | |
| miseréri | ter piedade (*Tende piedade de mim*) | working | Ps 4 |
| justítia | justiça | working | Ps 4 Ps 21:32 *justítiam ejus* → *a sua justiça*. |
| justificatiónes | preceitos | settled (D15) | Ps 118 — `words/ps118-terms.md`. 29 of its 30 places are in Ps 118 (+104:45); δικαιώματα. *justificações* means excuses / the act of justifying in Portuguese and needs a footnote; Matos Soares never uses it; Portuguese Bibles have *mandamentos e preceitos do Senhor* for Luke 1:6 *mandátis et justificatiónibus*. Live alternatives: *decretos* (DM1962), *justificações*. **Not** the singular 118:62 *judícia justificatiónis tuæ* (= δικαιοσύνη): decide it there |
| præcéptum | decreto | settled (D19) | proposed by Ps 118, which never has the word: 8× (2:6, 7:7b, 18:9, 80:5, 93:20, 98:7, 104:10, 148:6), always singular, πρόσταγμα in six of them; frees *preceitos* for *justificatiónes*. If *justificatiónes* moves to *decretos*, this returns to *preceito*. **Ps 2:6, the first verse to meet it, rules that *decreto* holds:** *proclamando o seu decreto* — a king set on Sion proclaims a decree and then quotes it (2:7); neither blind reader nor the Latinist remarked on it. Tried in the other seven places (`ps002/prayed.json`, decision `praeceptum`): it fails nowhere; 18:9 *o decreto do Senhor é luminoso* (the one ἐντολή) is the least natural. Ready to be `settled` **Ps 7:7b, its second place:** *levantai-vos, Senhor, meu Deus, no decreto que mandastes* — holds: a judicial word in a judicial psalm; the Latinist passed it twice, the blind reader heard "rise to carry out / in keeping with the decree"; the stylist asked for *preceito*, refused under D19. **Ps 18:9 *præcéptum Dómini lúcidum* → *o decreto do Senhor é luminoso*** — the list's third place, held; passed every reader. |
| judícia / judícium | juízos / juízo | settled (D15) | Ps 118 — confirmed by the study (23× there, 66× in the psalter; κρίμα and κρίσις merged by the Latin). DM1962 *sentenças* is the option in Ps 118; *julgamentos* refused (rhymes with *mandamentos*) |
| testimónia | testemunhos | settled (D15) | Ps 118 — confirmed (22×; μαρτύρια) |
| mandáta | mandamentos | settled (D15) | Ps 118 — confirmed (38×, the psalm's commonest law-word; ἐντολαί). Long (*os vossos mandamentos*, 8 syllables for *mandáta tua*'s 5): accepted psalm-wide |
| lex | lei | settled (D15) | Ps 118 — confirmed (28×; νόμος) |
| verbum / sermo / elóquium | palavra / **palavra** / ***o que dissestes*** in both numbers (before D26 the plural was *os vossos ditos*) (*segundo o que dissestes*, *guardei o que dissestes*). *sermo*: the main session overruled the agent's *fala* (D15: one Greek word, the stylist refused *falas* three times; verses that set the two side by side decide locally). *elóquium*: the noun *o vosso dito* failed the stylist six times out of six; the clause was the blind reader's own paraphrase and held on a second stylist pass (D16); where the singular must be a noun (118:123, 103:34, 104:19) the verse decides locally, *dito* available | settled (D15, D16, D26) | Ps 118 — `words/ps118-terms.md`. *verbum* and *sermo* are one Greek word (λόγος); *elóquium* is λόγιον. They meet in single verses (55:11, 102:20, 118:42 *verbum* + *sermo*; 147:4 *elóquium* + *sermo*), so three words. *sermo → fala*: L&S "talk, ordinary speech"; 54:22 *as suas falas são mais macias que o azeite*, 138:4 *não há fala na minha língua*. *elóquium → dito*: a thing uttered, God's or a man's (18:15a *os ditos da minha boca*); the least certain of the set — option *promessa* (DM1962) fits the formula *secúndum elóquium tuum* but not 118:67, 158, 172 nor a man's *elóquia*; *oráculo* refused (proparoxytone at ten cadences). Merging all into *palavra* is what Douay-Rheims, Matos Soares and DM1962 did. **Heard (118:1–32):** the stylist refused *falas* three times ("lines in a play", *guardar as vossas falas* "made up") and asked for *palavras*; the blind ambiguity reader understood it at once; *ditos* (118:11) drew no remark from either. *falas* stands under D2 — the ruling of the set most exposed to the ear. **Heard (118:33–80):** the singular *o vosso dito* was refused by the stylist in all six places (118:38, 41, 50, 58, 67, 76), each time for *palavra*; the blind reader understood it everywhere; see the formula row *secúndum elóquium tuum*. 118:42 (*verbum … sermónibus*) decides locally for *uma palavra … nas vossas palavras*. **Heard (118:81–128):** the clause passed the stylist twice and the blind reader (118:82 *à espera do que dissestes*, 118:116 *segundo o que dissestes*); the plural *os vossos ditos* was refused by the stylist twice at 118:103; the noun *o dito da vossa justiça* (118:123, decided locally as D16 asks) refused by him twice, understood by the blind reader; the Latinist marked the clause's past tense minor on his third reading. See the row *elóquium — evidence from 118:81–128* **D26 (draft 17): the clause now serves the plural too — *o que dissestes* in all twenty places of Ps 118 (the stylist refused *ditos* 8 times of 8 and passed the clause at 118:148, 158, 162 when re-read); the one noun left is 118:123 *o dito da vossa justiça*. Outside Ps 118 the psalm decides locally (11:7, 17:31, 104:19, 147:4 have a genitive or are subjects).** **Ps 11:7, the first *elóquium* outside Ps 118 (D26 asked for a local decision): *Elóquia Dómini, elóquia casta* → *As palavras do Senhor são palavras puras*.** Why *palavra* here: the clause cannot repeat as a predicate (the Latin's figure is the noun said twice), so it would drop a repetition (rule 2); draft 1's *Os ditos do Senhor são ditos castos* was refused by the stylist ("máximas moralmente recatadas") — the ninth refusal of plural *ditos*, now with a genitive and an adjective — and the blind reader paraphrased it *palavras puras*; the psalm has no *verbum* or *sermo*, so nothing collides; DRB and MS1932 have *words*. The Latinist passed both drafts. **17:31 *elóquia Dómini igne examináta* should follow (*as palavras do Senhor examinadas no fogo*); 147:4 (*elóquium* + *sermo* in one verse) and 106:11 must still decide locally.** Options kept in Ps 11: *ditos castos / puros*, the clause *O que o Senhor disse é puro*. **Ps 17:31, as D27 ruled: *as palavras do Senhor, examinadas no fogo*** — held; the Latinist passed it; the blind reader heard it rightly. **Ps 18 has all three in eleven verses:** verbum 18:3 (*a palavra*), 18:5 (*as palavras deles*); sermónes 18:4 (*nem palavras*); elóquia 18:15a. Draft 1 kept elóquia apart as D26's clause (*E há de agradar o que disser a minha boca*): **the Latinist marked it MAJOR** (a future act of saying the Latin lacks; *erunt ut compláceant* flattened) and the stylist named it the psalm's worst line; draft 2 took his line, *E as palavras da minha boca serão tais que agradem* — outside Ps 118 elóquia is *palavras* (11:7, 17:31, D27), so the psalm follows them and the three Latin words meet in one Portuguese word here. Passed three gates. **Merged from a duplicate row (the word alone, working beside this settled row, 2026-09-21 review):** verbum — palavra · working · Nunc dimittis (233) *secúndum verbum tuum* → *segundo a vossa palavra* (the same phrase: 118:25, 118:65, 118:107 — 118:25 now translated so, word for word); see the row *verbum / sermo / elóquium* **Ps 32:6 *Verbo Dómini cæli firmáti sunt* → *Pela palavra do Senhor os céus foram firmados*** — kept *palavra*, not explained (the brief); the Trinity versicle (Pent01-0) and Gregory's homily in C1.txt read *Verbum* as the Son, and the blind reader heard the creative word first. *Pelo Verbo* is option 2 in Ps 32's decision `verbo`. **Ps 55:11 decides locally, as D15 asked: *In Deo laudábo verbum, in Dómino laudábo sermónem* → *Em Deus louvarei a palavra, no Senhor louvarei a fala*.** Draft 1's *a palavra … a palavra* was refused by both the Latinist (minor) and the stylist (worst line: 'sounds like a copying slip'); the Greek differs here too (ῥῆμα / λόγον), D15's own test. *a fala* (the stylist's) over *o discurso* (the Latinist's, heard as an oration). 55:5 *sermónes meos* and 55:6 *verba mea* (both τοὺς λόγους μου) stay *as minhas palavras*; the v2 gate asked *falas* at 55:5 (minor) — refused under D15. So *sermo* is *palavra* and *fala* in one psalm: for whoever rules. |
| sémita | vereda | settled (D15) | Ps 118:35 *Guiai-me à vereda dos vossos mandamentos* (118:105 to come) — the blind reader listed *vereda* as unknown, the stylist let it pass; τρίβος, 14× in the psalter, often beside *via* (24:4, 26:11, 76:20); alternatives *senda*, *trilha* Ps 8:9 *as veredas do mar*: the blind reader again listed *veredas* as unknown; kept (D15). Ps 17:46 *a sémitis suis* → *fora das suas veredas*: *veredas* listed as unknown by the blind reader once more; kept (D15). Ps 22:3 *pelas veredas da justiça* (the blind reader listed *veredas* unknown again). Ps 24:4b *e ensinai-me as vossas veredas* — *veredas* unknown to a blind reader again (Pss 22, 24). |
| ǽquitas | equidade | working | Ps 118:40 *na vossa equidade*, 118:75 *os vossos juízos são equidade* (144, 172 to come) — the blind reader listed *equidade* as unknown; keeps *retidão* for *diréctio* (118:7) Ps 10:8 *o seu rosto viu a equidade*: listed as unknown by the blind reader twice more; kept. **Ps 16:2 *óculi tui vídeant æquitátes* (the only plural, grep) → *vejam os vossos olhos as equidades*:** the stylist heard 'as equidades' as technical, the plural unnatural, and asked for *as coisas justas* (refused: justus's word); the blind reader listed *equidades* as unknown (the word's fifth 'unknown'). Draft 2 tried the singular *a equidade*: **the Latinist marked the lost plural MAJOR**, so draft 3 returned to it (as 10:8 *as justiças*). The row's word is now the most often unknown of the working rows; *retidão* is diréctio's, *justiça* justítia's — if it is to change, the candidate that collides with nothing is *o que é reto* (DRB's clause). Ps 36:37 *vide æquitátem* → *vê a equidade*: unknown to the blind reader again (the antiphon *Custódi innocéntiam * et vide æquitátem*); kept. |
| tribulátio | tribulação | working | Ps 4 Ps 31:7 *a tribulatióne* → *contra a tribulação* — applied. **Ps 59:13 *Da nobis auxílium de tribulatióne* → *Dai-nos auxílio na tribulação*** (MS1932): draft 1 *contra a tribulação* (31:7's build) was marked by the v1 Latinist as leaning to the Hebrew; the v2 gate asked for *contra* back. The runs contradict each other; *na* held, *contra* the option — for Gustavo. The colon is a Matins antiphon (*Da nobis * Dómine auxílium de tribulatióne*, Psalmi matutinum). 107:13 copies. |
| mirificáre | fazer maravilhoso | working | 4:4, 15:3, 16:7, 30:22; must not collide with *magnificáre* → engrandecer Ps 15:3 *mirificávit omnes voluntátes meas in eis* → *fez maravilhosas todas as minhas vontades neles* (no subject supplied; the blind reader heard the Lord). The stylist wanted *tornou maravilhosas* — refused for this row (4:4), kept as an option. Ps 16:7 *Mirífica misericórdias tuas* (named in this row) → *Fazei maravilhosas as vossas misericórdias* (the imperative; the plural kept). **The stylist asked for *Tornai maravilhosas* — the second stylist in two psalms (15:3, 16:7) to hear *fazer maravilhoso* as translated**; refused for the row, his verb is option 3 in Ps 16. The Latinist passed it three times. For whoever rules the row: *tornar maravilhoso* keeps the -ficáre as well as *fazer* does. Ps 30:22 *mirificávit misericórdiam suam mihi* → *fez maravilhosa a sua misericórdia para mim*: the stylist again called the build a calque (*mostrou de modo maravilhoso*, refused, option); the blind reader did not stumble; passed the Latinist twice. |
| magnificáre | engrandecer | working | Ps 11:5 *Engrandeceremos a nossa língua* (the boast in the Latin's image; MS1932 explains it) — echoed by 11:4 *magníloquam* → *que fala grandezas*. Ps 17:51 *Magníficans salútes Regis ejus* → *Engrandecendo as salvações do seu Rei* (the plural kept; the participle as a gerund, dangling as the Latin's does). Ps 19:6 *magnificábimur* → *seremos engrandecidos* (the Latin; the LXX has μεγαλυνθησόμεθα). **Ps 40:10 *magnificávit super me supplantatiónem* → *fez grande contra mim a rasteira*:** a thing made great, not praise or boasting; draft 1's *engrandeceu … a sua rasteira* was the stylist's worst line (no such collocation) and *engrandeceu* was unknown to the blind reader there. Local; *engrandecer* stays the row's word. |
| sanctus (ὅσιος) | santo | working | Ps 4 Ps 31:6 *orábit ad te omnis sanctus* → *todo santo orará a vós* (the stylist's order). The blind reader heard the canonized saints first, as at 30:24; kept; *cada santo*, *todo homem santo* (MS1932) options. |
| compúngi | compungir-se | open | Ps 4; stylist prefers *arrepender-se* **Ps 29:13 *et non compúngar* → *e eu não seja compungido*** (draft 2): draft 1's reflexive *não me compunja* (4:5's form) drew the Latinist's minor (the voice), the stylist's worst line («exige decifração») and the blind reader's unknown word; the passive participle keeps the Latin's voice and is current (*coração compungido*). The stylist's *não seja ferido* (the image, plainer) is the option. So the row now has the reflexive for the imperative (4:5 *compungi-vos*) and the passive for the passive. Ps 34:16 *nec compúncti* → *e não compungidos* (the passive participle, 29:13; the second *foram* dropped with the Latin's ellipsis, after the stylist). The blind reader listed *compungidos* unknown; kept. The stylist's *não sentiram remorso* is option 4. |
| in idípsum | a um só tempo | open | 4:9, 33:4, 40:7, 61:10, 73:6, 121:3 — may not bear one rendering everywhere **Ps 33:4 *et exaltémus nomen ejus in idípsum* → *e exaltemos juntos o seu nome*** (ἐπὶ τὸ αὐτό, the Greek of *in unum*; Hebrew יחדו): persons acting together, so *juntos*, as *in unum*; *a um só tempo* would say simultaneity. Ruled locally under deferred D9; passed the Latinist twice, heard rightly. **Proposal for the row:** persons acting together → *juntos*; one agent or a thing → *a um só tempo* (4:9). **Ps 40:7b *et loquebátur in idípsum* (one man speaking; the Greek puts ἐπὶ τὸ αὐτό with the next verse's whispering) → *e falava no mesmo sentido*** (DRB 'to the same purpose'): neither *juntos* (a plural) nor *a um só tempo* fits one speaker. Ruled locally; the blind reader heard both readings the Latin allows (the same talk; in agreement with the others); the stylist's *do mesmo modo* (manner) is an option; the Latinist passed it twice. Third local rendering: evidence that D9 cannot be one word. **Ps 61:10 *ut decípiant ipsi de vanitáte in idípsum* → *para que eles mesmos, pela vaidade, enganem juntos*** — persons acting together, as 33:4 (*juntos*); *a um só tempo* option. The purpose clause made finite after the stylist (draft 1 *para eles … enganarem* heard as 'for them'); *mesmos* = *ipsi*. **Ps 73:6 *excidérunt jánuas ejus in idípsum* → *cortaram juntos as suas portas*** — many axemen, so *juntos* (the 33:4 / 61:10 use); the same Greek (ἐπὶ τὸ αὐτό) as 73:8 *simul → juntos*. v2 moved it after the verb: at the mediant the stylist heard it trailing with no clear referent. |
| singuláriter | a sós | open | Ps 4 |
| constitúere | estabelecer | working | Ps 4; Matos Soares: *firmar* Ps 8:6 *e o estabelecestes sobre as obras das vossas mãos* (= Heb 2:7) — holds with *super*; no reader remarked. |
| speráre in | esperar em | settled (D36) | Ps 90:2, 90:4, 90:14 — the blind ambiguity reader heard it first as *waiting for* God, second as trusting (`ps090/critic/v1.ambiguity.json`); kept, but whoever rules on it should know Ps 7:2 *em vós esperei*: the blind reader heard hope first, waiting second. Ps 20:8 *rex sperat in Dómino* → *o rei espera no Senhor* (heard as trust first — the stylist's best line). Ps 24:20 *porque esperei em vós*, apart from sustinére → *esperar por* (24:3, 24:5, 24:21). Ps 35:8 *in tégmine alárum tuárum sperábunt* → *esperarão ao abrigo das vossas asas* — the blind reader heard waiting first again. |
| confitéri (to God) | dar graças a (*darei graças ao vosso nome*) — proposed by Ps 53:8 in place of the pencilled *louvar* | settled (D5); the noun *conféssio* stays open | heavy — *confessar* / *dar graças* / *louvar* / *celebrar*; still wants a word study; *confessar* for sins (31:5). Ps 53's grounds: *confitéri* stands in one verse with *laudáre* (34:18, 108:30, 105:47), so *louvar* would make one word of two; *grátias ágere* never occurs in the psalms, so *dar graças* is free; *confitéri* to God always has a dative and *dar graças a* keeps it; *Dai graças* is safe at the *vós* imperative (*dai* ≠ *dei*). Costs: the root of confessing is faint; the noun *conféssio* will not always follow (*ação de graças* fits 41:5, 94:2, 99:3b, 146:7, not 95:6, 103:1, 110:3, 148:14a). Ps 117 follows this until Gustavo rules **Ps 6:6, the test D5 named:** *no inferno, porém, quem vos dará graças?* — holds: the dative is kept (*vos*), it is as natural as MS1932's and DM1962's *louvará*, and it closes on a paroxytone; the Latinist passed it twice, neither the stylist nor the blind reader remarked on it. **Ps 7:18, a place the coordinator named:** *Darei graças ao Senhor segundo a sua justiça* — holds (MS1932 *Glorificarei*, DRB *give glory*); passed the Latinist twice, no remark from the stylist or the blind reader. Ps 17:50 *Proptérea confitébor tibi in natiónibus, Dómine* → *Por isso vos darei graças entre as nações, Senhor* — D5 held; no reader remarked. **Ps 29 has it three times:** 29:5 *confitémini memóriæ sanctitátis ejus* → *dai graças à memória da sua santidade* (the blind reader found the dative to a 'memory' a little odd, and understood it), 29:10b *Numquid confitébitur tibi pulvis* → *Acaso o pó vos dará graças* (6:6's question again), 29:13 *in ætérnum confitébor tibi* → *para sempre vos darei graças*. D5 held; no reader objected. **Ps 31:5b — confitéri of sin → *confessar* (D5):** *Confitébor advérsum me injustítiam meam Dómino* → *Contra mim mesmo, confessarei ao Senhor a minha injustiça* (draft 2, the stylist's order: the blind reader heard draft 1's *confessarei contra mim a minha injustiça* as an injustice done to me). Ps 34:18 *Confitébor tibi in ecclésia magna, * in pópulo gravi laudábo te* → *Eu vos darei graças na grande assembleia, * num povo numeroso vos louvarei* — D5's named verse. gravis → *numeroso* (the Latinist, major on draft 1's *grave*; the blind reader heard 'gravely ill'). Ps 41:5b the noun, *exsultatiónis, et confessiónis* → *de exultação e de ação de graças* (the family beside the refrain's *darei graças*); the stylist's *louvor* (laus's) option 2. **Ps 66:4 = 66:6 *Confiteántur tibi pópuli, Deus: confiteántur tibi pópuli omnes* → *Que os povos vos deem graças, Deus: * que todos os povos vos deem graças*** — the third-person wish with *que* (grammar, D2; the anaphora audible); *Deem-vos graças os povos* (the Latin's order) is the option. The Latinist asked *louvem* (minor, both verses) as broader than thanks; held under D5, option. **Ps 73:19 *ánimas confiténtes tibi* → *as almas que vos dão graças*** — the latinist twice asked for *louvam*; held (D5; *louvar* is 73:21's *laudábunt*). **Ps 75:11 *cogitátio hóminis confitébitur tibi* → *o pensamento do homem vos dará graças*** — D5 held; the v2 Latinist asked *vos louvará* (minor, 'narrowed to thanksgiving') — refused under D5, an option. |
| salutáre / salus | salvação | settled (D6) | whether the two must differ; Ps 90:16 *salutáre meum* → *a minha salvação*; Nunc dimittis (233) *salutáre tuum* → *a vossa salvação*, matched — the blind reader heard it as "someone or something that brings the Lord's salvation", not as God being saved Ps 11:6b *Ponam in salutári* → *Porei na salvação* (no object, as the Latin; the blind reader supplied "the poor" without stumbling, twice). |
| ánima mea | a minha alma | working | Ps 53:5–6 shows the cost: in *quæsiérunt ánimam meam* (the idiom for seeking someone's death) the blind reader heard the spiritual soul first and "my life" second; MS1932, pt-PT and DM1962 write *vida* there; kept, with *vida* as an option |
| in sǽculum | para sempre | settled (D23) | refrain of 105, 106, 117: *quóniam in sǽculum misericórdia ejus* |
| in ætérnum | para sempre | settled (D23) | **D23: one with *in sǽculum* — one Greek phrase (εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα), merged by Douay-Rheims and Matos Soares 1932; the refrain of Ps 135 will be that of Ps 117. *Eternamente* is the option in Ps 118.** Before D23: kept distinct from *in sǽculum* on purpose. **Ps 118:89, 93, 98, 111, 112** (one slot, decision `in_aeternum`): the Latinist and the blind reader passed it, *Eternamente não esquecerei* (118:93, = never) included; the stylist never named the word, but **four of his proposed lines put *para sempre* in its place** (118:98, 111 twice, 112), and its position in 118:111 had to move twice. Evidence that the ear leans to *para sempre*; merging would cost the distinction from *in sǽculum* (118:44 has that) — for the main session, with Ps 135 in view **D23 held at 118:142, 144, 152, 160** (slot `{aet}` of decision `in_aeternum`): *A vossa justiça é justiça para sempre* — the stylist's best line of the portion on both readings; *são equidade para sempre*; *que os fundastes para sempre*; *todos os juízos da vossa justiça são para sempre* (natural order, from the stylist: *para sempre são todos …* hung until its subject came). No reader of four Latinist, two stylist and two blind readings remarked on the phrase. Ps 11:8 *desta geração para sempre* — D23 applied. **Ps 14:5b — D23 under a negation:** *para sempre não será abalado* (the 118:93 build). The Latinist passed it twice and the blind reader heard 'never'; the stylist refused it as foreign and asked for *jamais* — for Gustavo's ear: this is where D23 costs most. **5:12a** *exultarão para sempre* (Ps 5 draft 4; drafts 1–3 had *eternamente*, made before D23 and never swept — review 2026-09-21). |
| in sǽculum sǽculi | pelos séculos dos séculos | settled (D27) | **Ps 9:6 = 9:37 *in ætérnum, et in sǽculum sǽculi* → *para sempre, e pelos séculos dos séculos*** (one slot in Ps 9's decision `saeculi`): the Latinist asked for the singular *pelo século do século* five times (9:6 ×3, 9:37 ×2; major once, as part of a major); the stylist asked twice to reverse the order for the cadence. Held both; wants the row settled. **D27: settled — the plural is how the phrase lives in Portuguese; number is grammar (D2).** Ps 20:5 *in sǽculum, et in sǽculum sǽculi* → *para sempre, e pelos séculos dos séculos*; 20:7 *in sǽculum sǽculi* at the head of the verse (*Porque pelos séculos dos séculos fareis dele uma bênção*). The Latinist's minors on the plural came on draft 1 and again at the gate — refused (D27). Ps 18:10 *pérmanens in sǽculum sǽculi* → *permanece pelos séculos dos séculos*: the Latinist asked four times (minor) for *pelo século do século*, the stylist once for a cadence change — both refused (D27). Ps 21:27 *vivent corda eórum in sǽculum sǽculi* → *viverão os seus corações pelos séculos dos séculos*: the Latinist's minor twice (singular), refused (D27); the stylist asked for the formula first (proparoxytone final) — refused, the Latin's own cadence; option in decision `saeculi`. Ps 36:27, 36:29 → *pelos séculos dos séculos*: the Latinist asked the singular (minor) at both, twice (drafts 3 and 4); the stylist asked to move the formula off the final (proparoxytone). Both refused under D27. |
| beátus vir | bem-aventurado o homem | settled (D19); was: open — Ps 1:1 rules for it and asks for a settling | Ps 1 (decisions `beatus`, `vir`). *beátus* 29× (grep), nearly always first in its verse; the Latin psalter never has *felix*. *Bem-aventurado* because *feliz* is first a mood today and *beátus* is a verdict on a life (L&S quotes Cicero's definition), because it is the one word heard as a macarism, and because it serves every construction (143:15 *beátum dixérunt*, 40:3 *beátum fáciat*); MS1932 has it in 1:1 and 2:13, Ps 118:1–2 already uses it; neither blind reader remarked on it. Cost: 5 syllables for 3, at the head of the verse where it is cheapest. **Live alternative: *feliz*** (DM1962; the plainer word, which is what D2 favours — the ruling most worth Gustavo's eye); *ditoso* refused as literary. *vir → homem*: merges with *homo* (*Beátus homo* 83:13, 93:12); *varão* is the only way to keep them apart and is archaic Ps 31:2 *Beátus vir, cui non imputávit Dóminus peccátum* → *Bem-aventurado o homem a quem o Senhor não atribuiu pecado*; 31:1 plural *Beáti quorum* → *Bem-aventurados aqueles cujas* (the colon +8, D19's cost; = Rom 4:7). |

| adjutórium | auxílio | working | Ps 90; *Deus, in adjutórium meum inténde* will want the same word Ps 7:11 *Justo é o meu auxílio vindo do Senhor* — the Latin's reading, not the Hebrew's shield; the blind reader heard "the help is just" before "the helper is just". |
| suscéptor | amparo | settled (D19) | Ps 90:2; recurs 3:4, 17:3c, 41:9, 45:8, 53:6, 58:10, 61:3, 88:27, 118:114, 143:2; must stay apart from *protéctor* → protetor; MS1932 *defensor*; Ps 53:6 tries it with a genitive (*o amparo da minha alma*) and it holds — alternatives there *arrimo* (DM1962), *defensor* **Ps 17:3c *e o meu amparo*: held against the Latinist — minor on draft 1 (*amparador*), MAJOR on draft 2 with the words unchanged and another fix (*aquele que me ampara*).** The pair noun/verb is heard across the psalm: 17:36a *déxtera tua suscépit me* → *a vossa direita me amparou* (one decision `susceptor`, two slots). **Merged from a duplicate row (the pair, beside the rows it combined, 2026-09-21 review):** suscéptor + suscípere together — amparo … amparar · settled (D19); was: open — Ps 3 finds the pair holds; ready to settle · Ps 3:4 *sois o meu amparo* … 3:6 *porque o Senhor me amparou* (= 117:13b word for word): the one psalm with noun and verb two verses apart (Greek ἀντιλήμπτωρ … ἀντιλήμψεται), and the echo is heard; the abstract noun sits well beside *a minha glória*; no reader remarked on it. See the rows *suscéptor* and *suscípere* Ps 29:2 *quóniam suscepísti me* → *porque me amparastes* (the Matins antiphon and the versicle of 12-08 and of Our Lady Aparecida sing this colon, grep); passed the Latinist twice, heard rightly. Ps 45:8 = 45:12 *suscéptor noster Deus Jacob* → *o nosso amparo é o Deus de Jacó* — applied; the blind reader listed *amparo* as unknown. **Ps 58:10 = 58:18 *quia, Deus, suscéptor meus es* → *porque vós, Deus, sois o meu amparo*** (the vocative before the verb, as the Latin; the stylist heard *sois, Deus, o* stutter); 58:17b *factus es suscéptor meus → vos fizestes o meu amparo*. 58:18 is the respond of the Epi2-3 responsory. Ps 61:3 *suscéptor meus* → *o meu amparo*; the Latinist (v1, minor) asked *protetor* — refused (*protéctor*'s). |
| protéctio / protégere | proteção / proteger | working | Ps 90:1, 90:14 Ps 17:36a *protectiónem salútis tuæ* → *a proteção da vossa salvação* (the Latin's *tuæ*, not the Greek's μου). Ps 60:5 *prótegar* → *serei protegido* (future passive; the Latinist at the gate noted a jussive reading is possible — the Greek is future); MS1932 *abrigar-me-ei* the option. |

| liberáre | livrar | working | Ps 90:3, 90:14 |
| erípere | *libertar* where no source is named (6:5 *libertai a minha alma*, 21:9, 33:8, 90:15, 118:153, 118:170); *arrancar* where a *de …* follows or the source is named (16:13, 17:18, 30:16, 31:7, 34:10b …) | open | Ps 90:15; kept apart from *liberáre* (MS1932 and DRB merge them); works at the *vós* imperative (*Arrancai-me*); alternative *libertar*; Ps 53:9 *ex omni tribulatióne eripuísti me* → *de toda tribulação me arrancastes* **Ps 6:5 finds a place where it fails:** *éripe ánimam meam* with no source named — the only such *éripe* of the 17 in the psalter (grep) — would be *arrancai a minha alma*, "tear out my soul". Ps 6 has *libertai a minha alma* (this row's own alternative; DM1962's verb; safe: past *libertei*). Wherever a *de …* follows, or the object is *me*, *arrancar* is untouched. If one verb is wanted everywhere, *libertar* is the one that never fails; it stays apart from *liberáre* → livrar only by a suffix. **Ps 118:153, 170 — *éripe me* with NO source named** (*Vide humilitátem meam, et éripe me*; *secúndum elóquium tuum éripe me*): the row said that with *me* as object *arrancar* is untouched, but *e arrancai-me* with nothing to be torn from leaves the sentence hanging, as *arrancai a minha alma* did in Ps 6. Ps 118 has *libertai-me* in both (the Diurnal's verb at 153; safe); passed all readers. Evidence for the row's own last sentence: *libertar* is the verb that never fails. Ps 17:18, 30, 44, 49 — four times, each with a source named (*de inimícis*, *a tentatióne*, *de contradictiónibus*, *a viro iníquo*) → *arrancar* throughout; no reader remarked, and *serei arrancado da tentação* (17:30) was heard rightly. Ps 16:13 *éripe ánimam meam ab ímpio* → *arrancai a minha alma do ímpio* — a source named, so *arrancar* as the row says; no reader stumbled (the stylist wanted *tirai*, refused). **Ps 21:9 *erípiat eum* (no source named; the mockers' words) → *que ele o liberte*** — the row's own conclusion (*libertar* never fails), with *livrar* left to 21:5 *liberásti*. Mt 27:43 (Vulgate, fetched) has *liberet nunc, si vult eum* — another wording. See the new row *éruere*. Ps 30:16 *Éripe me de manu inimicórum meórum* → *Arrancai-me da mão dos meus inimigos* (a source named); no reader remark. Ps 33:5 *ex ómnibus tribulatiónibus meis erípuit me* → *e me arrancou de todas as minhas tribulações* (draft 2 the verb first, the stylist's rhyme); 33:8 *erípiet eos* → *e os libertará*. Passed the Latinist twice. **90:15 *erípiam eum* → *eu o libertarei*** (Ps 90 draft 3, review 2026-09-21): the one place left with *arrancar* and no source; its decision had argued that *na tribulação* supplies one, before the evidence above existed. **Ps 55:13 *eripuísti ánimam meam de morte, et pedes meos de lapsu* → *arrancastes a minha alma da morte, e os meus pés da queda*** — sources named, so *arrancar*; no reader remarked. **114:8** (*erípuit ánimam meam de morte … pedes meos a lapsu*) should copy it. **Ps 49:22 *et non sit qui erípiat* → *e não haja quem liberte*** — no source, no object: the row's *libertar*. The Latinist and the stylist both asked *livre* (liberáre's word) — refused; *arranque* (the row's other verb; the snatching image) and *livre* kept as options. **Ps 63:2 *a timóre inimíci éripe ánimam meam* → *arrancai a minha alma do temor do inimigo*** — a source named, so *arrancar*; no reader remarked (*libertai* the option). **Ps 58:2, 58:3 *Éripe me de inimícis meis … de operántibus iniquitátem* → *Arrancai-me dos meus inimigos … dos que praticam a iniquidade*** — the row applied twice, apart from 58:2b *líbera me → livrai-me*; 58:2 is the Passiontide versicle (Preces, Quad5-5) and each colon stands alone. No reader remarked. Ps 68:15 *Éripe me de luto* → *Arrancai-me da lama*; 68:19 *propter inimícos meos éripe me* → *libertai-me* (no source) — the gate Latinist (minor) asked *arrancai-me* for one verb; held under the row. **Ps 70:1 *et éripe me* → *e libertai-me*** (no source; beside *líbera me* → *livrai-me*, two Greek verbs); **70:4 *éripe me de manu peccatóris* → *arrancai-me da mão do pecador*** (source named); **70:11 *non est qui erípiat* → *não há quem liberte*** (= 49:22, no object supplied; the v1 stylist asked *o liberte*, held, option). |

| verúmtamen | todavia | open | Ps 90:8; 31:6, 38:6–7, 38:12, 48:16, 61:5–6, 61:10, 67:22, 72:18, 84:10, 139:14 Ps 31:6b *Verúmtamen in dilúvio aquárum multárum* → *Todavia, no dilúvio das muitas águas* — applied. **Ps 38:6b, 38:7, 38:12b — three times in one psalm, *Todavia* each time** (the row; the stylist: 'the three todavia give it a sober spine'). | tabernáculum | tenda | open | Ps 90:10; the same word must serve God's tent (14:1, 30:21, 60:5) **Ps 14:1 *Dómine, quis habitábit in tabernáculo tuo?* → *Senhor, quem habitará na vossa tenda?*** — God's tent, as this row foresaw; passed the Latinist twice; the blind reader heard 'living with the Lord' first and the concrete tent second. *no vosso tabernáculo* (MS1932) is the option. 31 lines (grep), God's and men's alike (77:51 *in tabernáculis Cham*, 83:11 *tabernáculis peccatórum*), which *tabernáculo* (a church's, in Brazil) could not serve. **Ps 18:6a *In sole pósuit tabernáculum suum* → *No sol pôs a sua tenda*** — the Latin's reading (the tent *in* the sun; the Hebrew's is *for* the sun); passed every reader; *tabernáculo* (MS1932) the option. **Ps 42:3 *et in tabernácula tua* → *e às vossas tendas*** (plural kept; the blind reader heard market stalls — noted, the row stands). Ps 41:5 *in locum tabernáculi admirábilis* → *ao lugar da tenda admirável*; the blind reader heard tent first (*casa de Deus* beside it). **Ps 45:5 *sanctificávit tabernáculum suum Altíssimus* → *o Altíssimo santificou a sua tenda*** — the row applied. **For Gustavo:** the colon is sung alone of the Virgin (antiphon *Adjúvit eam Deus mane dilúculo; sanctificávit tabernáculum suum Altíssimus*, Immaculate Conception 12-08 and Our Lady Aparecida Brasilia/10-12; Dedication 12-10-Domus), where *tabernáculo* (option) carries the Marian sense. The blind reader heard *tenda* as a shop or stall as a second reading. **Ps 60:5 *Inhabitábo in tabernáculo tuo in sǽcula* → *Habitarei pelos séculos na vossa tenda*** (the row's named place; the adverb moved so the colon does not end on *séculos*); the blind reader heard a camping tent as well. The colon is the Tract of 01-31 and is adapted in an All Saints antiphon (11-01) — *tabernáculo* the option. Ps 59:8 *convállem tabernaculórum* → *o vale das tendas* (the Latin translates the place name; MS1932 *vale dos Tabernáculos* option). |
| flagéllum | flagelo | settled (D39) | Ps 90:10 Ps 31:10 *Multa flagélla peccatóris* → *Os flagelos do pecador são muitos* (the copula supplied; subject first so the colon does not rhyme with *Senhor* at the final). The blind reader listed *flagelos* as unknown and heard 'the punishments the sinner receives'; kept. Ps 34:15 *congregáta sunt super me flagélla* → *juntaram-se sobre mim os flagelos*; the blind reader listed *flagelos* unknown again (heard calamities); kept. **Ps 38:10 *plagas tuas* → *os vossos flagelos*: plaga here is μάστιγας, the Greek of flagélla (31:10, 34:15), so D15 gives one word (MS1932, DRB 'scourges').** *flagelos* unknown to the blind reader again (heard punishments / plagues, which is the sense); *golpes* is option 2. plaga elsewhere (63:8 wounds, 72:4) decides locally. **Ps 63:8 *plagæ eórum* (πληγαί, a wound; D39 left it local) → *as feridas deles*** — *golpes* the option. |
| mandáre | dar ordem | open | Ps 90:11; loses the link with *mandáta* → mandamentos; MS1932 *mandar*. Ps 118:4 *Tu mandásti mandáta tua* is translated with *mandar* (*Vós mandastes que os vossos mandamentos…*) because there the link is the line; 118:138 will want the same — so either *mandar* everywhere or *dar ordem* except beside *mandáta* **Ps 7:7b *in præcépto quod mandásti*:** a noun object, which *dar ordem* cannot take → *no decreto que mandastes* (the row's first alternative, *mandar*, of one family with *mandamentos*). The stylist heard *mandar um decreto* as sending it and asked for *ordenastes* (option there, and the Ps 2 agent's trial wording); the blind reader heard "o decreto que emitistes". Three candidates now — *dar ordem*, *mandar*, *ordenar* — wants a ruling; 12 verses (grep: 7:7b, 32:9, 41:9, 77:5, 77:23, 90:11, 104:8, 110:9a, 118:4, 118:138, 132:3b, 148:5). **Ps 118:138 *Mandásti justítiam testimónia tua*: with a bare object *Mandastes os vossos testemunhos* was heard by the blind reader as "you SENT reports"** — *mandar* is two verbs in Brazil. *Mandar que* + subjunctive (as 118:4) is safe but the Latinist marked the added clause major; the verse now reads *Ordenastes como justiça os vossos testemunhos* (heard as "Determinastes"). So: *mandar* only with *que* or beside *mandáta*; with a thing as object, *ordenar*. **Ps 32:9 *ipse mandávit, et creáta sunt* → *ele ordenou, e foram criadas*** — no object, and *mandou* alone is heard 'sent' or colloquial; *ordenar* as 118:138. 148:5 is the near twin (formula row). Passed the Latinist twice. Ps 41:9 *mandávit Dóminus misericórdiam suam* → *o Senhor mandou* — with a thing as object *dar ordem* cannot stand (as 7:7b); *mandar* holds 'command' and 'send'. Ps 67:29 *Manda, Deus, virtúti tuæ* → *Dai ordem, Deus, ao vosso poder* (dative); heard rightly. |
| custodíre | guardar | working | Ps 90:11 Ps 16:4 *ego custodívi vias duras* → *eu guardei caminhos duros* (as 17:22 *guardei os caminhos do Senhor*): the blind reader was unsure what *guardar caminhos* means (followed? watched? remembered?), heard 'followed' first. 16:8a *custódi me* → *Guardai-me* (15:1 had left the verb free for it). Ps 18:12 *custódit ea, in custodiéndis illis* → *os guarda, em guardá-los*; the blind reader heard 'obeys' first. **Ps 38:2 *Custódiam vias meas* → *Guardarei os meus caminhos*; 38:2b the noun *Pósui ori meo custódiam* → *Pus guarda à minha boca*** — the Latin's (and Greek's) echo kept by verb and noun of one root. The blind reader heard 'be careful how I live' first. **140:3 *Pone, Dómine, custódiam ori meo* should copy the noun.** **Ps 70:10 *qui custodiébant ánimam meam* (hostile watchers) → *os que guardavam a minha alma*** — the row held; the ambiguity reader heard protectors first (a betrayal). Options *vigiavam* (DRB), *espreitavam* (MS1932's sense). |
| via | caminho | working | Ps 90:11 |
| conculcáre | calcar aos pés | settled (D44) | Ps 90:13; 55:2–3, 56:4, 138:11; plainer alternative *pisar* **Ps 7:6: *calque* failed two readers** — the stylist ("pouco corrente") and the blind reader (unknown word) — so Ps 7 has *pise aos pés na terra a minha vida*: this row's plainer verb with *aos pés* kept, because bare *pise na terra* is heard as treading the earth. Proposed for the row: *pisar aos pés*; Ps 90:13 still has *calcar*. **Ps 55:2–3 *conculcávit me homo … Conculcavérunt me* → *me calcou aos pés … Calcaram-me aos pés*** — draft 1 had the proposed *pisar aos pés*; the Ps 55 stylist refused it in both verses as two idioms crossed and asked for *calcar aos pés*, the set phrase (= 90:13, MS1932). Taken: Ps 7's readers stumbled on the rare subjunctive *calque*, not on the idiom. Proposal for the row: *calcar aos pés*, with Ps 7:6's *pise aos pés* as the one local exception (subjunctive). Passed the Latinist gate. **Ps 56:4 *conculcántes me* → *os que me calcam aos pés*** (D44); listed as unknown (*calcam*) by the blind reader; Latinist clean twice. |
| clamáre | clamar | working | Ps 4, Ps 90:15 |
| glorificáre | glorificar | working | Ps 90:15 Ps 14:4a *timéntes autem Dóminum gloríficat* → *mas glorifica os que temem o Senhor* — a man glorifying men; *honra* (MS1932, DM1962) is the option; passed the Latinist twice. |
| benedícere (man → God) | bendizer; accusative → *bendizei o Senhor*, dative → *bendizei ao Senhor* | working | Ps 133; the Latin has both cases (acc. 15:7, 33:2, 133; dat. 67:27, 102:20–22, 113:26, 134:19–20) and Portuguese keeps them at no cost; *bendizei* is safe at the *vós* imperative |
| benedícere (God → man) | bendizer (*O Senhor te bendiga*) | open | Ps 133:3; keeps the psalm's one verb (*benedícite… Benedícat*); every parallel has *abençoar*; recurs 66:7b–8, 127:5 — wants Gustavo's ruling; the stylist named the line the psalm's best and the blind reader understood it as a blessing **Ps 5:12b tests it without Ps 133's support:** *porque vós bendireis ao justo* (dative kept) — the Latinist passed it three times, neither the stylist nor the blind reader remarked on it. It can stand alone; *abençoareis o justo* (MS1932, DM1962) is the option there. **Ps 28:11 *Dóminus benedícet pópulo suo in pace* → *o Senhor bendirá o seu povo em paz*** — the row's verb, a people as object (the Latin's dative made direct, as current Portuguese construes *bendizer*); the stylist's best line; the blind reader heard a blessing. *abençoará* is the option. **Ps 27:9 *et bénedic hereditáti tuæ* → *e bendizei a vossa herança*** — the psalm has the turn inside it (27:6 *Bendito o Senhor*); no reader remarked. **Ps 66:2 *et benedícat nobis* → *e nos bendiga*; 66:7b *Benedícat nos Deus, Deus noster, benedícat nos Deus* → *Deus nos bendiga, o nosso Deus, Deus nos bendiga*** — the row's verb three times; both colons are said alone (the Lauds antiphon; the Matins blessing and Pent01-0 responsory). Subject first at 66:7b from the stylist (the enclitic *Bendiga-nos Deus* sounded translated and could be heard as said to God). *abençoe* is one decision away. No reader objected to *bendiga*. |
| ecce | eis | working | Ps 133:1a *Ecce nunc* → *Eis agora*; MS1932 / pt-PT *Agora, pois* |
| servus | servo | working | Ps 133:1a, Nunc dimittis (233) |
| stare | estar de pé | open | Ps 133:1b (= 134:2); 121:2 *Stantes erant pedes nostri*; pt-PT flattens to *estar* Ps 17:39 *nec póterunt stare* → *e não poderão estar de pé*; no reader remarked. Ps 23:3 *aut quis stabit in loco sancto ejus?* → *ou quem estará de pé no seu lugar santo?* — the blind reader heard standing in God's presence, the posture audible too (both the Latin's). **Ps 37:12, 12b *appropinquavérunt, et stetérunt … de longe stetérunt* → *e ficaram de pé … ficaram de longe*** (the Latinist's v1 fix): draft 1 *se detiveram* (1:1's *deter-se*) was marked minor twice, heard by the blind reader as 'were detained', and *se detiveram de longe* was unidiomatic to the stylist. So *deter-se* only for halting on a road (1:1). |
| átrium | átrio | working | Ps 133:1b; the blind reader listed it as unknown; 28:2, 64:5, 83:2, 83:11, 91:14, 95:8, 99:3b, 115:9, 121:2, 134:2 **Ps 28:2 *adoráte Dóminum in átrio sancto ejus* → *adorai o Senhor no seu átrio santo*** (= 95:8 word for word, grep — formula row). The Ps 28 blind reader listed *átrio* as unknown too (the second reader to do so); kept, the Latin's word; the Latinist passed it twice. |
| domus | casa | working | Ps 133:1b |
| extóllere | erguer | open | Ps 133:2 (from the Diurnal Monástico); 27:2 *extóllo manus meas*, 74:6; keeps *levantar* for leváre (120:1, 122:1) and *elevar* for eleváre (140:2) **Ps 27:2 *dum extóllo manus meas* → *enquanto ergo as minhas mãos*; 27:9 *extólle illos* → *erguei-os*** — applied, the psalm's two places one verb (the Te Deum's R). **Ps 74:6 *Nolíte extóllere in altum cornu vestrum* → *Não ergais para o alto o vosso chifre*** — applied; the ambiguity reader listed *ergais* unknown (the form, not the verb). |
| sancta / sanctum (a place) | o lugar santo | open | Ps 133:2 *in sancta*; kept apart from *sanctuárium* → santuário (72:17, 73:7, 82:13, 88:40); the Latinist wants the plural (*os lugares santos*), refused because in Brazil that names the Holy Land sites; *in sancto* (21:4, 59:8, 62:3, 76:14) is not always a place and is not settled by this **Ps 19:3 *de sancto* → *do lugar santo*** (the blind reader heard an earthly sanctuary first, heaven second — the Latin's two senses; 19:7b has *de cælo sancto suo* for heaven); the Preces feriales adapt the verse (formula row). **Ps 21:4 *Tu autem in sancto hábitas* → *Mas vós habitais no lugar santo*** (the row, DRB, MS1932; the Greek's plural ἐν ἁγίοις not followed). The apposition *laus Israël* → *vós, louvor de Israel*: bare, it was heard as the holy place's name. Ps 23:3 *in loco sancto ejus* → *no seu lugar santo*. **Ps 59:8 *Deus locútus est in sancto suo* → *Deus falou no seu santo*** (v2): the Latinist and the stylist both said *no seu lugar santo* settles as a place what the bare neuter leaves open (place / holiness / holy one); the stylist's word-for-word *no seu santo* taken and passed the gate. *no seu lugar santo*, *no seu santuário* options. The row stands where *in sancto* is plainly a place (19:3, 21:4). **Ps 62:3 *sic in sancto appárui tibi* → *assim no lugar santo me apresentei a vós*** — the row; no reader remarked. Ps 67:18 *in Sina in sancto*, 67:25 *qui est in sancto* → *no lugar santo* (beside a place name / after *está*); no remark. |
| nox, *in nóctibus* | *Durante as noites* | working | Ps 133:2; plural kept; stylist + MS1932 |
| dimíttere (let go) | deixar ir | open | Nunc dimittis (233); *despedir* a servant is to fire him in Brazil; the Magnificat's *dimísit inánes* is the other sense and need not match |
| in pace | em paz | working | Ps 4:9, Nunc dimittis (233) Ps 28:11 *benedícet pópulo suo in pace* → *em paz*: the blind reader heard 'giving peace' first, 'while they are at peace' and 'peacefully' second — the Latin's range. |
| paráre | preparar | working | Nunc dimittis (233) Ps 64:7 *Prǽparans*, 64:10b *parásti … præparátio* → *Vós que preparais … preparastes … a sua preparação*: *præparáre* and *paráre* one verb, the noun its own (the psalm's play kept; MS1932's *Dás firmeza aos montes* interprets). |
| fácies, *ante fáciem* | face, *diante da face de* | open | Nunc dimittis (233); the Hebraism kept (DRB 'before the face of'); alternative *à face de* **Ps 67 *a fácie* seven times → *à face de* (67:3 twice, fire and God; 5b; 9 twice), 67:2 *fujam da sua face*** — *diante da face de* put 67:3b seven syllables over; the fire/God echo kept. Passed the Latinist twice; no reader remarked. |
| gentes | nações | working | Nunc dimittis (233); chosen so that it can serve the whole psalter; *gentios* is the alternative for the canticle alone **Ps 42:1 singular *de gente non sancta* → *de uma nação não santa*** — the cognate *gente* barred (*da gente* = 'from us' in Brazil); the stylist's *de um povo* (pópulus's word) kept as option. |
| pópuli | povos | working | Nunc dimittis (233) |

Ps 52:5, 52:7 copied from 13:4, 13:7 as named. |
| lumen | luz | working | Ps 4:7, Nunc dimittis (233); collides with *lux* → luz (no second plain word) **Ps 35:10b *et in lúmine tuo vidébimus lumen* → *e na vossa luz veremos a luz*** — one word twice, as the Latin; heard as God's illumination. The stylist's best line. |
| revelátio | revelação | working | Nunc dimittis (233); the genitive kept open (*a revelação das nações*) |
| glória | glória | working | Nunc dimittis (233) |

| Deus (vocative) | *Deus,* bare | open | Ps 53:3–4; the *Senhor* rule extended (no *ó* unless the Latin has *o*); MS1932, pt-PT and DM1962 all write *ó Deus*; will serve *Deus, in adjutórium meum inténde* Ps 5:11a *julgai-os, Deus* follows it; the colon is four syllables for the Latin's seven, the first place where *ó Deus* would help the chant (option there). 5:3 *meu Rei e meu Deus*. **Ps 66:4 = 66:6 *pópuli, Deus*: the stylist asked *ó Deus* twice** (clipped at the mediant; mid-colon in 66:6 *graças, Deus, que* read as a list). Held bare under this row; *ó Deus* is the option (decision `vocDeus`). Second place after 5:11a where the chant would welcome *ó*. **Ps 69:2, 69:6 bare *Deus* kept** (the Hour versicle, *Deus, atendei em meu auxílio*; *Deus, auxiliai-me*); *ó Deus* (MS1932, DM1962) is option `deusvoc`. No reader remarked. |
| salvum fácere | salvar (*salvai-me*) | working | Ps 53:3; the formula *Salvum me fac* (11:2, 68:2) and *salvum fac* (19:10); merges with *salváre*, as the Greek does (σῴζω); safe at the *vós* imperative (*salvai* ≠ *salvei*); calque *fazei-me salvo* refused Ps 11:2 *Salvai-me, Senhor* — the formula *Salvum me fac, Dómine* (also 117:25 *O Dómine, salvum me fac*). Ps 59:7 *salvum fac déxtera tua* (no object) → *salvai com a vossa direita* — the Latin's missing object not supplied (*salvai-me* DRB, *salvai-nos* MS1932 options); no reader remarked. 107:7 copies. |
| judicáre, *júdica me* | julgar (*julgai-me*), bare | open | Ps 53:3; 7:9b, 25:1, 34:24, 42:1 (*Júdica me, Deus, et discérne causam meam* — *causa* is a word of its own there); the blind reader heard "pass sentence on me" before "do justice to my cause" — the Latin is open in the same way; MS1932 *julga a minha causa* Ps 5:11a *júdica illos* → *julgai-os*: heard by the blind reader first as "condemn them" — the Latin's range. Ps 7:9b *Julgai-me, Senhor, segundo a minha justiça* (= 25:1's opening), 7:8b *o Senhor julga os povos* — here the sense is plainly "do me justice"; no reader heard condemnation. **Ps 118:154 *Júdica judícium meum*: *Julgai o meu juízo* was heard by the blind reader FIRST as "evaluate the opinion I formed"** (*o meu julgamento* runs the same risk). Now ***Julgai em juízo a minha causa*** (from the stylist): the law-word and the figure kept, the right sense heard; passed the Latinist twice. Cost: *causa* is another Latin word — 9:5 *judícium meum et causam meam* and 34:23 set the two side by side and must decide locally. |
| virtus (δύναμις), *in virtúte tua* | poder | open | Ps 53:3, for the singular *in virtúte tua* only; heavy (72 verses; *Dóminus virtútum*, 117:16 *fecit virtútem*) — wants a word study; must stay apart from *fortitúdo* and *poténtia* (64:7, 67:36); *virtude* is a moral quality today; DRB *strength* **Ps 17:33, 17:40 *præcínxit me virtúte … præcinxísti me virtúte ad bellum* → *me cingiu de poder … me cingistes de poder para a guerra*** — the row's word, kept apart from 17:2 *fortitúdo* → *força* in the same psalm (DRB and MS1932 have *strength / força* here, which would merge them). Passed the Latinist twice; no other reader remarked. Ps 20:2 = 20:14a *in virtúte tua* → *no vosso poder*; **20:14b the plural *virtútes tuas* → *os vossos poderes*** (the Latinist's MAJOR on the singular *ao vosso poder*: plural, and the object sung, not the addressee). **Ps 21:16 *Áruit tamquam testa virtus mea* (ἰσχύς) → *O meu vigor secou-se como um caco*:** a man's bodily strength drying up — *poder* would be authority, *força* is fortitúdo's. MS1932's *vigor*. **Proposal: virtus of a man's own strength → *vigor*** (30:11 *infirmáta est … virtus mea*, 37:11 *dereliquit me virtus mea*, 70:9 *dum defécerit virtus mea* — grep before use); *poder* stays for God's (*in virtúte tua*) and for 17:33, 40. Passed the Latinist twice; no reader remarked. **Ps 23:10 *Dóminus virtútum* → *o Senhor dos poderes*** (its first place of 16: 45:8, 45:12, 47:9, 58:6, 67:13 *Rex virtútum*, 68:7, 79:5, 8, 15, 20, 83:2, 4, 9, 13, 88:9 — grep); the plural as 20:14b. Passed the Latinist twice; the blind reader heard 'the Lord who has all powers' (or 'of powerful beings' — the Greek's δυνάμεις, the hosts, are both). Options *dos exércitos* (DRB, the Hebrew's Sabaoth), *das virtudes* (MS1932). Formula row proposed; wants a ruling before Ps 45. **Ps 28:4 *Vox Dómini in virtúte* → *A voz do Senhor com poder*; 28:11 *Dóminus virtútem pópulo suo dabit* → *O Senhor dará poder ao seu povo*** (God's virtus given; one word for the psalm's two places). Passed the Latinist twice; no reader remarked; the stylist named 28:11 the best line. *força* (DRB) the option. **Ps 29:8 *præstitísti decóri meo virtútem* → *destes vigor ao meu esplendor*** — the proposal *vigor* for a man's strength applied (given by God to the man's decor, not God's own power); passed the Latinist twice; *poder* the option. **Ps 37:11 *derelíquit me virtus mea* → *abandonou-me o meu vigor*** — the proposal *vigor* applied at a place it named; passed the Latinist twice, no reader remarked. **Ps 45:2 *Deus noster refúgium, et virtus* → *O nosso Deus é refúgio e poder*** — God's virtus → *poder*, kept apart from 45:4 *in fortitúdine ejus* → *força* two verses later; the familiar *refúgio e força* (MS1932, DRB) is the option. Passed the Latinist twice. **Ps 43:10 the plural of the people's hosts *in virtútibus nostris* → *com as nossas forças*** (v2): draft 1 *poderes* was heard abstract by the blind reader, and the Latinist and the stylist asked *forças* independently. See the row *virtútes nostræ*. **Ps 47:14 *in virtúte ejus* (Sion's strength, αὐτῆς) → *no seu poder*** — a city's virtus, which the row did not cover; *força* (MS1932, DRB) the option. Passed the Latinist twice. **Ps 64:7 *Prǽparans montes in virtúte tua, accínctus poténtia* → *Vós que preparais os montes com o vosso poder, cingido de potência*** — the instrumental *in* as *com* (the stylist heard *no vosso poder* as locative); the formula keeps *no* where it is locative (20:2, 53:3). *poténtia* in the same verse → *potência* (new row). Ps 70:9 *cum defécerit virtus mea* (ἰσχύς, a man's strength) → *quando desfalecer o meu vigor*, as 21:16, 30:11b, 37:11. |
| aliénus | estranho | working | Ps 53:5; wider than *estrangeiro* (anyone not one's own) **Ps 17:46 *Fílii aliéni … fílii aliéni* → *Os filhos estranhos … os filhos estranhos*:** the blind reader heard 'other people's children' or 'odd children' first and did not perceive foreigners — inside the row's 'not one's own'; kept, *filhos alheios* and *filhos de estrangeiros* are options. **Ps 18:13 *et ab aliénis parce servo tuo* → *e dos alheios poupai o vosso servo*** — three drafts: *das estranhas* (draft 1, the faults of others with *faltas* understood) failed three readers at once (Latinist MAJOR, the stylist, the blind reader, all hearing 'odd faults'); *das alheias* (draft 2) failed the gate MAJOR because **18:14b's masculine *domináti* makes its subject persons** (delícta would give *domináta*); *dos estranhos* (draft 3, persons) failed the gate MAJOR for closing out others' faults. *dos alheios* (the Latinist's fix twice) holds both, as the masculine/neuter *aliénis* does, and passed. So: *estranho* for persons named as such (17:46 *filhos estranhos*); a bare substantival *aliéni* may want *os alheios*. **Ps 57:4 *Alienáti sunt peccatóres* → *os pecadores se fizeram estranhos*** — the verb kept in this row's family; the cognate *se alienaram* is modern jargon. The blind reader heard 'odd people' as well as estrangement; *se alienaram* option. |
| fortes (κραταιοί) | os fortes | working | Ps 53:5; open between strong and powerful, as the Latin is Ps 58:4 *irruérunt in me fortes* → *lançaram-se sobre mim os fortes*; the ambiguity reader heard powerful men. |
| insúrgere in / advérsum | levantar-se contra | open | Ps 53:5; 3:2, 17:40, 17:49, 26:12, 43:6, 58:2, 91:12, 108:28; 85:14 is a near-twin of 53:5 (*insurrexérunt super me … quæsiérunt ánimam meam: et non proposuérunt te in conspéctu suo*) — whoever does Ps 85 should echo it; shares *levantar-se* with God's *exsúrgere*, told apart by *contra*; alternative *insurgir-se* Ps 17:40, 17:49 (both in this row's list): *os que contra mim se levantavam* (the stylist's order), *acima dos que se levantam contra mim*; passed all readers. |
| quǽrere | buscar | working | Ps 4:3, Ps 53:5 *quæsiérunt ánimam meam* → *buscaram a minha alma* Ps 23:6 *quæréntium … quæréntium* → *dos que o buscam … dos que buscam* (the repetition kept). requírere (24:10) takes *procurar*, apart. |
| conspéctus, *ante conspéctum suum* | vista, *diante da sua vista* | open | Ps 53:5; kept apart from *óculus* → olho, which comes in 53:9; *in conspéctu* is everywhere and may want *diante de*; MS1932 *diante de si*, DRB *before their eyes* **Ps 5:9 proposes *in conspéctu* + genitive → *à vista de* (*endireitai o meu caminho à vossa vista*)**: keeps this row's noun and the seeing; 51 verses have *in conspéctu* (grep). Tried on 22:5 *Preparastes à minha vista uma mesa*, 137:1b *À vista dos anjos*, 115:15 *Preciosa à vista do Senhor* — it fails none. In Ps 5 it stays apart from *astábo tibi* → *diante de vós* and *ante óculos tuos* → *diante dos vossos olhos*. The blind reader heard "make my conduct right in your eyes" first. Options *na vossa presença*, *diante de vós*. **Ps 118:168–170 — *in conspéctu tuo* three verses running, two of them with a verb of MOTION** (*Appropínquet … in conspéctu tuo*, *Intret … in conspéctu tuo*): *à vossa vista* is excellent in 118:168 (*todos os meus caminhos estão à vossa vista*) and impossible in 118:170 (*Entre o meu pedido à vossa vista*). Ps 118 has the noun phrase ***a vossa presença*** in all three, each verb taking its own preposition (*estão na … · Aproxime-se … da … · Entre … na …* — the stylist refused *Aproxime-se na*); passed thereafter. For whoever studies the 51 verses: *à vista de* fails with verbs of motion. **Ps 9:20 *in conspéctu tuo* → *à vossa vista*, 9:26a *Non est Deus in conspéctu ejus* → *Não há Deus à vista dele*:** the noun kept; the blind reader heard 9:26a as 'God does not count for him' — the sense. Evidence for *vista* over *diante de*. **Ps 14:4a *in conspéctu ejus* → *À vista dele*:** the stylist asked for *Aos olhos dele* ('à vista' sounded like physical presence) — refused, *olho* is óculus's; the blind reader heard 'aos olhos do homem descrito', the judging look, rightly. Evidence for *vista* again (9:20, 9:26a, 14:4a). **Ps 17 has *in conspéctu* four times in 17:2–25 (one decision `conspectu`):** *à vista de* where it is a predicate (17:23 *todos os seus juízos estão à minha vista*; 17:25 *à vista dos seus olhos*), *na sua presença* where it hangs beside a noun (17:7b *o meu clamor, na sua presença, entrou nos seus ouvidos*; 17:13 *Diante do clarão, na sua presença, passaram as nuvens*). In 17:13 the genitive *o clarão da sua presença* was refused by the Latinist (major): the phrase is adverbial, not the brightness's owner. Evidence that *à vista* serves predicates, *presença* the rest. **Ps 15:8 *Providébam Dóminum in conspéctu meo semper* → *Eu via o Senhor sempre à minha vista*** (the Latinist's line); the stylist's *diante de mim* refused for this row's noun, kept as an option. Acts 2:25 quotes it (fetched). Ps 16:15 *apparébo conspéctui tuo* (a dative, no *in*) → *aparecerei à vossa vista* — the row's noun as a predicate place; the blind reader heard 'appear before God as a just man'. **Ps 21 (one decision `conspectu`, three slots): 21:26 *vota mea reddam in conspéctu timéntium eum* → *à vista dos que o temem*** (the place of witnesses); **21:28b *adorábunt in conspéctu ejus* and 21:30 *in conspéctu ejus cadent* → *na sua presença*** (with an act of worship; *adorarão à vista dele* is stiff) — the Ps 17 split again. No reader remarked. Ps 22:5 *in conspéctu meo* → *à minha vista* (the row's noun, as 5:9's *à vista de*). Ps 35:3 *in conspéctu ejus* → *à sua vista* (whose — God's or his own — left open, as the Latin; the blind reader heard God's). **Ps 55:9 *posuísti lácrimas meas in conspéctu tuo* → *pusestes as minhas lágrimas à vossa vista*** (the Wednesday Matins versicle's response); *à vista de* applied; the stylist named 55:9 the psalm's best line. Ps 49:3b *in conspéctu ejus* → *na sua presença*; 49:8 *in conspéctu meo* → *na minha presença* (God's sight: as 17:13, 21:30); *à vista dele / à minha vista* options. Ps 60:8 *Pérmanet in ætérnum in conspéctu Dei* → *Ele permanece para sempre na presença de Deus* (abiding before God, as 49:8); *à vista de Deus* the option. Ps 67:4, 5b *exsúltent in conspéctu Dei* → *exultem na presença de Deus* (joy, not being watched); 67:8 *egrederéris in conspéctu pópuli tui* → *saíeis à vista do vosso povo*. No remark. |
| adjuváre | auxiliar (*Deus me auxilia*) | open | Ps 53:6; keeps one family with *adjutórium* → auxílio and *adjútor* (117:7 next); plainer alternative *ajudar* splits the family. Ps 118:86, 117 *auxiliai-me* (safe at the imperative); no reader remarked Ps 21:12 *non est qui ádjuvet* → *não há quem auxilie* (no object, as the Latin); no reader remarked. **Ps 39:14b *Dómine, ad adjuvándum me réspice* → *Senhor, olhai para auxiliar-me*** (draft 1 *para me auxiliar* was heard 'look at me' by the stylist; the enclitic after the infinitive mends it). This is the half that Ps 69:2b shares (*Dómine, ad adjuvándum me festína*, the response of every Hour's opening versicle): it fixes *auxiliar-me* there, not *inténde in adjutórium* (D40, deferred). The stylist's *olhai em meu auxílio* refused: adjutórium's noun, and it would pre-empt D40. **Ps 45:6 *adjuvábit eam Deus* → *Deus há de auxiliá-la*** — draft 1 *Deus a auxiliará* lost the object in choir (*a au-*, the stylist); the periphrastic future keeps the enclitic audible. **Ps 69:2b *Dómine, ad adjuvándum me festína* → *Senhor, apressai-vos para auxiliar-me*** (39:14b's phrase kept whole; *para*, not 30:3's *a*, to avoid *a au-*); **69:6 *Deus, ádjuva me* → *Deus, auxiliai-me*** — the stylist asked *socorrei-me* (heavy at the urgent cry): refused, it breaks the psalm's *adjuvándum … ádjuva … Adjútor* echo; option `adjuva`. No reader remarked on 69:2b. |
| avértere (+ dative) | desviar … para | open | Ps 53:7 *Avérte mala inimícis meis* → *Desviai os males para os meus inimigos*; safe at the *vós* imperative (*desviai* ≠ *desviei*); can serve *avérte fáciem tuam*; MS1932 *Faze recair … sobre* **Ps 12:1 *avértis fáciem tuam a me* → *desviais de mim a vossa face*** (the formula; 9 lines with *fáciem tuam a*: 26:9, 29:8, 50:11, 68:18, 87:15, 101:3, 142:7b …; 9:32 *desviou a sua face* in Ps 9 agrees). *Desviar* keeps *avértere* one verb with or without a dative; the Latinist passed it twice. **Ps 13:7 *cum avérterit Dóminus captivitátem plebis suæ* → *quando o Senhor afastar o cativeiro do seu povo*:** draft 1 had this row's *desviar*; the stylist found *desviar o cativeiro* not Portuguese and the blind reader could not tell whether the captivity was present or threatened. *Afastar* (the stylist's) taken there — it shares a verb with *amovére / repéllere* (row *amovére*), which never meet *captivitátem*. *Desviar* stays right with a face (12:1) or anger (77:38, 105:24 *ut avérteret iram*). **84:2 *avertísti captivitátem Jacob* should follow Ps 13 (*afastastes o cativeiro de Jacó*). The twin 52:7 has *convérterit* (row *convértere*) and should differ.** Ps 21:25b *Nec avértit fáciem suam a me* → *Nem desviou de mim a sua face* (the face formula, 12:1). |
| dispérdere | exterminar | working | Ps 53:7; about fifteen verses, twin line 142:11b; **not** *dispérgere* (scatter, 58:12); *destruir* fails rule 3 (*destruí-os* = I destroyed them); MS1932, pt-PT Ps 11:4 *Extermine o Senhor todos os lábios enganadores* — the jussive with the subject after it; the blind reader heard "destroy the people who deceive". Ps 17:41 *et odiéntes me disperdidísti* → *e exterminastes os que me odeiam*. Ps 36:9 *exterminabúntur* (exterminári, ἐξολεθρευθήσονται) → *serão exterminados* — the cognate, shared with dispérdere: the two never meet in a verse (exterminári only 36:9, 79:14, grep). Ps 36 keeps the Latin's other verbs of ending apart: períre → perecer, disperíre → *perecer de todo* (36:22, 38; 82:11 to follow), interíre → *extinguir-se* (36:38; 48:18 of a man will want *morrer*). |
| voluntárie | de livre vontade | working | Ps 53:8; DM1962 *por livre vontade*, DRB *freely* |
| sacrificáre | sacrificar | working | Ps 4:6, Ps 53:8 *sacrificarei a vós*; the verb is kept so that *sacrificáre sacrifícium* (4:6, 106:22) can repeat; the stylist's *oferecer sacrifício* is an option in Ps 53 |
| despícere (enemies) | olhar de cima | open | Ps 53:9 *olhou de cima para* (with *super*); 91:12, 111:7b, 117:7 (accusative → *olharei de cima os meus inimigos*); one idiom with both the literal and the contemptuous sense; with *óculus* as subject it jingles (*o meu olho olhou*) — alternative there *viu de cima*; MS1932 *olhar com desprezo* |
| óculus | olho, singular where the Latin is singular | working | Ps 53:9 |
| latitúdo, *in latitúdine* | amplidão (*me escutou na amplidão*) | open | Ps 117:5 (from the stylist); a noun for the noun, nothing supplied; serves 17:20 *edúxit me in latitúdinem*, 118:45 *ambulábam in latitúdine*; *largueza* was heard by both blind readers as generosity; keeps *lugar espaçoso* free for 30:9 *in loco spatióso*; compare 4:2a *dilatásti mihi* → *me abristes espaço* **Ps 17:20 *Et edúxit me in latitúdinem* → *E me fez sair para a amplidão*** — the verse this row named; the stylist's best line of stage one; the blind reader heard a wide place and freedom. 17:37 *Dilatásti gressus meos* → *Alargastes* (same image, another word). Ps 30:9 *in loco spatióso* → *num lugar espaçoso*, as this row reserved. |
| invocáre | invocar | working | Ps 117:5 Ps 19:8 *invocaremos em nome do Senhor*; 19:10 *no dia em que vos invocarmos*. Ps 41:8 *Abýssus abýssum ínvocat* → *O abismo invoca o abismo*. |
| adjútor | auxílio (*O Senhor é o meu auxílio*) | open — for Gustavo (HANDOFF). The text is uniform, *auxílio* 14/14; D19 names *adjútor → auxílio* only in passing, as what *amparo* stays apart from — it is not a ruling, so the row stays open | Ps 117:6–7; one family with *adjutórium* and *adjuváre*; the Latinist asked twice (minor) for the agent noun *auxiliador* — refused for length and currency, kept as an option; the same metonymy as *suscéptor* → amparo. **Ps 118:114 *Vós sois o meu auxílio e o meu amparo*: the Latinist asked for *auxiliador … protetor* three times — minor, then MAJOR twice with the text unchanged; refused each time (*protetor* is protéctor's; D19)** — the final text stands against the gate there **Ps 9:10, 9:35b *auxílio … vós sereis auxílio para o órfão*:** the Latinist marked 9:10 MAJOR twice (drafts 2 and 3) for *auxiliador*; held on purpose (D24), his word is the option in Ps 9's decision `adjutor`. **Ps 17:3b *O meu Deus é o meu auxílio*: the Latinist asked for *auxiliador* again — minor on draft 1, MAJOR on draft 2 with the words unchanged; held (D24), recorded in Ps 17's decision `adjutor`.** Ps 17:2–3 sets four agent nouns side by side (*liberátor, adjútor, protéctor, suscéptor*): the Portuguese makes two agents (*libertador, protetor*) and two abstracts (*auxílio, amparo*). If Gustavo wants the series regular, Ps 17's decisions `adjutor` and `susceptor` turn both in one touch each. **Ps 18:15b *Dómine, adjútor meus, et redémptor meus* → *Senhor, meu auxílio, e meu redentor* — HELD against four Latinist MAJORS (drafts 1–4), each asking *meu auxiliador*:** here the agent noun *redentor* stands beside it and the metonymy is most exposed (77:35 has the same pair, *Deus adjútor est eórum: et Deus excélsus redémptor eórum est*). Held for D19 and the psalter's one word; **needs a ruling** — proposal: keep *auxílio* in the formula places (*O Senhor é o meu auxílio*) but allow *auxiliador* where adjútor is paired with another agent noun (18:15b, 77:35), or settle *auxílio* everywhere and record the gate's objection as D24 does. **Ps 29:11 *Dóminus factus est adjútor meus* → *o Senhor se fez o meu auxílio* — HELD against two Latinist MAJORS (drafts 1 and 2) asking *auxiliador*,** as 9:10 (*se fez refúgio … auxílio*), 17:19's build (*se fez o meu protetor*). No reader misheard it. Another place for the ruling this row asks. **Ps 26:9b *Adjútor meus esto* → *Sede o meu auxílio*** (a versicle, 05-01r.txt) — the Latinist's MAJOR *auxiliador* held again (D19/D24), as at 29:11. 27:7 (minor) the same. **Ps 27:7 *Dóminus adjútor meus … et adjútus sum* → *O Senhor é o meu auxílio … e fui auxiliado*** — the Latinist's remark (minor on draft 1, MAJOR on the gate) held (D19/D24). **Ps 32:20 *quóniam adjútor et protéctor noster est* → *porque o nosso auxílio e protetor é ele* — HELD against two Latinist MAJORS (drafts 1 and 2) asking *o nosso auxiliador e protetor*.** Here adjútor is paired with the agent noun protéctor — exactly the case this row's standing proposal names (with 18:15b, 77:35); add 32:20 to that list. *auxiliador* is option 2 in Ps 32's decision `adjutor`. **Ps 39:18b *Adjútor meus, et protéctor meus tu es* → *Vós sois o meu auxílio e o meu protetor*** (= 27:7's words; the pair with an agent noun again). The Latinist passed it twice this time; the stylist failed it ('an abstract noun beside a person-noun, the pair limps') and offered *socorro* / *amparo* — refused (*socorro* is as abstract; *amparo* is suscípere's, 39:12). **69:6b *Adjútor meus, et liberátor meus es tu* will copy the first half.** **Ps 51:8 *qui non pósuit Deum adjutórem suum* → *que não fez de Deus o seu auxílio*** — the Latinist asked for the agent noun on both runs (minor: *auxiliador*, then *ajudador*); held for this row. **Ps 61:7 *adjútor meus* → *o meu auxílio*, 61:9 *Deus adjútor noster* → *Deus é o nosso auxílio*** — the Latinist asked *ajudador* on both runs (major at v1 on 61:7, bundled with the voice fault; minor at v2), noting 61:8 *Deus auxílii mei* → *o Deus do meu auxílio* makes the two Latin words one; held for this row, decision *adjutor* records the option. **Ps 69:6b *Adjútor meus, et liberátor meus es tu* → *Vós sois o meu auxílio e o meu libertador*** (39:18b's first half copied) — beside the agent noun *libertador*, the row's hard case again; the Latinist asked *auxiliador* on both runs (minor); held, option in decision `adjutor`. Add 69:6b to the list of paired places (18:15b, 32:20, 39:18b, 77:35). Ps 70:7 *et tu adjútor fortis* → *e vós sois um auxílio forte* (no reader remarked this time); 70:12 *in auxílium meum* is auxílium's own noun. **Ps 71:12 *et páuperem, cui non erat adjútor* → *e o pobre que não tinha quem o auxiliasse*** — a local departure from *auxílio*: the v1 Latinist and stylist both asked for the person (*quem o ajudasse*); taken in the row's own verb (adjuváre → auxiliar) because here there is no *meus* formula, only a man without a helper. *auxílio*, *auxiliador* options. The gate passed it. |
| suscípere | amparar | settled (D19) | Ps 117:13 *et Dóminus suscépit me* → *e o Senhor me amparou*; goes with *suscéptor* → amparo **Ps 6:10 meets an object that is not a person:** *Dóminus oratiónem meam suscépit* (προσεδέξατο, not ἀντελάβετο) — *amparou a minha oração* props the prayer up. See the new open row *suscípere (a thing as object)*; D19 stands for persons. — the noun and verb as a pair (3:4 / 3:6, 29:2): see the row *suscéptor* (the combined row was merged into it, 2026-09-21). **Ps 47:4 *cum suscípiet eam* (the city; ἀντιλαμβάνηται) → *quando a amparar*** — D19 with a city as object, since the Greek is D19's verb. Draft 1 supplied *ele*; the stylist heard it clog. |
| confídere in | confiar em | working | Ps 117:8; kept apart from *speráre in* → esperar em in the twin verse 117:9 (MS1932 and the Diurnal blur them) Ps 10:2 *No Senhor confio* (the Latin's order; MS1932 has the same words); 24:1 *in te confído* is the other place of the form. Ps 24:1 *em vós confio* (applied). |
| *Bonum est … quam* (positive for comparative) | *É melhor … do que* | open | Ps 117:8–9; the degree is grammar and yields (MS1932); *É bom … mais que* (DRB) keeps the echo of *quóniam bonus* and is the option |
| circuíre | rodear | working | Ps 117:10; kept apart from *circumdáre* → cercar (117:11–12), though the Greek has one verb **Ps 26:6b *Circuívi, et immolávi* (no object) → *Andei ao redor, e imolei*** — *rodear* is transitive in Portuguese and the stylist asked *rodeei o quê?*; without an object the going round is said with *andar ao redor* (circum- kept). With an object (117:10) *rodear* stands. |

| ulcísci in | vingar-se de | working | Ps 117:10–12 |

| exardéscere | inflamar-se | working | Ps 117:12 Ps 38:4 *exardéscet ignis* → *um fogo se inflamará* (the Latin's future, passed the Latinist twice). concaléscere beside it → *arder* (*O meu coração ardeu dentro de mim*): draft 1 *se aqueceu* was heard by the blind reader as 'comforted'. Ps 49:3b *Ignis in conspéctu ejus exardéscet* → *Um fogo se inflamará na sua presença* (= 38:4). The stylist asked *arderá* (plainer): refused, concaléscere's verb; option. |
