No reader remarked. **Ps 60:4 *turris fortitúdinis* → *torre de força*** (no article, as the Latin; the Preces versicle *Esto nobis, Dómine, turris fortitúdinis*); passed every reader. *uma torre de força*, *torre forte*, *torre de fortaleza* options. |
| laus | louvor | working | Ps 117:14; free because *confitéri* is *dar graças* Ps 8:3 *aperfeiçoastes o louvor*. |

No reader remarked. In the same psalm 59:12's plural *virtútes* is *forças* (D43) — the accepted cost. |
| déxtera | a direita | open | Ps 117:16, Ps 90:7; MS1932 *dextra / destra* is the hieratic alternative Ps 17:36a *e a vossa direita me amparou*; the blind reader heard the right hand first. Ps 15:8 *a dextris est mihi* → *está à minha direita*; 15:11 *delectatiónes in déxtera tua* → *deleites na vossa direita* (in → *na*, a dextris → *à*); the blind reader heard the right hand first, the right side second. Ps 16:8a *A resisténtibus déxteræ tuæ* → *dos que resistem à vossa direita* (a dative): the blind reader heard 'oppose God's power' first and 'stand at God's right' second — the cost of *direita* without *mão*; kept (déxtera → a direita). | benedícere (men → men), *Benedíximus vobis* | bendizer, perfect (*Nós vos bendissemos*) | open | Ps 117:26b; keeps *benedíctus … benedíximus* one verb; MS1932 has a present; hangs on *benedícere* (God → man). Cost of *vós* found here: the blind reader heard *vos* as the Lord |
| illucéscere (+ dative) | brilhar para | working | Ps 117:26b |
| condénsa, *in condénsis* | ramos densos (*entre ramos densos*) | open | Ps 117:27b; *ramos* supplied with DRB (*boughs*) and the Greek; the Latinist objected on draft 1 (major) and not on draft 2; undetermined option *nas espessuras*; 28:9 *revelábit condénsa* **Ps 28:9 *et revelábit condénsa* → *e que desnudará as matas densas*.** Not *ramos densos*: the Greek differs (here δρυμούς, 'woods'; 117:27b πυκάζουσιν), so by D15's test the two places need not share a word, and *ramos* would add a noun the Greek lacks here. Draft 1 *as matas cerradas* (the Brazilian collocation) was **heard by the blind reader as *serradas* (sawn)** — homophones; draft 2 *densas*, con-densa's own root. The Latinist passed both. *reveláre* of woods → *desnudar* ('lay bare', ἀποκαλύψει; *descobrir* is heard as 'find', *revelar* as disclosing); the blind reader listed *desnudará* as unknown. |
| cornu | chifre | open | Ps 117:27b *cornu altáris*; the concrete image kept (rule 5); *corno* is an insult in Brazil; must serve 17:3c, 74:5–6, 74:11, 88:18, 88:25, 91:11, 111:9, 131:17, 148:14a (grep in the DO Latin); the blind reader listed *chifre do altar* as unknown **Ps 17:3c *o chifre da minha salvação*:** kept; the blind reader heard a concrete horn and could not tell how it relates to salvation — not a mishearing, the image unexplained, as the Latin leaves it. The Latinist passed it twice. Ps 21:22 *a córnibus unicórnium* → *dos chifres dos unicórnios*; no reader remarked. **Ps 74:5, 74:6, 74:11 → *o chifre … o vosso chifre … os chifres dos pecadores … os chifres do justo*** — kept five times; the Latinist passed it twice; the stylist named the cuckold connotation and said the image must stay; the ambiguity reader heard literal horn / cuckold first and listed 'chifre as power' unknown. The image stands unexplained, as in the Latin. |
| dies solémnis | dia solene | working | Ps 117:27b |
| exquírere | procurar | open | Ps 118:2, 10, 22 (8× in the psalm); kept apart from *quǽrere* → buscar (118:100, 118:176 *quære servum tuum*) and *requírere* (118:145, not yet met); MS1932 merges into *buscar* **118:145 *requíram* is merged into it (*procurarei os vossos preceitos*)**: the Greek is ἐκζητήσω, the very verb behind *exquírere* all eight times in the psalm, so the variation is the Latin's elegance (D15's test) and Portuguese has no plain third verb (*requerer* is to petition an office). Decision `requiram` follows decision `exquirere`. 118:155 *non exquisiérunt* → *não procuraram*; 118:176 *quære* → *buscai*, kept apart to the end. **Ps 13:2 *aut requírens Deum* (ἐκζητῶν, = 52:3b, = Rom 3:11) → *ou quem procure a Deus*:** requírere merged into this row's verb again, on the Greek, as at 118:145; *busque* (MS1932, DM1962) is the option. 9:13 *requírens sánguinem* (God, of blood) stays *pedir contas*. Ps 21:27 *qui requírunt eum* (ἐκζητοῦντες) → *os que o procuram* — requírere merged into this row's verb again, on the Greek, as at 118:145 and 13:2. Ps 33:5 *Exquisívi Dóminum* → *Procurei o Senhor*; with 33:11 *inquiréntes* and 33:15 *inquíre* one verb, *procurar* (one decision `quaerere` in Ps 33), so the psalm's seeking is heard as one. Passed the Latinist twice. |
| scrutári | sondar | open | Ps 118:2 (also 118:34, 69, 115, 129); must serve 7:10 *scrutans corda et renes Deus*; options *perscrutar* (the cognate, learned), *esquadrinhar*; the blind reader listed *sondam* as unknown, the stylist let it pass **Ps 7:10, the verse this row names:** *Deus, vós que sondais os corações e os rins* — holds; no reader remarked on the verb. **Ps 63:7 *Scrutáti sunt iniquitátes: defecérunt scrutántes scrutínio* → *Sondaram iniquidades: desfaleceram os que sondavam, no sondar*** — the root three times, as the Latin; the stylist named the verse the psalm's best. The ambiguity reader listed *sondar* as unknown in the sense 'devise'. |
| meditári / meditátio | meditar em / meditação | working | Ps 118:16, 24; *meditátio mea est* → *é / são a minha meditação* (seven places in the psalm) Ps 18:15a *meditátio cordis mei* → *a meditação do meu coração*, the Latin's verbless second colon kept. |
| exercéri in | exercitar-se em | open | Ps 118:15, 23, 27 (also 48, 78); stands beside *meditári* each time, so it cannot be *meditar* (MS1932 and DRB merge them in places); heard by the blind reader as practising before studying — as open as the Latin; option *ocupar-se em* |
| consideráre | considerar | working | Ps 118:15, 18 **Ps 21:18 *consideravérunt … me* (a person as object) → *me observaram***: *me consideraram* is heard as 'esteemed me'. *considerar* stays for things (118:15, 18). Ps 36:32 *Consíderat peccátor justum* → *O pecador observa o justo* (a person, 21:18's build), kept apart from 36:12 *Observábit peccátor justum* → *O pecador espreitará o justo* (observáre, παρατηρήσεται). |
| docére / díscere | ensinar / aprender | working | Ps 118:7, 12, 26; *ensinai* is safe at the *vós* imperative Ps 17:35 *Qui docet manus meas ad prǽlium* → *Que ensina as minhas mãos para a batalha*, echoed by 17:36b *docébit* → *me ensinará*; the stylist asked for *instrui* (refused: the echo, and *instruir* is *erudíre*'s). Ps 24:4b *édoce me* merged → *ensinai-me* (δίδαξον, as 24:5 *doce me* and 24:9 *docébit*: D15). |
| oblivísci, *non sum oblítus* | esquecer (direct object), *não esqueci* | working | Ps 118:16, 30; the phrase closes six more verses of the psalm **Ps 49:22 *qui oblivíscimini Deum* → *vós que esqueceis a Deus*** — the direct object, marked with *a* before a person; MS1932 *vos esqueceis de Deus* option. |
| vivificáre, *vivífica me* | vivificar, *vivificai-me* | open | Ps 118:17, 25 (12× in the psalm); one word for one, safe at the *vós* imperative; the blind reader listed it as unknown; option *dai-me a vida* (MS1932) **Ps 40:3 *et vivíficet eum* → *e o vivifique*** (the Preces versicle for the Pope): unknown to the blind reader again (Ps 118, Ps 40); *lhe dê vida* (MS1932) is the option there. Ps 70:20 *convérsus vivificásti me* → *voltando-vos, me vivificastes* — the ambiguity reader listed *vivificastes* as unknown again; *destes a vida* option. |
| confúndi | ser envergonhado; active *não me envergonheis* | settled (D15) | Ps 118:6, 31 (also 46, 78, 80, 116). Draft 1 had the traditional *ser confundido*; the blind reader heard "I shall not be *confused*" first in both verses, so it was changed. **Costs for Gustavo to weigh:** 30:2 = 70:1 *non confúndar in ætérnum* (the last line of the Te Deum) would not be the traditional *não serei confundido*; and *erubéscere*, which stands beside *confúndi* in 34:4/26, 39:15, 69:3–4, needs another word — proposed *corar* **Ps 6:11 is the first *erubéscere*, and *corar* holds:** *Que todos os meus inimigos corem … e corem muito depressa* — the Latin's own image (to redden), bare; the blind reader took it at once for shame, the stylist kept it twice in his own rebuilt line, the Latinist passed it. See the new row *erubéscere*. **Ps 13:6 the active with men as subject, *consílium ínopis confudístis* → *vós envergonhastes o conselho do indigente*:** D15's verb holds in the active; the Latinist passed it twice, the blind reader heard 'put to shame' without remark (he heard *conselho* as advice given). Ps 21:6 *non sunt confúsi* → *não foram envergonhados* (D15); heard as 'not humiliated'. Ps 24:3 *não serão envergonhados*, 24:4 *Sejam envergonhados* (beside erubéscere → corar in 24:1, 24:20). **Ps 30:2 and 30:17 — the Te Deum line, decided here as D15 foresaw:** *In te, Dómine, sperávi, non confúndar in ætérnum* → *Em vós, Senhor, esperei: para sempre não serei envergonhado*; 30:17 *Dómine, non confúndar* → *Senhor, não serei envergonhado* (one decision, `confundar`). *confúndar* is future and present subjunctive at once; the Greek has the optative (μὴ καταισχυνθείην) both times and DRB / MS1932 take the wish — the future is ruled, as 24:1 *não corarei*, with D23's formula for the negated *in ætérnum*. The blind reader heard 'never be put to shame'; the Latinist passed both places twice. The stylist asked *não serei envergonhado para sempre* (the 'not for ever' order, refused; option). Options: *jamais*, the wish *não seja eu envergonhado*, the traditional *confundido*. = 70:1 and the Te Deum's close (Common/Prayers.txt). Ps 34:4 *Confundántur et revereántur* → *Sejam envergonhados e desonrados*; 34:4b *confundántur* → *sejam envergonhados*; 34:26b *confusióne* → *de vergonha*. reveréri beside it needed a third word (new row). Ps 52:6b *confúsi sunt, quóniam Deus sprevit eos* → *foram envergonhados, porque Deus os desprezou* (D15). The stylist asked *ficaram envergonhados* («stiff, bookish passive») — refused, option: *ficar envergonhado* is a feeling, *confúsi sunt* is being put to shame; the Latinist passed it twice. **Ps 70:1 = 30:2a copied** (*para sempre não serei envergonhado*, decision `confundar` with Ps 30's options). The v1 Latinist asked the wish (*que eu não seja envergonhado para sempre*) and the v1 stylist the 'not for ever' order again — both held for identical Latin, options 3 and 5. 70:13 *Confundántur* → *Sejam envergonhados*; 70:24 *cum confúsi … fúerint* → *quando forem envergonhados*. |
| concupíscere | ansiar por | open | Ps 118:20 *Concupívit ánima mea desideráre* → *A minha alma ansiou por desejar* (the doubled desire kept); 118:40, 118:174, 83:3; draft 1 *cobiçar* failed the stylist (no infinitive after it) Ps 44:12 *concupíscet Rex* → *o Rei ansiará por*; the stylist asked *desejará*, refused (118:20 has *concupíscere* and *desideráre* together). **Ps 61:11 *rapínas nolíte concupíscere* → *não cobiceis rapinas*** — a local departure: the object is another's goods (the commandment's verb); *ansieis por* option. Proposal: split — *cobiçar* for goods, *ansiar por* for the good (118:20). |
| oppróbrium / exprobráre | afronta / afrontar | open | Ps 118:22; draft 1 *opróbrio* (MS1932, DM1962) failed both blind readers; keeps 68:10 *oppróbria exprobrántium* → *as afrontas dos que vos afrontam*; option *desonra* (stylist) Ps 14:3b *oppróbrium non accépit advérsus próximos suos* → *não aceitou afronta contra os seus próximos*: heard by the blind reader as 'did not tolerate offences against his neighbours' first and 'did not take up an accusation against them' second (the Latin's letter); passed the Latinist twice. Ps 21:7 *oppróbrium hóminum* → *a afronta dos homens*; heard as insult suffered. Ps 30:12 *factus sum oppróbrium* → *tornei-me uma afronta*; the blind reader heard 'someone who offends' first, 'an object of scorn' second. *Super omnes inimícos meos* → *Mais que todos os meus inimigos* (the Latinist, minor: the comparison). **Ps 38:9 *oppróbrium insipiénti dedísti me* → *fizestes de mim a afronta do insensato*** — draft 1 *uma afronta para o insensato*: the blind reader could not tell the direction; the stylist asked for a genitive (and *opróbrio*, refused per this row). The gate (minor) wanted the dative and *dar* back (*destes-me como afronta ao insensato*) — held: *afronta ao X* is heard as an insult offered to X. Option in Ps 38 `dedisti`. Ps 41:11 *exprobravérunt mihi* → *afrontaram-me* (heard as insulted, the blind reader). **Ps 68:8, 10, 11, 20, 21 applied, and *impropérium* (68:20, 68:21; the same Greek ὀνειδισμός) shares *afronta* (D15)**; 68:10 *oppróbria exprobrántium tibi* → *as afrontas dos que vos afrontam* — the stylist's best line. **Ps 73:10, 18, 22 *improperáre / impropérium* → *afrontar / afronta*** — only in this psalm (grep); the same Greek (ὀνειδίζω / ὀνειδισμός), so one Portuguese. 73:22 *lembrai-vos das vossas afrontas* — the ambiguity reader first heard God's own offences, and the clause that follows resolves it; the Latin genitive is as open. |
| contémptus | desprezo | working | Ps 118:22 **Ps 21:7 *abjéctio plebis* (only here) → *o desprezo do povo***: its Greek ἐξουδένημα is of the root of 118:22 ἐξουδένωσιν (*contémptum*) and of 21:25 ἐξουδένωσεν (*non sprevit* → *não desprezou*), so the psalm's own echo is kept. Options *o refugo*, MS1932 *a abjeção*. |
| declináre a | apartar-se de | open | Ps 118:21 (also 51, 102, 157; 118:115 *Apartai-vos de mim, malignos*); keeps *desviar* for *avértere* **Ps 13:3a *Omnes declinavérunt* (absolute; = 52:4 = Rom 3:12) → *Todos se desviaram*:** without *a*, and next to *juntos* (*simul*), *se apartaram* risks 'they parted from one another'; the reflexive *desviar-se* is not avértere's transitive *desviar*. The blind reader heard 'turned from the good'. Absolute *declinavérunt* elsewhere (20:12, 54:4 *in me*, 101:12 of days like a shadow) is another build; 77:57 *avertérunt se* will also be *se desviaram* — two Latin verbs share a form there. **Transitive, *declináre in* + object:** Ps 20:12 *declinavérunt in te mala* → *voltaram males contra vós* (the stylist's worst line was *desviaram males contra vós*); the twin 54:4 *declinavérunt in me iniquitátes* should follow (*voltaram iniquidades contra mim*). *voltar* is also *convértere*'s — they do not meet in these lines. **Ps 33:15 *Divérte a malo, et fac bonum* → *Aparta-te do mal, e faz o bem*** — another verb (divértere, ἔκκλινον as in 36:27), the same image, so this row's *apartar-se de*. **36:27 *Declína a malo, et fac bonum* is the near twin (one Greek) — it should copy the colon.** Ps 36:27a *Declína a malo, et fac bonum* → *Aparta-te do mal, e faz o bem* (= 33:15a, the formula row); passed the Latinist twice. **Ps 54:4 departs from the wording foreseen here:** *declinavérunt in me iniquitátes* → *Porque as iniquidades se voltaram contra mim* (v2). All three v1 readers heard bare *voltaram iniquidades contra mim* as "came back, returned"; the reflexive says "turned against". The object reading (LXX, DRB *cast*) survives only as the option *lançaram*. **20:12 *voltaram males contra vós* carries the same risk — for review.** Passed the Latinist gate. |
| amovére / repéllere a | afastar | open | Ps 118:29 *Afastai de mim* / 118:10 *não me afasteis dos vossos mandamentos* — two Latin verbs, one Portuguese: *rejeitar de* failed both blind readers at 118:10, and *rejeitar* is *reprobáre*'s (Ps 117:22); *repéllere* without *a* (42:2, 59:3) is not settled by this Ps 17:23 *justítias ejus non répuli a me* → *e não afastei de mim as suas justiças*. So in 17:22 *nec ímpie gessi a Deo meo* took *apartar-se* (*e não me apartei impiamente do meu Deus* — the Latinist's major, taken) to keep the two verbs apart. Ps 38:10 *ámove a me plagas tuas* → *afastai de mim os vossos flagelos* (= 118:29's verb). Ps 65:20 *qui non amóvit oratiónem meam* → *que não afastou a minha oração* — applied; no remark. |
| adhærére | apegar-se a | open | Ps 118:25 *A minha alma apegou-se ao chão*, 118:31 *Apeguei-me aos vossos testemunhos* (one verb twice in the stanza); fits 62:9 *Adhǽsit ánima mea post te*, 21:16, 72:28; MS1932 *aderir*, DM1962 *colar-se* **Ps 21:16 *lingua mea adhǽsit fáucibus meis* → *a minha língua se colou à minha garganta*** — a physical sticking: *se apegou* (draft 1, this row) was heard by the stylist as affection before touch, so the row's own option (DM1962 *colar-se*) is taken there. Proposal: *apegar-se* where the soul clings (118:25, 31; 62:9), *colar-se* where a body part sticks (21:16; 101:6 *adhǽsit os meum carni meæ*, 136:6a *Adhǽreat lingua mea fáucibus meis* — grep before use). Ps 24:21 *Os inocentes e os retos apegaram-se a mim* (the blind reader heard affection first, support second). **Ps 62:8b *adhǽsit ánima mea post te* → *a minha alma apegou-se a vós, seguindo-vos*** — *post te* kept by a participle (the Latinist's v1 fix; on the v2 gate he asked *atrás de vós*, held as unidiomatic; option). The colon is the antiphon of St Stephen / St Lawrence. **Ps 72:28 *Mihi autem adhærére Deo bonum est* → *Mas para mim é bom apegar-me a Deus*** — applied as the row names; the versicle read alone; passed every reader. |
| paviméntum | chão | working | Ps 118:25; not the Hebrew-family *pó* |

| reveláre (óculos) | desvendar | working | Ps 118:18 *Desvendai os meus olhos*; *abri* and *descobri* fail rule 3; *revelátio* stays *revelação* |

| increpáre | repreender | working | Ps 118:21. Since D21 it shares the word with *árguere* (different Greek verbs, never in one verse); a good second word for God’s rebuke at the sea and the nations (*ab increpatióne tua fúgient*) would restore the split **Ps 9:6 *Increpásti gentes* → *Repreendestes as nações*:** held; no critic remarked, and the blind reader heard a rebuke. No better verb for nations; 103:7's noun still open. **Ps 17:16b *Ab increpatióne tua, Dómine* → *À vossa repreensão, Senhor*** (the noun; *À* = DRB's causal *At*). The same phrase opens 75:7, 103:7 and closes 79:17 (grep). No reader remarked; *ameaças* (MS1932) is the option. Ps 15:7 *os meus rins me repreenderam* (the Greek here is ἐπαίδευσαν, 'chastened'; the Latin's 'rebuked' followed). |
| operári iniquitátem | praticar a iniquidade | working | Ps 118:3; the frequent *qui operámini iniquitátem* (6:9 …) Ps 5:7a *todos os que praticam a iniquidade*; Ps 6:9. |
| ambuláre | andar | working | Ps 118:1, 3; not *caminhar* beside *caminho* |
| immaculátus | imaculado | open | Ps 118:1 (118:80, 18:8 *Lex Dómini immaculáta*); option *sem mancha* **Ps 17:24, 17:31 (*impollúta*), 17:33 → *imaculado* all three times**: one Greek word (ἄμωμος), so *impollútus* is merged by D15's test (*impoluto* the option). The blind reader listed *imaculado* as unknown (twice) — surprising; kept. **Ps 18:8 *Lex Dómini immaculáta*, 18:14b *tunc immaculátus ero* → *imaculada / imaculado*** — the two places this row named; passed every reader. Ps 63:4 *immaculátum* (a person) → *o imaculado*; the ambiguity reader heard the blameless man, a Marian echo possible, and listed the word as unknown in this sense; kept (*o inocente*, MS1932, is *ínnocens*'s). |
| dirígere | dirigir | working | Ps 118:5 *sejam dirigidos*; 118:128, 133 **Ps 5:9: at the *vós* imperative it is banned** — bare *dirigi* is "I directed", and with the enclitic (*dírige me*, 24:5, 26:11) *dirigi-me* is the everyday "I made my way to". See the new row *dirígere at the vós imperative*. **Ps 118:128 *dirigébar* → *eu era dirigido para todos os vossos mandamentos*: the passive is kept at the Latinist's insistence (major on the middle *eu me dirigia*), against the stylist, who twice called it mechanical.** **Ps 7:10 *díriges justum* → *e dirigireis o justo*:** a future, which the ban does not reach; with a person as object it says "guide, steer" (DRB *direct*). Passed all three readers; options *encaminhareis* (MS1932), *endireitareis*. **Ps 58:5 absolute *diréxi* → *segui reto*** (the stylist's, for draft 1 *segui direito*); no object supplied. The Latinist (v2, minor) asked *dirigi os meus passos* (DRB, MS1932) — option. |
| diréctio (cordis) | retidão | working | Ps 118:7 |
| derelínquere | abandonar | working | Ps 118:8 Ps 21:2 *quare me dereliquísti?* → *por que me abandonastes?* — the Passion's cry (Mt 27:46, Mk 15:34 Vulgate have *Deus meus, Deus meus, ut quid dereliquisti me?*, fetched: not the psalm's wording). *desamparastes* (MS1932) is the option. |

| nimis | no one rendering; 118:4 *à risca* (MS1932) | open | 118:96 and 118:138 will need another (*sobremaneira* is the literal). **118:96:** *sobremaneira* was refused by the stylist and listed as unknown by the blind reader → *é muito amplo* (as D22's *valde / veheménter → muito*; σφόδρα) **118:138 *et veritátem tuam nimis* → *e a vossa verdade sem medida***: Lewis & Short derive *nimis* from the root "to measure" and gloss "beyond measure"; passed all readers (the Latinist's own line had *sobremaneira*, which failed the other two readers at 118:96). Three renderings in the psalm, as this row foresaw: *à risca*, *muito*, *sem medida*. Ps 45:2 *quæ invenérunt nos nimis* → *que nos encontraram sem medida* (= 37:9); the Latinist (v2 gate, minor) asked for *grandemente*, refused. **Ps 47:2 *laudábilis nimis* → *muito digno de louvor*** — as valde (D22); the Pent08-0 Gradual quotes the line with *laudábilis valde* (grep). 95:4 and 144:3 have the same words and should copy it. |
| confirmáre | confirmar | open | Ps 118:28 *confirmai-me nas vossas palavras*; options *fortalecer* (MS1932), *firmar* **Ps 36:17 *confírmat autem justos Dóminus* → draft 2 *mas o Senhor sustenta os justos*:** draft 1 *confirma os justos* was heard by the blind reader as 'reconhece ou atesta que eles são justos'. With persons as object *confirmar* is heard as attesting; *sustentar* (ὑποστηρίζει, 'props up'; free — sustinére is *esperar por*, D36) passed the Latinist. The Tuesday Matins antiphon *Brácchia peccatórum * conteréntur, confírmat autem justos Dóminus* follows. Proposal: *confirmar* only where a thing or a word is confirmed; with persons, *sustentar* (or *firmar*). **Ps 37:3 and 37:20 *confirmásti super me manum tuam … confirmáti sunt super me* → *firmastes sobre mim a vossa mão … se firmaram sobre mim*** — the row's option *firmar*, one verb for the psalm's echo (*confirmastes a mão* would be heard as attesting, as 36:17). The gate Latinist asked the passive *foram fortalecidos* (minor), refused for the echo; the blind reader heard 'gained the upper hand'. Evidence for a split: with a thing or a hand pressed upon someone → *firmar*. **Ps 40:13 *confirmásti me in conspéctu tuo* → *me firmastes na vossa presença*** — a person as object, the row's proposed *firmar*; passed the Latinist twice; the blind reader heard 'set me firmly before you'. **Ps 50:14** *e firmai-me com um espírito soberano* (στήρισον): with a person as object *confirmai-me* is heard as the sacrament; *firmar* taken, passed the readers. Proposal: *firmar* whenever a person is the object. **Ps 70:6 *In te confirmátus sum* → *Em vós fui firmado*** (ἐπεστηρίχθην, the passive kept; MS1932 *me firmei* option); passed every reader. **Ps 74:4 *ego confirmávi colúmnas ejus* → *eu firmei as suas colunas*** — a thing set fast, the row's *firmar* (ἐστερέωσα); passed every reader. More evidence for *firmar* except where a word is confirmed. **Ps 73:13 *Tu confirmásti in virtúte tua mare* → *Vós firmastes o mar com o vosso poder*** — a thing made firm (ἐκραταίωσας); passed every reader. |
| dormitáre | dormitar | working | Ps 118:28; kept apart from *dormíre* (120:4); the blind reader listed it as unknown **Ps 75:7 *dormitavérunt qui ascendérunt equos* → *dormitaram os que montaram cavalos*** — kept apart from 75:6 *Dormiérunt somnum suum* → *Dormiram o seu sono* one verse earlier; the ambiguity reader listed *dormitaram* unknown; *adormeceram* the option. |
| tǽdium | tédio | open | Ps 118:28 (ἀκηδία); heard as boredom; option *abatimento* |

| étenim / nam et / enim | pois / pois também / pois | working | Ps 118:3, 23, 24 Ps 18:12 *Étenim servus tuus* → *Pois o vosso servo*. Ps 57:3 *Étenim in corde* → *Pois no coração*. **Ps 61:3 *Nam et ipse Deus meus* → *Pois ele mesmo é o meu Deus*** — a local departure: *Pois também ele* was heard as 'he too, besides other gods' by the stylist and the blind reader; *mesmo* carries *et ipse*. The v2 gate asked *Pois também ele mesmo* (minor); held, option. 61:7 *Quia ipse* → *Porque ele é*. Watch *também* wherever *nam et* falls on God. Ps 64:14 *clamábunt, étenim hymnum dicent* → *clamarão, pois hão de entoar um hino*: the blind reader heard *pois* as 'because' and the Latinist (v2, minor) called *étenim* intensive here (καὶ γάρ, 'yea') — held for the row; worth a look where *étenim* follows a verb it does not explain. **Ps 67:9, 17b, 19b → *pois*** — draft 1 *pois também* (καὶ γάρ) was heard as translationese by the stylist; draft 2 *pois até* (9, 19b) was refused by the gate ('even' added). The row's bare *pois* ends it. |
| consílium | *conselho* (advice; a deliberating body; where a verse can mean both) / *desígnio* (what someone intends: 9:23, 19:5, 20:12, 32:10–11) — D33 | settled (D33) | Ps 1:1 (Ps 118:24 already has it); open between advice and a deliberating body, as the Latin is — the blind reader heard both Ps 12:2 *Até quando hei de pôr conselhos na minha alma* — the stylist's *hei de pôr* (a deliberative future) for draft 1's *porei*; the blind reader heard advice kept first, deliberation second — the Latin's range; *planos* (MS1932 *projectos*) is an option. **Ps 19:5 *omne consílium tuum* → *todo o teu desígnio*** — a local exception: a person's own plan, confirmed by God (the Latinist's MAJOR on *todos os teus conselhos*, and the blind reader heard advice given to others first). Ps 20:12 *consília* follows (*desígnios*). Proposal: *conselho* for advice / a council, *desígnio* for a plan. Ps 20:12 *consília, quæ non potuérunt stabilíre* → *desígnios que não puderam firmar* (Ps 19:5's word). **Ps 32:10–11, the test D33 named: *O Senhor dissipa os desígnios das nações … e rejeita os desígnios dos príncipes. * Mas o desígnio do Senhor permanece para sempre*** — the plans of nations, princes and God set against each other; the Latinist passed *desígnio(s)* on drafts 1 and 2, the stylist raised nothing. **The blind reader listed *desígnios* and *desígnio* as unknown** (as the Ps 9 reader did). The test holds for sense; for the ear it is evidence that *planos* (one touch away in Ps 32's decision `consilia`) may be wanted where the plan is not God's. **Ps 9:23 *comprehendúntur in consíliis quibus cógitant* → *ficam presos nos desígnios em que pensam*** (Ps 9 draft 8; D33, the build of 20:12; review 2026-09-21). 12:2 and 13:6 keep *conselho* under D33’s “where a verse can mean both”. Ps 70:10 *consílium fecérunt in unum* → *tomaram conselho juntos* (the idiom for deliberating; *fizeram conselho* option; 30:14's *planejaram* is consiliári's). |
| concílium | by its Greek (D34): *congregação* where συναγωγή (21:17); *assembleia* at 1:5 (βουλή, for the echo with 1:1 *consílio*); *conselho* at 25:4 (συνέδριον) | settled (D34) | Ps 1:5 *in concílio justórum* → *na assembleia dos justos* (from the stylist; DM1962's word). Draft 1 had the cognate *concílio*, for the Latin's echo *consílio impiórum … concílio justórum* (the Greek has βουλῇ twice): both blind readers stopped at it ("a council of bishops"; listed as unknown). **Collision to rule on:** *assembleia* is also the natural word for *ecclésia*, and Ps 21 has both (21:17 *concílium malignántium*, 21:23, 21:26 *in ecclésia*); other places 25:4, 39:11. *congregação* (MS1932) is *congregátio*'s **Ps 21 met the collision this row foretold, and rules it for review: two Latin words six verses apart, two Greek words (21:17 *concílium malignántium* = συναγωγὴ πονηρευομένων; 21:23, 21:26 *ecclésia* = ἐκκλησία), so two Portuguese words.** *ecclésia → assembleia* everywhere (see the new row *ecclésia*); *concílium* here → ***a congregação dos malvados***, on D15's test with synagóga → congregação (συναγωγή). The Greek behind concílium varies (read in the parallels files): 1:5 βουλή, 21:17 συναγωγή, 25:4 συνεδρίου, 39:11 συναγωγῆς. **Proposal: concílium → *congregação* where it is συναγωγή (21:17, 39:11); 1:5 (βουλή) and 25:4 (συνέδριον) decide locally — 1:5's *assembleia* would then share the word with ecclésia in another psalm.** Passed the Latinist twice; the blind reader heard 'a group of wicked people' (the cães as its image), never a religious order. **Ps 25:4 *Non sedi cum concílio vanitátis* (συνεδρίου) → *Não me sentei no conselho da vaidade*** — decided locally as D34 directs; draft 1 had *com o conselho*, and the blind reader heard advice (a recommendation dictated by vanity), so the preposition changed, not the word: one sits *in* a council. The gate Latinist (minor) wants *com* back for *cum*'s company; refused, draft 1's words stay the option. **Ps 39:11b *a concílio multo* → *da congregação numerosa*: checked — ἀπὸ συναγωγῆς πολλῆς (Rahlfs, in the parallels).** D34's 'not yet checked' is closed. The stylist heard it 'administrative' and asked *assembleia* (ecclésia's, one verse before) — refused. multus → *numerosa*, not *grande* (magna, 39:10). |

Ps 41:11 *Dum confringúntur ossa mea* → *Enquanto se quebram os meus ossos* (the pronominal passive, the stylist's; *são quebrados* option). **Ps 45:10 *confrínget arma* → *despedaçará as armas*** — the row's option used where contérere stands beside it (συγκλάσει, another Greek verb); see the row contérere. **Ps 57:7 *molas leónum confrínget Dóminus* → *o Senhor despedaçará os molares dos leões*** — beside *cónteret* in the same verse and a second Greek verb (συνέτριψεν … συνέθλασεν), so 45:10's split. Passed the Latinist twice. Ps 74:11 *ómnia córnua peccatórum confríngam* → *quebrarei todos os chifres dos pecadores* (συγκλάσω); alone in the psalm; no reader remarked. **Ps 73:14 *confregísti cápita dracónis* → *quebrastes*** — beside 73:13 *contribulásti → esmagastes* (συνέτριψας / συνέθλασας). Ps 75:4 *Ibi confrégit poténtias árcuum* → *Ali quebrou as potências dos arcos*; no reader remarked on the verb. |
| intellégere | entender | working | Ps 2:10; *entendei* is safe at the *vós* imperative (past *entendi*) Ps 18:13 *Delícta quis intéllegit?* → *Quem entende as faltas?* **Ps 57:10 *Priúsquam intellégerent spinæ vestræ rhamnum* → *Antes que os vossos espinhos entendessem o espinheiro*** — the Latin (= the Greek συνιέναι) kept; the blind reader found it incomprehensible, as the Latin is. *conhecessem* (DRB) option. |
| erudíri at the *vós* imperative | *deixai-vos instruir* | open | Ps 2:10; *instruí-vos* fails rule 3 and D13's exception does not save it ("I instructed you" is a good sentence); *erudíre* → instruir elsewhere (93:12) |
| exsultáre + dative | exultar para (*exultai para ele*) | open | Ps 2:11; 80:2 *Exsultáte Deo*, 94:1 *exsultémus Dómino*; the stylist heard a translation and asked for *diante dele*, refused as another relation (*in conspéctu*) |
| timor … tremor (2:11) | temor … tremor, rhyme kept | working | the Latin rhymes at the same two cadences (*timóre / tremóre*) — rule 5's exception; the stylist's re-ordering is the option |
| apprehéndere disciplínam | abraçar a disciplina | working | Ps 2:12 — the Latin's reading, not the Hebrew's "kiss the son"; DRB, MS1932; *agarrar* is the literal option; *disciplína* → disciplina (17:36, 49:17, 118:66) |
| nequándo … et | para que não … e não | working | Ps 2:12; eight places; the second *não* is supplied as Portuguese grammar needs (MS1932) **Ps 27:1 *nequándo táceas a me, et assimilábor descendéntibus in lacum* → *para que não vos caleis comigo, nem eu me torne semelhante aos que descem à cova*** — the second negation said as *nem* (the stylist: breath). Here the Latin's second verb is a future indicative (the consequence feared), and the Latinist made it a MAJOR (the negation and the subjunctive added; his fix *não suceda que … e eu serei assemelhado*): held — without the negation Portuguese hears the likeness wished for, and *para que não* / *não suceda que* govern the subjunctive. **The same build returns in 142:7b** (*et símilis ero descendéntibus in lacum*, after *Ne avértas*: there no *nequándo*, so the future can stand). **Ps 37:17 in quoted speech — *Quia dixi: Nequándo supergáudeant mihi inimíci mei* → *Porque eu disse: Que os meus inimigos não se alegrem à minha custa*** (draft 2): draft 1 *Para que não se alegrem* was heard by the blind reader as a purpose clause ('I said this so that…') and was the stylist's worst line. After a verb of saying the quoted words are a wish: *Que … não* + subjunctive. *para que não* stays where the clause depends on a verb (2:12, 27:1). The gate Latinist asked *nunca* for the *quando* (minor, refused; option). **Ps 49:22 *nequándo rápiat, et non sit qui erípiat* → *para que não arrebate, e não haja quem liberte*** — no subject and no object supplied (the Latin leaves God and *you* understood; DRB and MS1932 supply *you*). The stylist's *ele arrebate* an option. |
| *de* via (períre de) | fora de | settled (D20) | Ps 2:12 *e não pereçais fora do caminho justo* — **stands against the Latinist (major, twice)**, who wants *pereçais do caminho*: refused because *perecer de* names the cause of death; MS1932 has *fora do* **D27: only in períre de via; 9:37 peribítis de terra → perecereis da terra (fora da terra was heard as dying outside it).** |
| in brevi | em breve | working | Ps 2:13 |
| exsúrgere; *Exsúrge, Dómine* | levantar-se; *Levantai-vos, Senhor* | open | Ps 3:6 *e me levantei*, 3:7 *levantai-vos, Senhor* — with 3:2 *insúrgunt* → *se levantam contra mim* (row *insúrgere*), one Portuguese verb for the Latin's one root, three times in the psalm; safe at the *vós* imperative (*levantai* ≠ *levantei*); the formula recurs 7:7, 9:20, 9:33, 16:13, 43:26 …; *erguer-se* is *astáre*'s (Ps 2:2) Ps 7:7a, 7:7b *Levantai-vos, Senhor … E levantai-vos, Senhor, meu Deus* — applied twice; no reader remarked. Ps 11:6a *agora me levantarei, diz o Senhor* (God speaking of himself); heard as "now I will act for the poor". Ps 16:13 *Exsúrge, Dómine* → *Levantai-vos, Senhor* — applied. Ps 75:10 *Cum exsúrgeret in judícium Deus* → *Quando Deus se levantou para o juízo*. |
| tribuláre | atribular (*os que me atribulam*) | working | Ps 3:2; family of *tribulátio* → tribulação; the blind reader listed *atribulam* as unknown Ps 12:5b *Os que me atribulam* — listed as unknown by both blind readings of Ps 12; kept. Ps 22:5 *os que me atribulam*: the stylist asked *afligem* («livresco») and the blind reader listed it unknown — refused (the family of tribulação; *afligir* is afflígere's). A third psalm with the same report: whoever rules should weigh it. Ps 41:11 *qui tríbulant me* → *que me atribulam* — unknown to the blind reader again; kept. Ps 55:2 *tribulávit me* → *me atribulou* — unknown to the blind reader again (the fourth psalm); kept. Ps 59:14 *tribulántes nos* → *os que nos atribulam* — unknown to the blind reader again (the fifth psalm); kept, beside *tribulação* in 59:13. |
| multiplicári | multiplicar-se | working | Ps 3:2, Ps 4:8 |
| soporári, *soporátus sum* | ser tomado pelo sono | open | Ps 3:6 *Eu dormi, e fui tomado pelo sono: e me levantei* — three verbs kept apart (4:9 has *dormirei e repousarei* for *dórmiam et requiéscam*); the passive kept at the Latinist's insistence (minor, then major) against drafts 1–2 *caí em sono profundo*, which said *sopor*'s depth but was active; MS1932 *estive sepultado no sono* |
| *míllia pópuli circumdántis me* | *o povo que me cerca aos milhares* | working | Ps 3:7 (the stylist's syntax; the Latinist passed it) |
| percútere | ferir | working | Ps 3:8 |
| adversári (+ dative) | opor-se a (*os que se opõem a mim*) | open | Ps 3:8; a participle, not *adversárius*; MS1932 *perseguir* is *pérsequi*'s Ps 34:19 *qui adversántur mihi iníque* → *os que injustamente se opõem a mim* (the adverb first, after the stylist; *iniquamente* was unknown to the blind reader). |
| sine causa | sem causa | working | Ps 3:8 — the Latin's reading (Greek ματαίως), not the Hebrew's "on the cheek" |
| contérere | quebrar (*quebrastes os dentes*) | open | Ps 3:8; the same Greek verb (συντρίβω) as *confríngere* in 2:9, and the same Portuguese; **45:10 *arcum cónteret et confrínget arma* sets the two side by side and must decide locally**; 50:19 *cor contrítum* will want *contrito*; 57:7 *cónteret dentes eórum* should match 3:8 **Ps 45:10 decided locally, as asked: *Arcum cónteret, et confrínget arma* → *Quebrará o arco, e despedaçará as armas*** — the Greek has two verbs here (συντρίψει … συγκλάσει), so by D15's test the two stay two: contérere keeps *quebrar*, confríngere takes *despedaçar* (MS1932 *fará em pedaços*). Passed the Latinist twice. Ps 47:7 *cónteres naves Tharsis* → *quebrareis os navios de Társis*. **Ps 50:19** *cor contrítum* → *coração contrito* (the sacramental word; beside *contribulátus → atribulado*). **Ps 57:7 *Deus cónteret dentes eórum in ore ipsórum* → *Deus quebrará os dentes deles na própria boca*** — matches 3:8, as this row asked; *confrínget* in the same verse takes *despedaçar* (45:10). |
| benedíctio | bênção | working | Ps 3:9 Ps 20:4 *in benedictiónibus dulcédinis* → *com bênçãos de doçura*; **20:7 *dabis eum in benedictiónem* → *fareis dele uma bênção*** (the blind reader heard *o dareis como bênção* as blessings given to the king — the Greek's dative, not the Latin's). |
| *Deus meus* (vocative) | *meu Deus* | working | Ps 3:7 |
| supersperáre in | pôr toda a esperança em (*pus toda a esperança nos vossos juízos*) | open | Ps 118:43, 74 (also 81, 114, 147 — only in this psalm); the calque of ἐπελπίζω; one verse after *sperávi* (118:42 → *esperei*). Draft 4 *esperei muito em* (DRB "hoped exceedingly") was heard by the blind reader FIRST as "I waited a long time for", both times; MS1932 has this at 118:74 (with *minha*) |
| intelléctus, *da mihi intelléctum* | entendimento (*Dai-me entendimento, e …*) | open | Ps 118:34, 73 (also 125, 130, 144, 169); one family with 118:27 *Fazei-me entender* (the Greek has that verb here too); MS1932 and DM1962 *inteligência* Ps 31:8 *Intelléctum tibi dabo* → *Eu te darei entendimento* (the Lord to one man, tu, D17); 31:9 *quibus non est intelléctus* → *que não têm entendimento* — one noun both times. |
| dedúcere, *deduc me* | guiar (*Guiai-me à vereda*) | settled (D22) | Ps 118:35; ὁδηγέω; 5:9, 22:3, 42:3, 60:4, 138:10. The cognate fails rule 3 even with its enclitic: *conduzi-me* is also "I behaved myself" (D13 does not rescue it); MS1932 *Guia-me* **Ps 5:9 tests it and it holds:** *Senhor, guiai-me na vossa justiça* (MS1932 and DM1962 both *guia-me*); Latinist clean twice, no remark from stylist or blind reader. Ready to settle. **Ps 7:6 meets another build:** *glóriam meam in púlverem dedúcat* (an enemy brings a man's glory down into the dust; 21:16 *in púlverem mortis deduxísti me* is the same) → *faça descer ao pó a minha glória* (L&S "lead or bring down", DRB). *Guie ao pó* makes the enemy a kindly guide. D22 stands for *deduc me*. **D25: *guiar* is for guidance; *dedúcere in púlverem* (7:6; 21:16) is *fazer descer ao pó*.** Ps 21:16 *in púlverem mortis deduxísti me* → *e me fizestes descer ao pó da morte* (D25, as named); heard as 'God brought me to death or near it'. **Ps 54:24 *dedúces eos in púteum intéritus* → *os fareis descer ao poço da destruição*** (D25's build, κατάξεις); passed the Latinist twice. **Ps 60:4 *Deduxísti me* → *Vós me guiastes*** (the stylist: the enclitic *Guiastes-me* at a verse head sounded bookish; a subject pronoun, D2); passed the gate. **Ps 59:11 *Quis dedúcet me … quis dedúcet me* → *Quem me guiará … quem me guiará*** (the repetition kept where the Greek varies; MS1932 *conduzirá* option); the stylist's best line. 107:11 should copy. |
| statúere | firmar (*Firmai o vosso dito para o vosso servo*) | open | Ps 118:38; 39:3 *státuit super petram pedes meos*, 118:106 *státui*; keeps *estabelecer* for constitúere and *confirmar* for confirmáre; will share a word with firmáre. 118:106 *státui* + infinitive is another sense — see the row *statúere + infinitive* Ps 17:34 *et super excélsa státuens me* → *e sobre as alturas me firmando* — the row's verb, the participle kept as a gerund at the Latinist's request. **Ps 24:12 *legem státuit ei* → *ele lhe firmou uma lei*** — the stylist found *firmar uma lei* unnatural and the hidden subject hard to place; the subject taken as a pronoun (D2), *firmar* kept; his *O Senhor lhe estabeleceu* is an option (constitúere's verb). Ps 30:9 *statuísti in loco spatióso pedes meos* → *firmastes os meus pés num lugar espaçoso* — the stylist's best line of Ps 30. **Ps 39:3b *Et státuit super petram pedes meos* → *E firmou os meus pés sobre uma rocha*** (passed all three readers). **Ps 49:21 *státuam contra fáciem tuam* → *porei diante da tua face*** — no object, as the Latin and the Greek (παραστήσω); DRB *set* keeps it bare. The stylist's *o porei* and MS1932 *porei tudo* options. |
| vánitas | vaidade | working | Ps 118:37 = Ps 4:3; the blind reader heard conceit about one's looks first, emptiness second; option *o que é vão* Ps 30:7 *Odísti observántes vanitátes, * supervácue* → *Vós odiais os que observam vaidades, * em vão*: the blind reader heard 'pay attention to futile things' first, practices without value second, conceit third. Kept (the row); *coisas vãs* is the option in Ps 30. **Ps 37:13 *locúti sunt vanitátes* → *falaram vaidades*:** the stylist heard pride and asked *coisas vãs*; the blind reader listed this sense unknown and heard boasting. Kept for the row (*coisas vãs* is inánia / vana's, 2:1, 11:3) — but the row's word has now been heard as conceit by readers in Pss 4/118, 30 and 37: **for the coordinator**: *o que é vão* / *coisas vazias* are the candidates that collide with nothing. Ps 39:5 *vanitátes et insánias falsas* → *as vaidades e as loucuras enganosas*: the blind reader heard conceit first again (the fourth psalm). Ps 61:10 *de vanitáte* → *pela vaidade* — the blind reader heard pride; the stylist's *por vaidade* refused (without the article it is 'out of conceit'). 61:10a *vani* → *são vãos* (order after the stylist, *vãos são* a nasal clash). |
| suspicári | recear (*a minha afronta, que receei*) | open | Ps 118:39; L&S "suspect, apprehend"; DRB *apprehended*; the cognate *suspeitar* loses the dread; kept apart from timére → temer Ps 67:16b *ut quid suspicámini montes coagulátos?* → *por que suspeitais de montes coalhados?* — the object reading (MS1932); the bare object (draft 1, meant to keep DRB's vocative) sounded broken to the stylist and the vocative was inaudible. *recear* is 118:39's context. |
| amputáre | cortar | working | Ps 118:39; the image kept; *tirar* is auférre's (118:22, 43) |
| jucúndus | agradável | open | Ps 118:39 *os vossos juízos são agradáveis*; 80:3, 103:34, 111:5, 132:1 *quam bonum et quam jucúndum*, 146:1; *suave* is suávis', *deleitoso* delectáre's |
| velle | querer | working | Ps 118:35 *porque foi ela que eu quis*; apart from desideráre → desejar and concupíscere → ansiar por Ps 5:5 *um Deus que queira a iniquidade* (MS1932 *que ame* refused). Ps 17:20 *quóniam vóluit me* → *porque me quis* (heard as 'loved me / chose me'); DRB's *was well pleased with me* is the option. Ps 21:9 *quóniam vult eum* → *porque o quer* (17:20's build); the blind reader heard 'because the Lord wants to save him' — near; *porque lhe quer bem* is the option. Ps 36:23 *et viam ejus volet* → *e ele quererá o seu caminho*: the blind reader heard the man wanting the Lord's way — the Latin leaves subject and owner open; DRB *he shall like well his way* (*se agradará do seu caminho*) the option. |
| avarítia | avareza | working | Ps 118:36 |
| humílitas / humiliáre | humilhação / humilhar | open | Ps 118:50, 67, 71, 75 (also 92, 107, 153; 9:14, 24:18 *vide humilitátem meam*); one family, as the psalm sets them together; *humildade* is heard as the virtue (but is the Magnificat's traditional word — unverified from memory); heard by the blind reader as being brought low first, affliction second **Ps 21:22 *et a córnibus unicórnium humilitátem meam* → *e dos chifres dos unicórnios a minha pequenez*** — a local departure: the object to be SAVED (a metonymy for the lowly self, like *únicam meam*); *salvai … a minha humilhação* would be heard as keeping the humiliation, and *leão … humilhação* rhymed at mediant and final. DRB *my lowness*; heard as 'my fragility'. *humilhação* is the option there. Ps 24:18 *Vede a minha humilhação e a minha fadiga* (the stylist's best line; heard as contempt suffered). Ps 30:8 *respexísti humilitátem meam* → *olhastes para a minha humilhação*; the blind reader heard 'the shame I suffered' first, the low state second. Ps 34:13b *Humiliábam in jejúnio ánimam meam* → *Humilhava com o jejum a minha alma*; 34:14 *humiliábar* → *eu me humilhava*. |
| a sǽculo | desde sempre | open | Ps 118:52; the mirror of *in sǽculum* → para sempre (89:2 *a sǽculo et usque in sǽculum*; 24:6, 92:2); hangs on either neighbour as the Latin does; option *de outrora* (DM1962) Ps 24:6 *que são desde sempre* (applied). **Ps 40:14 *a sǽculo et usque in sǽculum* → *desde sempre e para todo o sempre*** (this row + D37); the Latinist asked twice (minor) for *de século em século*, refused. 89:2 and 105:48 should copy it. |
| semper | sempre | working | Ps 118:33, 44 |
| deféctio / defícere | desfalecimento / desfalecer | open | Ps 118:53 *O desfalecimento me tomou* (118:81, 82, 123 have the verb); ἀθυμία; heard as a physical faint first; option *desânimo* **Ps 11:2 *quóniam defécit sanctus*: *desfalecer* fails with a person as subject meaning "is gone"** ("the holy man fainted"). *desapareceu o santo* (draft 1) was heard as one particular person's death → ***porque já não há santo*** (DRB "there is now no saint"), heard generically. **Stands against the Latinist (major, draft 2), who wants the perfect back** — held: resultative perfect, both Vulgate-family versions have the present state. The other *defécit* (30:11, 54:12, 72:26, 76:4, 106:5, 142:7a *defécit spíritus meus*) have things as subject and may keep *desfalecer*. **Ps 17:38 *donec defíciant* (enemies pursued) → *até que desfaleçam*:** with persons, but meaning 'give out', not 'be gone' (11:2) — the blind reader heard 'until they lose their strength' (and listed the word as unknown). Holds there. Ps 30:11 *defécit in dolóre vita mea* → *a minha vida desfaleceu na dor*; heard as 'lost strength to live'; *desfaleceu* listed as unknown by the blind reader. **Ps 36:20 *deficiéntes, quemádmodum fumus defícient* → *desfalecendo, desfalecerão como a fumaça*** (draft 2; the two forms together, as the participle + verb row, the stylist's order). The blind reader heard vanishing like smoke and listed *desfalecendo / desfalecerão* as unknown; the Latinist (minor, draft 2) asked *desvanecendo-se, desvanecer-se-ão* — refused for the row. 101:4 *defecérunt sicut fumus dies mei* should agree. Ps 38:12 *ego deféci in increpatiónibus* → *eu desfaleci nas repreensões* (a person fainting — the right sense here, unlike 11:2). The stylist wanted *eu* dropped; kept for the Latin's *ego*. **Ps 72:19 *súbito defecérunt* (persons, ἐξέλιπον 'came to an end') → *desfaleceram*; 72:26 *Defécit caro mea, et cor meum* → *Desfaleceu*** — one verb kept for the end of the wicked and the psalmist's own failing (the psalm's echo), despite the 11:2 warning about persons; the ambiguity reader listed *desfaleceram* unknown, heard nothing wrong; the Latinist passed it twice. *se acabaram* at 72:19 is the option. |
| cantábilis | digno de canto | open | Ps 118:54 (only here); L&S "worthy to be sung"; as laudábilis → digno de louvor; the cognate *cantáveis* is also "vós cantáveis"; DM1962 *cânticos* is the Hebrew's noun |
| peregrinátio | peregrinação | working | Ps 118:54; apart from íncola → forasteiro; heard as a religious journey first |
| pórtio | porção | working | Ps 118:57 (141:6); apart from pars → parte (15:5, 72:26) **Ps 49:18 *cum adúlteris portiónem tuam ponébas* → *com os adúlteros punhas a tua porção*** (μερίδα; cast in your lot). The stylist's worst line; he asked *parte* (pars's word): option, with *tomavas a tua parte* and MS1932 *fazias sociedade*. |
| deprecári / deprecátio | suplicar / prece | settled (D35) | Ps 118:58 *Supliquei a vossa face* (the face kept; 44:13 *vultum tuum deprecabúntur*); apart from oráre / orátio → oração **Ps 6:10 proposes the noun → *prece*:** *súplica* is a proparoxytone and the noun stands at a cadence in 11 of its 14 verses (*deprecatiónem meam* 6:10, 16:1a, 38:13, 54:2, 60:2, 141:7; *vocem deprecatiónis meæ* 27:6, 65:19, 85:6, 129:2b, 139:7) — rule 4. *O Senhor escutou a minha prece, * o Senhor acolheu a minha oração*: a plain word of its own beside *oração*, of the root of *precári*; passed all three readers. The verb stays *suplicar*. Wants a ruling before Ps 16. **Ps 118:169 follows the Ps 6 proposal: *Aproxime-se a minha prece*** (not at a cadence there, so *súplica* — the family of 118:58 *Supliquei* — could have stood; one word through the psalter was preferred; option in decision `deprecatio`). No reader remarked. It stands beside *postulátio* → *pedido* in 118:170. **Ps 16:1a, the place this row asked to be ruled before: *inténde deprecatiónem meam* → *atendei à minha prece*** — the Ps 6 proposal followed, the noun at the verse's end (a cadence, as the row foresaw); passed the Latinist three times, no remark from the stylist (who named 16:1a the psalm's best line) nor the blind reader. *súplica* is the option in Ps 16's decision `deprecatio`. **Proposed: settle *deprecátio → prece*** (three places now agree: 6:10, 118:169, 16:1a). Ps 21:25 *deprecatiónem páuperis* → *a prece do pobre* (a fourth place agrees; not at a cadence). **Ps 27:2, 27:6 *vocem deprecatiónis meæ* → *a voz da minha prece*** — applied (D35); 27:6 the stylist's best line. Ps 38:13 *Exáudi oratiónem meam, Dómine, et deprecatiónem meam* → *Escutai a minha oração, Senhor, e a minha prece* — this row's named place; the blind reader heard the two nouns as one thing said twice (the Latin's parallelism). The stylist's best line. Ps 44:13 *vultum tuum deprecabúntur* → *suplicarão o teu rosto* — the place this row named; heard as 'will entreat you'. Ps 60:2 *deprecatiónem meam* → *a minha prece* (at the mediant) — applied; no remark. Ps 65:19 *voci deprecatiónis meæ* → *à voz da minha prece* — applied; no remark. |
| cogitáre | pensar em | working | Ps 118:59; *considerar* is consideráre's Ps 20:12 *cogitavérunt consília* → *pensaram em desígnios* (the blind reader heard *pensaram conselhos* as advice). Ps 34:4b *cogitántes mihi mala* → *os que pensam em males contra mim*; 34:20 *dolos cogitábant* → *pensavam em enganos* — the stylist wanted a preposition (draft 1 had the bare object) and *tramar*; the row's regency taken, *tramar* option 3 (it explains). **Ps 51:4 *injustítiam cogitávit lingua tua* → *a tua língua pensou em injustiça*: the stylist (v1) and the Latinist (v2 gate, minor) both found *pensar em* weak where cogitáre has a direct object ('devise, plot'; they asked *maquinou* / *planejou*); the blind reader heard 'plotted' first.** Held for the row. **Proposal for the coordinator:** split — cogitáre + an evil thing as object (51:4, 34:4b *mala*, 20:12 *consília*; the full list wants a grep) → *maquinar*; *pensar em* elsewhere. Ps 51 decision `cogitavit` has both. Ps 61:5 *prétium meum cogitavérunt repéllere* → *pensaram em repelir o meu preço*; the stylist asked *tramaram* (option; *tramar* is concinnáre's) — a third vote for the *maquinar* split. |
| convértere | voltar (*voltei os meus pés*, *Voltem-se para mim*) | open | Ps 118:59, 79; *converter* is heard as religious conversion; will have to serve *Convérte nos, Deus* and 125:1 **Ps 6:5, 6:11:** *Voltai-vos, Senhor* (the formula: 79:15, 89:13 *Convértere, Dómine, úsquequo?* → *Voltai-vos, Senhor, até quando?*; safe: past *voltei*) … *que se voltem* (6:11 *convertántur*, nothing added: the blind reader heard retreat, turning to God and merely turning round — the Latin's three). The echo between the two ends of the psalm is kept. **Ps 7:13, 7:17:** the verb twice — *Se não vos voltardes … A sua dor se voltará sobre a sua cabeça* — one verb for the echo (the turn refused is the turn suffered). In 7:13 conversion *is* the sense, MS1932 and DRB have the cognate, and the blind reader found **a cost of *vós*: *Se não vos voltardes* can be heard as said to God** (whom 6:5 asks *Voltai-vos*), which the Latin's plural excludes; he heard repentance first, and *ele brandirá* corrects it at once. *Converterdes* is option 2 there — for Gustavo's ear. **D25: the one exception — *converter* where men are told to turn to God (7:13 *Se não vos converterdes*; “vos voltardes” was heard as said to God).** **Ps 18:8 *convértens ánimas* → *converte as almas*** — D25's exception again (the sense is conversion; DRB *converting*, MS1932 *converte*); the blind reader heard religious conversion first, as meant; *faz voltar as almas* is the option. **Ps 21:28 *converténtur ad Dóminum univérsi fines terræ* → *Hão de lembrar-se e converter-se ao Senhor*** — D25's exception (men turning to God); heard as 'come to believe in the Lord' first. *voltar-se* the option. Ps 22:2 *ánimam meam convértit* → *converteu a minha alma* (D25, the 18:8 precedent); the blind reader heard conversion to faith first. **Ps 29:12 *Convertísti planctum meum in gáudium* → *Convertestes o meu lamento em júbilo*:** with *in* + result the verb is 'turn X into Y', which Portuguese says only with *converter em* (*voltar em* is not said), and no reader heard conversion. **Proposal:** *converter em* for change-into (65:6, 77:44, 104:29, 113:8 — grep). *Mudastes* the option. Ps 31:4 *convérsus sum in ærúmna mea* → *voltei-me no meu sofrimento* (ἐστράφην). The blind reader heard turning to God first; the Latin's word carries both; kept, *revolvi-me* (MS1932, the tossing) the option. Ps 52:7 *convérterit … captivitátem* → *fizer voltar o cativeiro* (causative, as 9:4 *Ao fazer voltar para trás*); see the row *captívitas*. **Ps 50:15** *e os ímpios se converterão a vós*: D25's exception (men turning to God → *converter*) extended from the imperative to the promise; *se voltarão para vós* option. Passed the Latinist twice. |
| turbáre | perturbar | working | Ps 118:60 *não fui perturbado* (the Latinist wanted the perfect passive kept: major) Ps 6:4, 6:8 — see *conturbáre*. |

| funes | cordas | working | Ps 118:61 Ps 15:6 *Funes cecidérunt mihi in præcláris* → *As cordas me caíram em lugares excelentes*: the blind reader heard physical ropes first, the measuring lines of an allotment second; the image kept. |

The stylist found it ceremonious and asked *faz o bem* — refused for that collision; passed the Latinist twice. |
| disciplína | disciplina | working | Ps 118:66 (παιδεία); heard as keeping rules first Ps 17:36b twice (*E a vossa disciplina me corrigiu até o fim, * e a vossa própria disciplina me ensinará*); the blind reader heard correction/punishment first, instruction second — both are in the verse. Ps 49:17 *Tu vero odísti disciplínam* → *Tu, porém, odiaste a disciplina*. |
| sciéntia | o saber | open | Ps 118:66; 18:3, 93:10, 138:6; *ciência* (MS1932) was heard first as science; option *conhecimento* **Ps 18:3 *nox nocti índicat sciéntiam* → *uma noite indica o saber à outra noite*** — the place this row named; passed every reader; *o conhecimento* the option. **Ps 72:11 *Quómodo scit Deus, et si est sciéntia in excélso?* → *Como é que Deus sabe, e se há saber no alto?*** — *scit … sciéntia* → *sabe … saber*, the root kept; 72:21 *nescívi* → *não soube*. |
| crédere (+ dative) | acreditar em | open | Ps 118:66; *cri* is the Latin's length and almost never said; 115:1 *Crédidi* |
| delínquere / delíctum | cometer faltas / falta | open | Ps 118:67; L&S "to be wanting in one's duty, commit a fault"; apart from peccáre → pecar; serves 24:7 *as faltas da minha juventude*, 18:13; *delinquir* belongs to the police report **Ps 18:13, 18:14b → *falta* three times** (*Quem entende as faltas? † Das minhas faltas ocultas purificai-me … da maior falta*): the noun supplied once for *ab occúltis meis*. The blind reader heard *faltas* as sins first ('absences' only third). Passed every gate. Ps 21:2 *verba delictórum meórum* → *as palavras das minhas faltas*; the blind reader heard sins first, 'what I lack' second. **Ps 24:7, 24:8, 24:18 applied:** *Das faltas da minha juventude*, *aos que cometem faltas no caminho*, *perdoai todas as minhas faltas* — heard as 'those who err' by the blind reader; apart from peccátum → pecado (24:11). **Ps 33:22–23, the psalm's last words: *delínquent … non delínquent* → *cometerão faltas … nenhum … cometerá falta*** — one verb both times (πλημμελήσουσιν / πλημμελήσωσιν). The blind reader heard 'commit sins', rightly. Passed the Latinist twice. Ps 31:5 *Delíctum meum cógnitum tibi feci* → *Dei-vos a conhecer a minha falta* — applied; the stylist's best line of Ps 31. Ps 35:2 *ut delínquat in semetípso* → *que cometeria faltas em si mesmo* — the stylist's worst line asked *pecaria*; refused (peccáre's), an option. Passed the Latinist twice. The blind reader heard 'against himself' first, 'within himself' too. Ps 38:2 *ut non delínquam in lingua mea* → *para não cometer faltas com a minha língua* — the Greek is ἁμαρτάνειν here; the row held. The stylist found *cometer faltas* 'school register' and asked *pecar* (peccáre's; option in Ps 38 `delinquam`); the Latinist passed it twice. **Ps 58:13 *Delíctum oris eórum* → *A falta da sua boca*** (hanging accusative, no preposition supplied). The stylist and the ambiguity reader heard *falta* first as 'lack' here; the stylist asked *O delito* — refused (police word), option. |

Held, because his lines put *veníre* for *fíeri* and the build is that of 118:76, which he passed; the Latinist and the blind reader had nothing against it. For Gustavo's ear — option in decision `fiat`. **Not for *fíeri* + predicate adjective:** Ps 19:4 *holocáustum tuum pingue fiat* → *o teu holocausto se torne gordo* (the Latinist's MAJOR on *seja*: becoming, not a state; πιανάτω). | dirígere at the *vós* imperative | *endireitai* (*endireitai o meu caminho à vossa vista*) | open | Ps 5:9; *dirigi* is also "I directed" (D1/D13), and the enclitic does not save *dírige me* (24:5, 26:11: *dirigi-me* = "I made my way"). *Endireitar* is the verb's first sense (L&S "to lay straight, set in a straight line"), the Greek's (κατεύθυνον), and of the family of *diréctio* → retidão; safe (past *endireitei*). Places: 5:9, 89:16–17, 118:133; with *me* as object (24:5, 26:11) the psalm that meets it decides (*encaminhai-me* is a candidate). Options *tornai reto*; DM1962 *aplainai* is the Hebrew's image. No reader objected **Ps 118:133 *Gressus meos dírige* → *Endireitai os meus passos segundo o que dissestes***: the row followed; no remark from any reader in eight readings. Cost there: the echo with 118:128 *dirigébar* (*eu era dirigido*) is not heard. Options *Encaminhai* (MS1932), *Firmai* (DM1962, the Hebrew's). **Ps 24:5 *Dírige me* → *Encaminhai-me*** (the row's candidate; safe, past *encaminhei*), and 24:9 *Díriget* → *Encaminhará*, so the psalm's one verb is heard twice. Passed the Latinist twice; no reader remarked. 26:11 should copy. **Ps 26:11 *dírige me in sémitam rectam* → *encaminhai-me à vereda reta*** — copied as the row directs; no remark. |
| pérdere | fazer perecer (*fareis perecer todos os que falam mentira*) | settled (D24) | Ps 5:7a; the causative of *períre* → perecer, as ἀπόλλυμι is of ἀπόλλυμαι; keeps 72:27 *períbunt: perdidísti* as one figure and stays apart from *dispérdere* → exterminar, its neighbour in 142:12; 7 places (5:7a, 20:11, 25:9, 27:3, 33:17, 72:27, 142:12b), tried in each. The cognate *perdereis* (MS1932) is heard as "you will lose"; *destruir* (DRB) is banned at the *vós* imperative (*destruí*). No reader objected. **Ps 118:95 *ut pérderent me* → *para me fazer perecer*** — the same rendering, reached independently (118:92 has *periíssem* three verses before). The stylist found it "ceremonious and heavy" and drafts 8–10 had his *para me destruir* (safe as an infinitive); restored in draft 11 for this row's sake — if the row is ruled the other way, 118:95 returns to *destruir* in one touch Ps 20:11 *Fructum eórum de terra perdes* → *Fareis perecer da terra o seu fruto*. **Ps 25:9 *Ne perdas cum ímpiis, Deus, ánimam meam* → *Deus, não façais perecer com os ímpios a minha alma*** — the vocative moved first at the stylist's request; the Passiontide versicle stands alone. **Ps 33:17 *ut perdat de terra memóriam eórum* → *para fazer perecer da terra a memória deles*** — D24 followed at the place the row names. The stylist: «“Perecer da terra” não se acomoda à regência portuguesa» → *fazer desaparecer*; refused (D24), recorded. Passed the Latinist twice. **Ps 72:27 *períbunt: perdidísti* → *perecerão: fizestes perecer*** — applied as D24 names; the colon runs +6. |
| dolus / dolósus / dolóse ágere | engano / enganador / enganar (*o homem de sangue e enganador*; *com as suas línguas enganavam*) | open | Ps 5:7b, 5:11a; 19 verses (*lábia dolósa* 11:3–4, 16:1b, 30:18; *lingua dolósa* 51:6, 108:3, 119:2–3; *dolos* 34:20, 37:13, 49:19; *dolóse egit* 35:3). The cognate *doloso* is criminal law in Brazil. MS1932 has *enganadores* at 54:24. In 5:11a the light verb yields (the Greek has one verb, ἐδολιοῦσαν); *agiam com engano* is the option. **5:11a = 13:3b word for word as far as *agébant*: Ps 13 copies *A sua garganta é um sepulcro aberto, † com as suas línguas enganavam*** **Ps 11:3–4 *lábia dolósa* twice → *lábios enganadores* both times** (one decision, two slots); in 11:3 *com* is supplied (*com lábios enganadores*) so that the lips are not heard as the subject of *falaram*. No reader remarked on the adjective. Ps 9:28 *et dolo* → *e de engano*: held; no critic remarked. **Ps 14:3a *qui non egit dolum in lingua sua* → *que não usou de engano com a sua língua*** (the noun kept; the light verb *usar de*, DRB's 'used deceit'; *com* for the instrumental in, from the stylist). Not the one verb *enganar* of 5:11a / 13:3b, because 14:4b has *décipere* (→ *não engana*; Greek ἀθετῶν) — a noun and a verb of one family for two Latin words. The stylist's *praticou engano* refused: *praticar* is operári's (14:2). **23:4 *nec jurávit in dolo próximo suo* (this psalm's cousin) → *nem jurou com engano ao seu próximo*.** **Ps 16:1b *non in lábiis dolósis* → *que não é de lábios enganadores*** — the adjective held (a place this row named); the Latin's bare phrase given a relative and a copula (the blind reader and the stylist passed it; the Latinist passed it three times). *não com lábios enganadores* (11:3's *com*) is the option. **Ps 23:4 applied, reordered:** *nem jurou ao seu próximo com engano* — the pencilled *com engano ao seu próximo* would end the verse on the proparoxytone *próximo* (rule 4); heard as swearing falsely to deceive. Ps 33:14 *ne loquántur dolum* → *e os teus lábios não falem engano* (the stylist's *com engano* refused: the object is the thing said); the blind reader heard 'not tell lies'. Ps 31:2 *nec est in spíritu ejus dolus* → *e em cujo espírito não há engano* — applied. Ps 35:3 *dolóse egit* → *agiu com engano* (ágere kept, as 35:4 *agir bem*; *enganou* alone asks 'whom?'). Passed the Latinist twice. 35:4 *dolus* → *engano*. **Ps 51:4 *fecísti dolum* → *tramaste o engano*:** draft 1 *fizeste engano* (MS1932) was heard by the blind reader as 'you made a mistake' — *engano* after *fazer* is an error in daily speech; the verb gave way (decision `dolum`), losing the echo with 51:11 *quia fecísti*. Watch *fazer engano* elsewhere. **Ps 51:6 *lingua dolósa* → *língua enganadora*** (bare, vocative or apposition left open; *ó* the option). Ps 49:19 *concinnábat dolos* → *tramava enganos* (see concinnáre). **Ps 72:18 *propter dolos* → *por causa dos enganos*** — whose deceits left open, as the Latin. |
| vir sánguinum | homem de sangue | open | Ps 5:7b (DM1962's words); the Hebraism kept concrete, the plural lost (as in DRB); 25:9, 54:24 *Viri sánguinum, et dolósi* (→ *Os homens de sangue e enganadores*), 138:19; MS1932 *sanguinário* everywhere **Ps 15:4b *conventícula eórum de sanguínibus* → *os seus ajuntamentos de sangue*: the plural lost as in this row, HELD against a Latinist major twice (he wants *de sangues*).** The blind reader heard 'reuniões com derramamento de sangue' first, 'ritos com sangue' second — both readings of the plural. See the new open row *sanguínes (plural)*. Ps 54:24b applied as foreseen: *Os homens de sangue e enganadores*; the stylist's *e os enganadores* (two groups) is an option (decision `viri`); the blind reader heard violent men. |
| multitúdo (+ genitive) | multidão (*na multidão da vossa misericórdia*; *segundo a multidão das suas impiedades*) | open | Ps 5:7b, 5:11b — the psalm sets the two against each other, so one word; 17 verses (50:3b *secúndum multitúdinem miseratiónum tuárum*; 68:14 = 5:7b's phrase; 36:11 *in multitúdine pacis*; 146:4 *multitúdinem stellárum*). *Abundância* (MS1932 in 5:8) is *abundántia*'s (29:7, 71:7 *abundántia pacis*), *grandeza* *magnitúdo*'s (150:2 has both). Odd with a singular abstract, as the Latin is; no reader stumbled on it — the blind reader heard "because they are many" before "in proportion to" at 5:11b Ps 30:20 *Quam magna multitúdo dulcédinis tuæ* → *Como é grande, Senhor, a multidão da vossa doçura*: the blind reader heard people first ('multidão' suggests a crowd), quantity at once after. Ps 36:11 *in multitúdine pacis* → *na multidão da paz*: the stylist asked *numa multidão de paz* (heard as a crowd); the blind reader heard 'abundância de paz' first, a crowd second. Kept; passed the Latinist twice. **Ps 43:13 *e não houve multidão nas trocas deles*:** the Latin's openness (a great sum, or a crowd of buyers) kept; the blind reader found no clear meaning; options *abundância*, *concorrência* (MS1932). Ps 51:9 *in multitúdine divitiárum suárum* = 48:7 → *na multidão das suas riquezas*; the stylist asked *abundância* (refused: abundántia's), the blind reader heard a crowd second. Ps 63:3 *a multitúdine operántium iniquitátem* → *da multidão dos que praticam a iniquidade*. Ps 65:3 *In multitúdine virtútis tuæ* → *na multidão do vosso poder* — the ambiguity reader heard 'in the crowd' once; kept. |

| inférnus / ínferi | inferno | open | Ps 6:6 *no inferno, porém, quem vos dará graças?* — the Latin's word, kept (DRB *hell*). **The blind reader heard the hell of the damned first, the world of the dead second.** In 6:6 both are true; the hard tests are 15:10 *non derelínques ánimam meam in inférno* and 138:8 *si descéndero in inférnum, ades*; 19 verses. Options: *os infernos* (the old plural for the abode of the dead — the Creed's wording, from memory, unverified), *a mansão dos mortos* (the Creed as now said in Brazil — unverified; a paraphrase), MS1932 *habitação dos mortos*, DM1962 *limbos*. Heavy: wants a ruling **Ps 9:18 *Convertántur peccatóres in inférnum* → *Voltem os pecadores para o inferno*:** kept; here the damned's hell is no mishearing — the verse is a curse on the wicked; none of the three critics stopped at it. **Ps 17:6 *As dores do inferno me cercaram*:** kept (DRB *the sorrows of hell*); the blind reader heard the torments of the damned first, as foreseen — here the parallel *laços da morte* in the same verse points to the realm of the dead. For Gustavo with the rest of the row. **Ps 15:10 — the hard test this row named: *Quóniam non derelínques ánimam meam in inférno* → *Porque não abandonareis a minha alma no inferno*:** kept (DRB *hell*; MS1932 here *no inferno*). The Clementine of Acts, fetched (consult/bolls-VULG-44-2.json, -44-13.json), quotes the colon word for word (Acts 2:27) and applies it to Christ in 2:31 *neque derelictus est in inferno, neque caro ejus vidit corruptionem*; 13:35 quotes 15:10b. The blind reader heard, as foreseen, **the place of punishment first, the realm of the dead second**; the Latinist passed it twice; no critic asked for a change. Options in Ps 15: *nos infernos*, *na mansão dos mortos*, *na morada dos mortos* (the Creed's wordings still unverified). With 6:6, 9:18, 17:6 the row now has its four tests — ready for Gustavo's ruling. **Ps 29:4 *eduxísti ab inférno ánimam meam* → *tirastes do inferno a minha alma*:** kept (DRB *hell*; MS1932 *inferno* with a note, 'the grave'). The blind reader heard 'the place of eternal damnation' first, the world of the dead second — the fourth place so heard (after 6:6, 17:6, 15:10); here, as in 15:10, it is a soul brought back from it, where the damned's hell is the wrong sense. Passed the Latinist twice. *mundo dos mortos* is the option in Ps 29. Ps 30:18 *deducántur in inférnum* → *sejam conduzidos ao inferno* — a curse on the wicked, as 9:18; no reader stopped at it. *dedúci in* → *ser conduzido* (the passive of dúcere; D25's *fazer descer* has no sayable passive). **Ps 48:15, 15b, 16** (three places): *Como ovelhas foram postos no inferno*; *envelhecerá no inferno*; *da mão do inferno* — the wicked's fate twice, the psalmist rescued from it once (as 29:4); the blind reader heard damnation first, as foreseen. |

| dissipáre | dissipar | open | Ps 118:126 *dissiparam a vossa lei*; heard by the blind reader as "made the law disappear / destroyed it"; options *desfazer*, DM1962 *violar* (the Hebrew's); 9 verses (17:15, 32:10, 34:16, 52:6, 67:2, 67:31b, 140:8, 143:6) **Ps 17:15 *dissipávit eos* (people) → *e os dispersou*:** *dissipou* was unknown to the blind reader and heard as translated by the stylist. Cost: it meets *dispérgere* (88:11 *dispersísti inimícos tuos*; one Greek family, σκορπίζω / διασκορπίζω), never in one verse. Proposal: *dissipar* where the object is a thing (118:126 the law), *dispersar* where it is people. Ps 32:10 *Dóminus díssipat consília géntium* → *O Senhor dissipa os desígnios das nações* — passed the Latinist twice; heard rightly. Ps 34:16 *Dissipáti sunt* (people) → *Foram dispersos* — the row's proposal applied. **Ps 52:6b *dissipávit ossa* → *dispersou os ossos*** — bones take *dispersar*, as 21:15 *foram dispersos todos os meus ossos* (dispérgere, the same Greek family διασκορπίζω). Proposal for the row: *dispersar* for people and for things strewn (bones); *dissipar* for what is undone (the law 118:126, designs 32:10). *dissipou* (MS1932) option; passed the Latinist twice, no reader remarked. Ps 67:2 *dissipéntur inimíci* → *sejam dispersos*; 67:31b *Díssipa gentes* → *Dispersai as nações* — the people proposal applied; no remark. |

Ps 31:3 *dum clamárem tota die* → *enquanto eu clamava o dia todo* — applied. Ps 34:28 *tota die laudem tuam* → *o vosso louvor o dia todo* — the object first, so that the Latin's order ('o dia todo o vosso louvor') is not heard as 'all your praise'. The Tuesday Matins versicle (R. *Tota die laudem tuam, Dómine*). Ps 36:26 *Tota die miserétur et cómmodat* → *O dia todo tem piedade e empresta* — applied. |

| **elóquium — evidence from 118:81–128 for whoever reviews D16** | clause kept (118:82, 116); plural *os vossos ditos* kept (118:103); noun *o dito da vossa justiça* (118:123) | open | The **clause** passed the stylist twice and the blind reader; the Latinist, on his third reading only, marked its past tense minor (118:82, 116) — D16's named cost. The **plural** *ditos*, which had passed every reader at 118:11, was **refused by the stylist twice at 118:103** ("maxims, popular sayings … a translator's choice"). The **noun** at 118:123 was refused by him twice and understood at once by the blind reader. Alternatives for the main session: (a) stand; (b) D16's retreat, *palavra*, for the whole term (one touch: label *palavras* of decision `eloquia` in Ps 118); (c) the clause at 118:123 too — *à espera do que disse a vossa justiça* (option in decision `v123b`; it makes justice the speaker) **EVIDENCE FROM 118:129–176 (nine places; four Latinist, two stylist and two blind readings, `ps118/critic/v13–v16.*.part4.json`).** SINGULAR, the clause, six places, none of which forced a noun: 118:133 *segundo o que dissestes* · 118:140 *O que dissestes está todo em brasa … o amou* (the clause as SUBJECT, with a predicate and a pronoun pointing back) · 118:154 *pelo que dissestes vivificai-me* (after *propter*: cause is what the Latin says, so the hearing the handoff feared is here the right one) · 118:169 *conforme o que dissestes* · 118:170 *segundo o que dissestes libertai-me* · 118:172 *pronunciará o que dissestes*. **Stylist: no remark against the clause in any of the six places on either reading**; on the first reading his own lines for 118:154 and 118:169 keep it; on the second his line for 118:154 reads *pela vossa palavra*, though the faults he named there were an echo and a rhyme. **Blind reader: understood every place, twice** (118:169 → *como prometestes*); nothing misheard after a preposition. **Latinist: six minors on draft 13 and again on draft 14, all one remark — the clause dates an utterance the noun leaves timeless (D16's named cost); then NO remark on drafts 15 and 16 with the clause unchanged.** PLURAL *os vossos ditos*, three places (118:148 *meditar nos vossos ditos*, 118:158 *não guardaram os vossos ditos*, 118:162 *alegrarei com os vossos ditos*): **refused by the stylist in all three, on both readings — six of six** ("máximas ou frases célebres", "uma coleção de máximas", "coloração de máxima ou anedota", "deixam aparecer o tradutor"), each time for *palavras*; the blind reader understood it (118:158 → *Não obedeceram ao que Deus disse*) and never listed *ditos* as unknown; the Latinist never remarked on it. **So over the whole psalm the verdicts have converged: the clause has held with the ear everywhere since D16 (stylist and blind reader), at the price of a recurring Latinist minor; the plural noun is what the stylist refuses — 118:11 passed, then 118:103 twice, 118:148, 158, 162 twice each.** Alternatives for Gustavo: (a) stand; (b) *palavra(s)* for the whole term (one touch per psalm: label *palavras* of decision `eloquia`) — costs λόγιον / λόγος, and makes *secúndum elóquium tuum* = *secúndum verbum tuum*; (c) a split nobody has tried: keep the clause for the singular (15 of 20) and give only the plural (5 of 20: 118:11, 103, 148, 158, 162) to *palavras* — the ear's verdicts exactly, at the cost of one Latin noun having a clause and a borrowed noun. **Ruled in D26: a fourth way, the clause in both numbers.** |
| redímere | resgatar (*enquanto não há quem resgate, nem quem salve*) | open | Ps 7:3; λυτρόομαι; 12 verses with the present stem (grep: 7:3, 25:11, 33:23, 43:26, 48:8, 48:16, 54:19, 71:14, 102:4, 118:134, 118:154, 129:8) and 9 with the perfect (30:6 *redemísti me, Dómine, Deus veritátis* — the verse of *In manus tuas*; 70:23, 73:2, 76:15, 77:42, 105:10, 106:2, 135:24, 143:10): *resgatastes-me* must be weighed there against *remir* (*vós me remistes* — the traditional verb, from memory, unverified). The cognate fails at the four imperatives: *redimi-me* is also "I redeemed myself" (D13's test) — *resgatai-me*, *resgatai-nos* are safe. The noun *redémptio* (110:9a, 129:7) can stay *redenção*. MS1932 *livrar* is *liberáre*'s **Ps 118:134, 154 reached the same verb independently: *Resgatai-me das calúnias dos homens*; *Julgai em juízo a minha causa, e resgatai-me*** — for the reason this row gives (*Redimi-me* / *Remi-me* are also "I redeemed myself"; rule 3). The Diurnal has the verb at 118:134. Passed the Latinist four times, the stylist and the blind reader twice each, with no remark. In 118:154 it rhymes with *vivificai-me* at the cadence — the Latin's own echo (*rédime me … vivífica me*), accepted. **Ps 25:11 *rédime me* → *resgatai-me*** — applied (safe: past *resgatei*). **Ps 30:6 *redemísti me, Dómine, Deus veritátis* → *vós me resgatastes, Senhor, Deus da verdade*** — weighed against *remistes*, as this row asked: *remir* is safe in the perfect and is the Redeemer's verb, but the imperatives cannot take it (*remi-me*), so it would split one Latin verb (λυτρόομαι) in two. Serves the Compline responsory's versicle *Redemísti nos* (*Vós nos resgatastes*). Passed the Latinist twice; *remistes* is the option (decision `redemisti`). Ps 33:23 *Rédimet Dóminus ánimas servórum suórum* → *O Senhor resgatará as almas dos seus servos*; the blind reader heard salvation after death first, rescue from danger second — the Latin's range. **Ps 48:8 *Frater non rédimit, rédimet homo* → *O irmão não resgata, o homem resgatará*** (the Latin as punctuated: no question, no second *non*; MS1932's question and DRB's *nor* are options); 48:16 *Deus rédimet ánimam meam* → *Deus resgatará a minha alma*; the noun 48:9 *prétium redemptiónis* → *o preço do resgate*, to keep the psalm's one root (the redémptio note's *redenção* is the option there). Passed the Latinist twice. Ps 70:23 *quam redemísti* → *que resgatastes*. |
| rápere | arrebatar | working | Ps 7:3 *Para que não arrebate a minha alma como um leão* (MS1932); 9:30b, 21:14 *leo rápiens*, 49:22, 103:21; apart from *erípere* Ps 21:14 *sicut leo rápiens et rúgiens* → *como um leão que arrebata e ruge*; *arrebata* listed as unknown by the blind reader, kept. Ps 68:5b *quæ non rápui, tunc exsolvébam* → *o que não arrebatei, então eu pagava* — goods as object; the stylist asked *roubei*, the ambiguity reader listed *arrebatei* as unknown again (as at 21:14); kept, *roubei* the option for Gustavo. |
| réddere | pagar (*Se paguei aos que me retribuíam males*) | open | Ps 7:5 (MS1932's verb), beside *retribúere* → retribuir — the Greek has one verb twice, the Latin two; will serve *vota réddere* (*pagarei os meus votos*, 21:26, 49:14, 55:12, 60:9, 65:13, 115:5, 115:9 — grep) and *redde retributiónem* (27:4, 93:2); *pagai* is safe **Ps 21:26 *vota mea reddam* → *pagarei os meus votos*** (a place this row named); the blind reader heard religious promises first ('electoral votes' third). **Ps 27:4b *redde retributiónem eórum ipsis* → *pagai-lhes a retribuição deles*** — applied; the stylist asked *retribuí-lhes a sua retribuição* (rule 3: *retribuí* = 'I repaid') — refused, an option; 93:2 should copy. **Ps 55:12 *vota tua, quæ reddam* → *os vossos votos, que pagarei*** — the row held (decision `reddam`); the stylist found *pagar votos* commercial and asked *cumprir* (MS1932) — refused for the formula's six places, an option. Ps 49:14 *redde Altíssimo vota tua* → *paga ao Altíssimo os teus votos*. **Ps 50:14 *Redde mihi* 'give back' (ἀπόδος) → *Devolvei-me*** (a sense apart from paying): *Restituí-me* D13-legal but heard as 'I restored' at the head of a Preces versicle; *Tornai a dar-me*, *Dai-me* (MS1932) options. **Ps 60:9 *ut reddam vota mea de die in diem* → *para pagar os meus votos de dia em dia*** — the formula held against the stylist's *cumprir* (a second request, after Ps 55); the blind reader heard vows first. The colon is the R. of the Prime Preces (*V. Sic psalmum dicam …*). Ps 61:12 *quia tu reddes unicuíque juxta ópera sua* → *porque vós pagareis a cada um conforme as suas obras*; no remark. Ps 65:13 *reddam tibi vota mea* → *eu vos pagarei os meus votos* (21:26, 60:9) — subject named so the clitic can precede (the mesóclise the option). **Ps 75:12 *Vovéte, et réddite Dómino* → *Fazei votos, e pagai-os ao Senhor*** — the row with the object supplied (*pagai* bare is safe but hangs); the ambiguity reader heard *Fazei votos* as 'wish well' at first, *pagai-os* fixes it. |

Passed all three readers in Ps 9.
