Tried on 10:4 *quæ perfecísti, destruxérunt*, 17:34 *perfécit pedes meos*, 39:7 *aures autem perfecísti mihi*, 67:10, and the imperative 79:16 *Et pérfice eam* (*aperfeiçoai-a* is safe; past *aperfeiçoei*) — it fails none. The Latinist (minor) wanted *Anteciparam-se a mim*; refused. The friendly transitive places (20:4, 58:10, 87:14) are not settled by this. **Ps 16:13 *prǽveni eum* (God the subject, the enemy the object; πρόφθασον) → *adiantai-vos a ele*:** draft 1 had 17:19's *surpreendei-o* (the same Greek verb); **the Latinist marked it MAJOR** — «pede antecipação: chegar antes dele ou adiantar-se a ele» — and asked for *antecipai-vos a ele*. Taken with this row's own verb (*antecipar* is anticipáre's). So with a person as object: hostile to the speaker *surpreender* (17:19), God forestalling the enemy *adiantar-se a* (16:13). The friendly places (20:4, 58:10, 87:14) are still open. **Transitive, God forestalling with good:** Ps 20:4 *prævenísti eum in benedictiónibus dulcédinis* → *vos adiantastes a ele com bênçãos de doçura* (the Latinist's minor on draft 1's *viestes ao encontro dele*: the anticipation; his *antecipar* is anticipáre's). The stylist found it bureaucratic, the final *a mim* limp, and asked *me precederá*; the ambiguity reader heard 'go ahead of me' and also 'overtake'. Held; *me precederá* is option 2 of decision `praeveniet` — **the friendly places are still open**, and 87:14 *orátio mea prævéniet te* will test it again. |

Heard rightly ("Cumpri") **Ps 11:8, the place this row named: *servábis nos: et custódies nos* → *nos conservareis: e nos guardareis*** — with a person as object *serváre* is *conservar* (the verb of the blessing *Deus vos conserve*); *custodíre* keeps *guardar*; no reader remarked. Seven verses (48:14, 49:20, 68:23, 105:35, 118:165, 139:6 *juxta iter scándalum posuérunt mihi*, 140:9 — grep), several beside *láqueus* → laço. Heard as obstacle first, fall into sin second — both are in it. MS1932 *ocasião de queda* explains **Ps 48:14 *Hæc via illórum scándalum ipsis* → *Este caminho deles é tropeço para eles*** — the row applied; unknown to the blind reader, kept. **Ps 49:20 *advérsus fílium matris tuæ ponébas scándalum* → *contra o filho da tua mãe punhas um tropeço*** — the row applied; the article is the stylist's (draft 1 bare *tropeço* sounded clipped). MS1932 *armavas laços* changes the image. |
| gratis | sem motivo (*Os príncipes me perseguiram sem motivo*) | open | Ps 118:161 (DM1962's phrase; δωρεάν). Six verses (34:7, 34:19, 68:5 *odérunt me gratis*, 108:3, 118:161, 119:7 — grep). *De graça* is free of charge; *sem causa* is *sine causa*'s (3:8) — MS1932 and DRB merge them Ps 34:7 and 34:19 applied (*sem motivo*), 34:7 beside supervácue → *em vão*, as the row foresaw. |

Five verses (18:3 *Dies diéi erúctat verbum*, 44:2a *Eructávit cor meum verbum bonum*, 118:171, 143:13, 144:7 — grep); tried on 44:2a and 144:7. Passed every reader. Ps 18:9 *illúminans óculos* → *ilumina os olhos*, beside *luminoso* for lúcidum (the pair); heard as 'gives understanding'. **Ps 30:17 *Illústra fáciem tuam super servum tuum* → *Iluminai a vossa face sobre o vosso servo*** — illustráre (only here) is the same Greek as 118:135 (ἐπίφανον τὸ πρόσωπόν σου ἐπὶ τὸν δοῦλόν σου, both in Rahlfs), so D15's test merges them and the colon is 118:135a word for word (formula row). Draft 1 had *olham para* (MS1932); the stylist refused *os olhos olham* (a jingle the Latin does not have) and gave *estão voltados para* — a participle of state, not the reflexive *voltar-se* that is *convértere*'s (6:5); it lets the re- be heard; the Latinist passed it. Passed the Latinist twice; no reader remarked. **Ps 24:16 *Réspice in me* → *Olhai para mim*** (118:132's words), 24:19 *Réspice inimícos meos* → *Olhai para os meus inimigos*; **24:15 the verbless *Óculi mei semper ad Dóminum* → *Os meus olhos estão sempre voltados para o Senhor*** — this row's 10:5b build, taken from the stylist in draft 2 (draft 1 verbless, as the Latin and the Matins antiphon). Ps 65:7 *óculi ejus super gentes respíciunt* → *os seus olhos estão voltados para as nações* — 10:5b's formula with *super*; no remark. |

| calix / pars | cálice / parte (*são a parte do cálice deles*) | working | Ps 10:7; 15:5 *pars hereditátis meæ et cálicis mei*, 22:5, 74:8, 115:4 *Cálicem salutáris*. *Cálice* is a proparoxytone: at a cadence it wants a word after it (10:7 *do cálice deles*, which also keeps *seu* from being heard as God's). *pórtio* keeps *porção*. The blind reader heard the cup's sense — the lot of punishment — at once **Ps 15:5 *Dóminus pars hereditátis meæ, et cálicis mei* → *O Senhor é a parte da minha herança e do meu cálice*:** the proparoxytone at the mediant accepted; the stylist's *A parte … e do meu cálice é o Senhor* (the mediant on *Senhor*) refused — the verse opens on Dóminus and turns to *sois vós* — kept as an option, with *e do cálice meu*. **Ps 74:8 *calix in manu Dómini vini meri plenus misto* → *na mão do Senhor há um cálice de vinho puro, cheio de mistura*** — *há* supplied; the paradox kept (merum → *puro*, mistum → *mistura*). The ambiguity reader heard the Eucharistic chalice first and the paradox as a contradiction, as the Latin is. |
| justítiæ (plural) | as justiças (*e amou as justiças*) | open | Ps 10:8, where the Greek is δικαιοσύνας (plural of δικαιοσύνη); DRB and MS1932 make it singular. Heard by one blind reading as 'just acts', by another as 'justice as a quality' — never as the courts, which was the risk. The other plurals (17:23, 49:16, 74:3, 88:32, 147:8 — grep) are in several places δικαιώματα and should decide locally (*preceitos* may be wanted) **Ps 17:23 *justítias ejus* (δικαιώματα) → *as suas justiças***, not *preceitos*: the stanza is built on the word (*justítiam meam* 17:21 — *justítias ejus* 23 — *justítiam meam* 25). The blind reader was unsure of the plural's sense (norms? acts of justice?); never heard as the courts. Passed every reader. **Ps 49:16 *justítias meas* → *as minhas justiças*** — the place this row left open; τὰ δικαιώματά μου, as 17:23 (*as suas justiças*). *os meus preceitos* (D15's justificatiónes) and MS1932 *mandamentos* options. **Ps 74:3 *ego justítias judicábo* (εὐθύτητας) → *eu julgarei as justiças*** — the internal object kept with the row's plural; the ambiguity reader heard 'judge with justice' (right) but found the plural puzzling and listed it unknown; the Latinist passed it twice. *com justiça* (MS1932) and *retamente* (57:2) options. |
| vultus | rosto (*o seu rosto viu a equidade*) | working | Ps 4:7 *a luz do vosso rosto*, Ps 10:8 (the Latin's reading — his face saw equity — not the Hebrew's 'the upright shall see his face'); keeps *face* for fácies Ps 20:7 *cum vultu tuo* → *com o vosso rosto*; 20:10 *in témpore vultus tui* → *no tempo do vosso rosto* (the image, unexplained); 20:13 *vultum eórum* → *o rosto deles*. Ps 66:2 *vultum suum* → *o seu rosto* (with *iluminar*; see the illumináre row). |
| sedes (God's) | trono (*o Senhor tem no céu o seu trono*) | working | Ps 10:5a (θρόνος); *sede* is headquarters or thirst. Ps 44:7 *Sedes tua, Deus* → *O teu trono, ó Deus* (the king addressed as God). |
| inops / pauper / egénus | carente / pobre / (necessitado) | settled (D38) | Ps 11:6a *Por causa da miséria dos indigentes, e do gemido dos pobres*. The Latin keeps three words (and *mendícus*, 39:18, 108:17) and pairs them constantly: *inops* 14 lines (13:6, 34:10, 36:14, 71:13, 73:21 *pauper et inops*, 85:1 *inops, et pauper sum ego*, 108:17, 112:7, 139:13 …), *egénus* 6 (34:10, 40:2, 69:6, 81:3–4, 108:22 — grep). Proposed: *pauper → pobre*; *inops* ("without means") → *indigente*, whose noun *indigência* serves *inópia* (33:10, 43:24, 87:9, 106:41); *egénus → necessitado*, reserved. **The blind reader listed *indigentes* as unknown twice**; options *desvalidos* (MS1932), *necessitados*. The stylist named 11:6a the psalm's best line **Ps 9 (stage one + two): *pauper* → *pobre* in 12 places (9:10, 13, 19 ×2, 23, 30a, 30b ×2, 31, 33, 35b, 38); *húmilis* 9:39 → *humilde* (the blind reader heard 'poor and simple', beside *órfão*) — proposed as the fourth member: *húmilis → humilde*, *humílitas → humilhação* (9:14).** **D27: húmilis → humilde settled beside pauper → pobre; inops stays open (indigente was unknown to the blind reader twice).** **Ps 13:6 *consílium ínopis* → *o conselho do indigente***, kept under this row: **listed as unknown by the blind reader for the third time** (Ps 11 twice, Ps 13 once). Heard otherwise rightly (the next colon's *a sua esperança* taken as his). The evidence against *indigente* is now three readings of three; if it is to change, *desvalido* (MS1932 in Ps 11) is the candidate that keeps *pobre* for pauper. egére (the verb), Ps 15:2 (DO 15:1) *quóniam bonórum meórum non eges* → *porque não precisais dos meus bens* — the plain verb; *não tendes necessidade* (MS1932, the link to *necessitado*) is the option. Only place (grep). **Ps 33:10 *quóniam non est inópia timéntibus eum* → draft 2 *porque nada falta aos que o temem*:** draft 1's *não há indigência* (this row's proposal) was unknown to the blind reader and 'administrative' to the stylist; the noun turned into the verb *faltar* (Douay-Rheims 'there is no want to them that fear him'). *indigência* is option 2 in Ps 33's decision `inopia`. **Evidence against *indigência* for inópia (43:24, 87:9, 106:41 still to come):** the noun fared no better than the adjective. 33:7 *Iste pauper* → *Este pobre* (the blind reader heard the speaker himself). 33:11 *Dívites eguérunt* → *Os ricos passaram necessidade e fome*, egére kept apart from inópia. **Ps 34:10b — all three in one verse: *Erípiens ínopem … egénum et páuperem* → *Vós que arrancais o carente … o necessitado e o pobre*.** Draft 1 tried *desvalido* (Matos Soares 1932): unknown to the blind reader, like *indigente* before it. Draft 2 *carente* passed the Latinist. **Proposal for the row: inops → *carente* (known, and apart from *pobre* and *necessitado*); *indigente* has now failed three blind readers and *desvalido* one.** **Ps 36:14b *páuperem et ínopem* → *o pobre e o indigente*: unknown to the blind reader for the FOURTH time in four readings (Pss 11 ×2, 13, 36).** Kept on the row so the evidence stays in one place. **Proposal for a ruling: *inops* → *desvalido* psalter-wide** (MS1932 in Ps 11; keeps *pobre* for pauper and the pair's two words). **D38 (review 2026-09-21): inops → *carente*** in all four finished places — 11:6a *dos carentes*, 13:6 *do carente*, 34:10b, 36:14b *o pobre e o carente* (Pss 11 draft 3, 13 draft 3, 36 draft 5). *Indigente* was unknown to four blind readers of four; *desvalido* to one. Both stay options in each psalm. *inópia* (33:10, 43:24, 87:9, 106:41) is not ruled by D38. **Ps 40:2 *super egénum, et páuperem* → *acerca do necessitado e do pobre*** — egénus → *necessitado* beside *pobre*, as 34:10b; no reader stopped at either word. **Ps 71:13 *páuperi et ínopi* → *o pobre e o carente*** (D38); the stylist asked *necessitado* ('social-work vocabulary') — refused: egénus's word. |

| fiduciáliter (ágere) | com confiança (*nele agirei com confiança*) | open | Ps 11:6b only (L&S cite it); παρρησιάσομαι. *nele* as open as *in eo*. The blind reader heard the colon as the psalmist's, twice — the Latin marks the Lord's words off no better. MS1932 *confiadamente* |
| diminúere, *diminútæ sunt* | diminuir, intransitive (*diminuíram as verdades*) | open | Ps 11:2 only; ὠλιγώθησαν "became few"; the intransitive for a medio-passive, as *se elevou* (8:2b). | altitúdo | altura (*segundo a vossa altura*) | open | Ps 11:9 (of God); 55:4, 68:3 *altitúdinem maris*, 94:4 *altitúdines móntium*, 102:11 *secúndum altitúdinem cæli* (grep) — the concrete word everywhere; *alteza* is a royal title; the blind reader heard "greatness" first **Ps 55:4 *Ab altitúdine diéi timébo* → *Da altura do dia temerei*** — the concrete word kept at the crux (noon, or height as pride); the Latinist (v1) asked plain *da* for draft 1's *desde*, which made *ab* only temporal. MS1932 *desde que desponta o dia* refused. The blind reader listed *altura do dia* as unclear — the Latin's own obscurity. Ps 68:3b *Veni in altitúdinem maris* → *Cheguei ao alto-mar* (MS1932) — *à altura do mar* is heard as a position. The gate Latinist (minor) asked *à profundeza do mar* (the Greek's βάθη); held: it would merge with *profúndum → profundeza* (68:3, 68:16). Option. |

| próximus | próximo | working | Ps 11:3; a proparoxytone: at a cadence it needs a word after it (11:3 was recast so that the mediant falls on *coisas vãs*); 14:3b, 14:4b, 23:4, 27:3, 34:14, 37:12 (grep) Ps 14:3b *Nec fecit próximo suo malum* → *nem fez ao seu próximo o mal* (the Latin's order, so that the mediant falls on *mal*; the stylist's *fez mal ao seu próximo* is the option, refused under rule 4) and *e contra os seus próximos não aceitou afronta* (the phrase fronted so that the verse does not end on *próximos*). 14:4b *jura ao seu próximo* (not at a cadence). **Ps 27:3b *Qui loquúntur pacem cum próximo suo* → *Os que com o seu próximo falam de paz*** — the proparoxytone kept off the cadence, as the row asks. Ps 34:14 *Quasi próximum* → *como a um próximo*, off the cadence (draft 2 puts the *sic* clause first). **Ps 37:12 *Amíci mei, et próximi mei ** → *Os meus amigos e os meus próximos ** — the one place where the proparoxytone stands at the mediant: draft 1's postposed *Os amigos meus e os próximos meus* (to save the cadence) was refused by the stylist as calqued and precious; the plain order won (D2: the ear leads on order). His *vizinhos* refused (vicínus's; and *próximos … aproximaram* echoes *próximi … appropinquavérunt*). Ps 44:15b *próximæ ejus* (αἱ πλησίον, the bride's companions) → *as que lhe são próximas* (the root kept; MS1932 *companheiras* would merge with 44:8 *consórtes*). |
| lábia … a nobis sunt | os nossos lábios são nossos | open | Ps 11:5; possession (DRB *our own*, MS1932 *somos donos*); the Latinist (draft 2, minor) prefers origin, *vêm de nós* — option |

DRB, DM1962 *duplo coração* and MS1932 *coração dúplice* explain |

| obdormíre | adormecer (*para que eu nunca adormeça na morte*) | open | Ps 12:4b; 43:23 *quare obdórmis, Dómine?* (of God) is the only other (grep) and should follow (*por que adormeceis*); the blind reader heard "so that I do not die" first; *dormir* is *dormíre*'s (3:6, 4:9) **Ps 43:23 *por que adormeceis, Senhor?*** — applied; no reader remarked. |
| movéri (*motus fúero*, *movébor*) | ser abalado (*se eu for abalado*) | open | Ps 12:5b; *Non movébor* 9:27 (Ps 9 agent: *Não serei abalado*, agreeing), 29:7, 61:3; 93:18 *Motus est pes meus*. Heard as shaken in spirit first, defeated second — the Latin's range. *vacilar*, *cair* are options **Ps 14:5b *non movébitur in ætérnum* → *para sempre não será abalado*:** D23's words, the adverb before the negation as 118:93, so that 'never' is the only hearing (the blind reader: 'Permanecerá firme'). **The stylist's worst line** ('soa estrangeira'); he wants *jamais será abalado* (the Diurnal's), which drops in ætérnum's word — option in Ps 14. 124:1 *non commovébitur in ætérnum* and 111:6 *in ætérnum non commovébitur* will meet the same choice; commovéri (15:8 *ne commóvear*, 13 lines) shares the verb, one Greek (σαλεύω). **commovéri, Ps 15:8 *ne commóvear* → *para eu não ser abalado*** (the 118:80 build; one Greek, σαλεύω); = Acts 2:25 (fetched). Passed the Latinist twice. **Merged from a duplicate row (*Non movébor*):** Ps 9:27, 29:7, 61:3 (grep) — the proud man's boast and the just man's trust in one phrase. **Ps 16:5 *ut non moveántur vestígia mea* → *para que não sejam abaladas as minhas pegadas*** — the row's verb with a thing (footprints) as subject, as 93:18 *Motus est pes meus* will need; the blind reader heard 'that the steps stay firm' first; no other remark. *não vacilem* (MS1932) is the option. Ps 20:8 *non commovébitur* → *não será abalado*. **The active, Ps 28:7:** *vox Dómini concutiéntis desértum: et commovébit Dóminus desértum Cades* → *a voz do Senhor que abala o deserto: e o Senhor abalará o deserto de Cades* — concútere and commovére are one Greek verb (συσσείοντος … συσσείσει), so one Portuguese verb, the active of this row's *ser abalado*. Passed the Latinist twice. Ps 35:12 the active *non móveat me* → *não me abale* (σαλεύσαι); the blind reader heard faith / composure shaken first — the row's hearing. Passed the Latinist twice. **Ps 47:6 *commóti sunt* → *foram abalados*** (σαλεύω); the stylist's *abalaram-se* refused — colloquially 'took off'. Ps 61:3 *non movébor ámplius* → *já não serei abalado* (no remark); kept apart from 61:7 *non emigrábo* (*não partirei*). **Ps 72:2 *pæne moti sunt pedes* → *quase foram abalados*** — the passive kept; no reader remarked. *vacilaram* (MS1932) option. |
| præválere (advérsus) | prevalecer (contra) (*Prevaleci contra ele*) | open | Ps 12:4b(5a); 51:9 *et præváluit in vanitáte sua*, 64:4 *Verba iniquórum prævaluérunt super nos* (grep, 3 lines); the Latin's own descendant **Ps 51:9 *et præváluit in vanitáte sua* → *e prevaleceu na sua vaidade*:** the stylist asked *se fez forte* (the Greek's ἐδυναμώθη) — refused, rule 1; the blind reader heard 'won out through his pride'. |
| per diem | durante o dia | open | Ps 12:2; heard as "all day long" first; *cada dia* (MS1932), *o dia todo* are options; *tota die* (37:7 …) is another phrase and should keep *o dia todo* Ps 21:3 *clamábo per diem … et nocte* → *clamarei durante o dia … e de noite* (MS1932's pair): the row held beside *nocte*; *de dia* is the option. |
| bona tríbuere | dar coisas boas (*que me deu coisas boas*) | open | Ps 12:6b; draft 1 *que me concedeu bens* was heard by the blind reader as property and called administrative by the stylist, whose wording this is; the Greek has τῷ εὐεργετήσαντί με. *tríbuere* and *dare* never meet in a verse, so *dar* is free; 15:7 *qui tríbuit mihi intelléctum* can read *que me deu entendimento* Ps 15:7 *qui tríbuit mihi intelléctum* → *que me deu entendimento*, as this row proposed; passed the Latinist twice. |
| in finem | para sempre where it says perpetuity (each psalm decides) | working (D27) | Ps 9:7a, 9:19 ×2, 9:32 (*ne vídeat in finem* → *para nunca mais ver*). εἰς τέλος. Draft 1 had *até o fim*: in the negation of 9:19 (*non in finem … non períbit in finem*) the Latinist (major) and the blind reader both heard a bounded 'not until the end' — i.e. it will perish at the end. *para sempre* everywhere since; 9:32 *nunca mais* where the negative is a final clause. Affects 12:1, 43:23, 67:17b, 73:1, 3, 10, 11, 19, 76:9, 78:5, 88:47, 51:7 (grep, 19 lines); **not** *usque in finem* (15:11, 37:7), and 17:36b, 48:9 want their own reading. **D27: NOT merged with in ætérnum — mostly another Greek phrase (εἰς τέλος), and 48:9 has both side by side. The wordings of Pss 9 and 12 stand.** **Ps 17:36b, the place D27 named: *corréxit me in finem* → *me corrigiu até o fim*** — not *para sempre*: nothing is negated (the failure at 9:19 was *não … até o fim*), and with a past act *para sempre* would make the correcting perpetual; εἰς τέλος, DRB *unto the end*, MS1932 *até ao fim*. The blind reader heard 'corrected me completely' first — the other sense of εἰς τέλος — and 'until the end of my life' second. Passed the Latinist twice. So *in finem* has two renderings, as D27 allowed: *para sempre* (perpetuity) and *até o fim* (Ps 17 decision `finem`, *para sempre* the option). **Merged from a duplicate row:** Ps 12:1 *me esquecereis para sempre?* (Ps 9 proposed it for 9:7a, 9:19, 9:32; followed). 22 lines have *finem* (grep), the adverbial *in finem* in most: 43:23, 51:7, 67:17b, 73:1, 73:3, 73:10, 73:11, 73:19, 76:9, 78:5, 88:47 … — with *úsquequo* three times (12:1, 78:5, 88:47), where "how long … for ever?" is the Latin's own. **Collides with D23 *in ætérnum* → para sempre** where both occur near each other (48:9 has both in one colon: *in ætérnum, et vivet adhuc in finem*) — 48:9 must decide locally; *até o fim* is the option. The blind reader heard 12:1 as "forgotten me for good?" **D27: NOT merged with in ætérnum — mostly another Greek phrase (εἰς τέλος), and 48:9 has both side by side. The wordings of Pss 9 and 12 stand.** **usque in finem, Ps 15:11 *delectatiónes in déxtera tua usque in finem* → *deleites na vossa direita até o fim*** (D27 keeps usque in finem apart; affirmative, so none of 9:19's negation fault); passed the Latinist twice; the blind reader heard 'to the end of my life' first, 'of time' second. *para sempre* is the option. 37:7 *usque in finem* can follow. Ps 37:7 *curvátus sum usque in finem* → *fiquei curvado até o fim* (as 15:11, *usque in finem*); the blind reader heard it temporal ('until the end'); DRB 'even to the end'. Kept. **Ps 48:9, the place D27 named: *laborábit in ætérnum, et vivet adhuc in finem* → *trabalhará para sempre, e viverá ainda até o fim*** — the two phrases kept two; affirmative, so 9:19's fault does not arise. Passed the Latinist twice. **Ps 51:7 *déstruet te in finem* → *te destruirá para sempre*** — the place D27 named: a future of destruction, affirmative; DRB, MS1932 'for ever'; *até o fim* (heard as a limit) and *por completo* are options. Passed the Latinist twice. **Ps 73 (five places):** *para sempre* at 73:1, 73:10 (the *Úsquequo … in finem?* formula), 73:11, 73:19 (*não esqueçais para sempre*); **73:3 *Leva manus tuas … in finem* → *até o fim*** (17:36b's sense, utterly: *para sempre* would make the lifted hands perpetual). No reader remarked. |
| orbis terræ | o mundo (*julgará o mundo com equidade*); *o orbe da terra* the option | settled (D30) | Ps 9:8b (twin 95:13, grep). The Latin's word; **the blind reader listed *orbe* as unknown**; options *o mundo*, *a terra inteira*. **Ps 17:16a *fundaménta orbis terrárum* → *os fundamentos do mundo*:** *orbe* failed again — unknown to the blind reader (as in Ps 9) and 'livresca' for the stylist. **The Latin psalter never has *mundus* for the world** (grep: *mundus* is only 'clean', 18:13, 23:4, 50:4, 50:9, 50:12), so *mundo* collides with nothing. **Proposed: the row → *o mundo* in all 12 lines** (9:8b, 18:5, 23:1, 49:12, 71:8, 88:12, 92:1b, 95:10, 95:13, 97:6, 97:9 — *o orbe da terra* the option); Ps 9:8b would follow. **Ps 18:5 *in fines orbis terræ* → *até os confins do mundo*** — D30 held at the verse St Paul quotes (Rom 10:18, word for word in the Vulgate; τῆς οἰκουμένης). **Ps 23:1 applied:** *o mundo, e todos os que nele habitam* — the masculine lets *nele* (in eo) and 23:2 *o fundou … o preparou* (eum) point to the world, as the Latin's pronouns point to orbis (the Hebrew's are feminine, the earth). Passed the Latinist twice; no reader stopped. Ps 32:8 *omnes inhabitántes orbem* → *todos os que habitam o mundo* (D30). Ps 49:12 *meus est enim orbis terræ* → *pois meu é o mundo* (D30); the Latinist's *o orbe da terra* refused. |

Open as the Latin (not folly in me / not to my folly). Ps 37:6 *a fácie insipiéntiæ meæ* → *diante da minha insensatez* (the noun, as the row names); the blind reader listed *insensatez* unknown; kept. **Ps 48:11 *insípiens … stultus* → *o insensato e o tolo*** (stultus → *tolo*, new row); **48:13 = 48:21 *juméntis insipiéntibus* → *aos animais insensatos*** — the adjective kept on the beasts so that the refrain echoes 48:11 (MS1932 *sem razão* option). Ps 36:38 *Injústi autem disperíbunt simul* → *Mas os injustos juntos perecerão de todo* (the stylist's order, *juntos* next to the verb). Ps 48:3 see *in unum*. **Ps 48:11 *simul insípiens, et stultus períbunt* → *juntos perecerão o insensato e o tolo*** (36:38's order). **Ps 73:8 *cognátio eórum simul* → *os da parentela deles, juntos*** — = 73:6 *in idípsum* (one Greek). |

Elsewhere *esca* is food given (68:22, 103:21, 135:25 *Qui dat escam omni carni*, 144:15 …, 17 lines) and will be *alimento* or *comida* **Ps 52:5 *ut cibum panis* → *como quem come pão*, copied from 13:4.** The Latinist of Ps 52 asked *como alimento de pão* on both reads (minor), the stylist offered *como se come o pão* (the *como … come* jingle, which he said to keep) — both refused for the twin, options in Ps 52's decision `cibum`. The second colon (*dentre os filhos dos homens*) tells the ear it is offspring. **Proposal for Gustavo: *semente* where the image is paired with fruit or harvest (20:11), and psalter-wide if the ear bears it; *descendência* only if the ear fails on *semen Jacob / Ábraham* (21:24, 104:6).** **Ps 21:24 *univérsum semen Jacob* (the test D32 named), 21:25 *omne semen Israël*, 21:31 *semen meum* → *descendência* ×3** (one decision `semen`, three slots); no plant image in these verses; *semente* the option. No reader remarked. Ps 24:13 *e a sua descendência herdará a terra* (applied). Ps 36:25, 26, 28b → *descendência* ×3 (*nem a sua descendência buscando pão*; *e a sua descendência será abençoada*; *e a descendência dos ímpios perecerá*). No plant image; no reader remarked. Ps 68:37 *Et semen servórum ejus* → *E a descendência dos seus servos* — applied; the Latinist (v1, minor) asked *semente*, refused under D32. |

In Portuguese *sangue* is a mass noun and *sangues* is heard as a slip; one ruling must serve all six places. **For Gustavo.** Ps 50:16 applied *Livrai-me do sangue*; the Latinist asked for *dos sangues* at both reads — refused by this ruling; the ambiguity reader heard bloodshed rather than bloodguilt either way. |

| ecclésia | assembleia (*no meio da assembleia vos louvarei*; *na grande assembleia*) | settled (D34); was: open — **for a ruling** (with *concílium*) | Ps 21:23, 21:26. Ten lines, ἐκκλησία in all that were read (grep; Greek read at 25:5, 25:12, 39:10): 21:23 (= Heb 2:12, whose Clementine has *in medio ecclesiæ*, fetched), 21:26, 25:5 *Odívi ecclésiam malignántium*, 25:12 *in ecclésiis benedícam te*, 34:18 and 39:10 *in ecclésia magna* (21:26's words), 67:27 *In ecclésiis benedícite Deo*, 88:6 and 149:1 *in ecclésia sanctórum*, 106:32 *in ecclésia plebis*. ***igreja*** (MS1932 *no meio da Igreja*, DRB *church*; the Church's reading of 21:23 through Heb 2:12) was weighed on D19's reasoning for *Christus* and refused: it cannot serve 25:5 (*a igreja dos malvados*), and at 67:27 and 21:26 (*na grande igreja*) it is heard as a building. So one word for all ten, which forces *concílium* elsewhere (row *concílium*). Passed the Latinist twice; no reader remarked; the blind reader heard *irmãos* (21:23) as the religious community. Option in Ps 21's decision `concilium`: *da igreja / na grande igreja*. **Ps 25:5 *Odívi ecclésiam malignántium* → *Odeio a assembleia dos malvados*; 25:12 *in ecclésiis benedícam te, Dómine* → *nas assembleias vos bendirei, Senhor*** (D34 applied; the Lavabo's last line). | éruere | arrancar (*Arrancai da espada, Deus, a minha alma*) | open | Ps 21:21. 13 lines (grep: 21:21, 24:17, 24:20, 31:7, 36:40, 38:9, 42:1, 49:15, 85:13, 88:49 ×2, 143:11 …), nearly always *érue me* with a source (*de, a, ab*); L&S 'pluck out, dig out; rescue'. The Greek is ῥύομαι, as behind liberáre and erípere, but the Latin keeps three verbs and **36:40 sets *liberábit … éruet* side by side**, so not *livrar*. Shares *arrancar* with erípere-with-a-source (the two never stand in one verse — grep before a psalm that has both); 24:20 *et érue me* (no source) and 49:15 *éruam te* will want the erípere row's *libertar*. MS1932 *Livra*. Passed the Latinist twice; no reader remarked. **Ps 24:17, 24:20 → *libertai-me* both times** — the psalm says *érue me* twice (24:17 with a source, 24:20 without), so one wording is heard twice; *arrancai-me* fails at 24:20. *arrancar* then serves evéllere in 24:15. Proposal: arrancar with a source, libertar without, and libertar in both places where one psalm has both. Passed the Latinist twice. Passed the Latinist twice; no reader remarked. Ps 38:9 *Ab ómnibus iniquitátibus meis érue me* → *Arrancai-me de todas as minhas iniquidades* (a source named, as 31:7); passed the Latinist twice. Ps 39:14 *ut éruas me* (no source) → *libertar* (*que me liberteis*), as 30:3. **Ps 49:15 *éruam te* → *eu te libertarei*** — the row's proposal (no source → *libertar*). The Monday Prime antiphon adapts it (*… ascéndet in montem Dómini*). 24:21 *Innocéntes et recti* → *Os inocentes e os retos* |

The blind reader heard waiting for God to act — the verb's sense **Ps 26:14 *sústine Dóminum* → *espera pelo Senhor*** — see exspectáre: the three verbs apart. The blind reader heard 'without result' first, 'without cause' second Ps 30:7 — alone after the asterisk (*em vão*); whether it goes with *odiais* or with *observam* stays open, as in the Latin (the blind reader heard both). Ps 37:17 *supergáudeant mihi* → *se alegrem à minha custa* — the family's third place, applied. | petra | rocha (*Sobre uma rocha me exaltou*) | open | Ps 26:6; *lapis* → pedra (117:22). Indefinite, as the Latin has no article. **60:3 *In petra exaltásti me* should copy.** 12 other lines (grep: 39:3, 60:3, 77:15, 77:16, 77:20, 80:17, 103:12, 103:17, 104:41, 113:8, 136:9, 140:6). Ps 60:3 *in petra exaltásti me* → *sobre uma rocha me exaltastes* — copied from 26:6 as the row asked; the stylist's *sobre a rocha* refused. |
| hóstia | vítima (*uma vítima de aclamação*) | open | Ps 26:6b; the cognate *hóstia* is heard first as the Host, *sacrifício* is sacrifícium's (115:17 *tibi sacrificábo hóstiam laudis*, DO id 115:7b, has both roots, so they must differ). The blind reader heard *vítima* as someone harmed (he did not know *imolei*) — the cost, recorded. Other *hóstia*: 95:8 *Tóllite hóstias*, 115:17 (DO id 115:7b) — grep. |
| vociferátio | aclamação | open | Ps 26:6b *hóstiam vociferatiónis*; 32:3 *bene psállite ei in vociferatióne* can follow. ἀλαλαγμός, the shout. *júbilo* left to gáudium / jubilátio (46:6). **Ps 32:3 *bene psállite ei in vociferatióne* → *entoai-lhe bem os salmos com aclamação*** — the row followed; passed the Latinist twice; heard rightly. |
| viríliter ágere | portar-se como homem (*porta-te como homem*) | open | Ps 26:14; **30:25 *Viríliter ágite* → *Portai-vos como homens*** (only the two, grep). Draft 1 had MS1932's *porta-te varonilmente*: the blind reader did not know *varonilmente*, the stylist (worst line) called it bookish and asked *age como homem*; his *como homem* taken (the Latin's vir), the verb kept for 30:25, where *Agi* is also 'I acted' (rule 3). **Ps 30:25 *Viríliter ágite* → *Portai-vos como homens*** — applied as proposed (*Agi* is 'I acted'); the blind reader heard 'be brave' first, 'as males' second. Passed the Latinist twice; no stylist remark. |
| silére / tacére | *ficar em silêncio* / *calar-se* (*não fiqueis em silêncio comigo: * para que não vos caleis comigo*) | open | Ps 27:1 — two Latin verbs where the Greek has one twice (παρασιωπάω); kept two (rule 1); *a me* → *comigo* (the Brazilian idiom: silent towards me). Other places (grep before copying): 34:22, 38:3, 38:13, 49:3, 49:20, 82:2, 106:29–30, 108:2, 31:3 (grep). No reader remarked. Ps 31:3 *Quóniam tácui* → *Porque me calei* — applied. Ps 34:22 *Vidísti, Dómine, ne síleas* → *Vós vistes, Senhor, não fiqueis em silêncio* — applied; the Passiontide responsory; the stylist's best line. **Ps 38:3 *sílui a bonis* → *fiquei em silêncio quanto às coisas boas*** (draft 1 *sobre coisas boas*: topic, refused by the Latinist and the stylist; draft 2 *longe do bem*: spatial, refused at the gate; *quanto às* is the gate's fix and keeps both readings — withheld even good things / silent about them). Both readers asked for *calar-se* — refused, tacére's verb, and 38:13b *Ne síleas* → *Não fiqueis em silêncio* (= 34:22) is the same Latin verb. **Ps 49:3 *et non silébit* → *e não ficará em silêncio*; 49:20 *et tácui* → *e eu me calei*** — the two verbs kept apart in one psalm (the Greek differs too: παρασιωπήσεται / ἐσίγησα). The stylist asked *se calará* at 49:3 — option. |
| adinvéntio | invenções (*segundo a maldade das suas invenções*) | open | Ps 27:4; six lines (27:4, 76:13 God's *in adinventiónibus tuis*, 80:13, 98:8, 105:29, 105:39) — must serve God's works too, so not *maquinações*; the blind reader heard schemes (the sense, from *maldade*). |
| salvátio | salvações (*o protetor das salvações do seu Cristo*) | open | Ps 27:8 only (grep), plural for σωτηρίων, as 17:51 *salútes* → *salvações*; the blind reader doubted the plural — the Latin's. |
| ex voluntáte mea | *de minha própria vontade* (*e de minha própria vontade lhe darei graças*) | open | Ps 27:7b (ἐκ θελήματός μου); draft 1 *de minha vontade* sounded incomplete to the stylist; *própria* completes the idiom and ex → *de* stays; *de livre vontade* is voluntárie's (53:8). |
| refloréscere | reflorescer (*E refloresceu a minha carne*) | open | Ps 27:7b only (grep); the blind reader heard the body's vigour restored. |
| commendáre | entregar (*Nas vossas mãos entrego o meu espírito*) | open | Ps 30:6, only place (grep); παραθήσομαι, 'deposit with'. The plain verb, heard as entrusting at death (the blind reader); loses the deposit. *encomendar* (MS1932, DRB) is 'to order goods' in Brazil; *confiar* heard as 'trust'. Both are options. Serves the Compline responsory and Lk 23:46 (Clementine: *Pater, in manus tuas commendo spiritum meum*, fetched). The stylist asked the object first for the proparoxytone *espírito* at the mediant — refused (the responsory's order). |
| enutríre | nutrir (*me guiareis, e me nutrireis*) | open | Ps 30:4; 54:23 *et ipse te enútriet* (grep). διαθρέψεις, the root of 22:2 *educávit me* (ἐξέθρεψεν) → *me nutriu*: one root, one verb. MS1932 *sustentar* the option. Ps 54:23 applied: *e ele te nutrirá*; the blind reader heard 'sustain', with feeding behind it. |
| conclúdere (a person) | encerrar (*E não me encerrastes nas mãos do inimigo*) | open | Ps 30:9 (συνέκλεισας); 77:50, 77:62 *conclúsit* (in morte, in gládio) will test it; 16:9b *conclusérunt* (their fat) → *fecharam*, another use; 34:3 *conclúde*. MS1932 *entregaste* the option. Ps 34:3 *conclúde advérsus eos* has no object: *fechai a passagem aos que me perseguem* (the stylist's regency; DRB supplies 'the way', MS1932 'corta a passagem'); *encerrar* is option 3 there. |
| sortes (*in mánibus tuis sortes meæ*) | as minhas sortes | open | Ps 30:15, only place; the Latin reads 'lots' where the Greek (and the Hebrew) has 'times' (οἱ καιροί μου) — the Latin followed. The blind reader listed *sortes* unknown and heard 'my luck / fate'; singular *a minha sorte* and MS1932 *o meu destino* are options. |
| excéssus mentis / in excéssu meo | fora de mim (*Eu, porém, disse, fora de mim*) | open | Ps 30:23 (ἐν τῇ ἐκστάσει μου); 115:2 *Ego dixi in excéssu meo* and 67:28a *in mentis excéssu* (grep) can follow. The going-out said plainly; *excesso* is 'too much', *êxtase* mystical joy. The blind reader heard 'in despair, not in control' first, 'ecstasy' second; the Latinist passed it twice. Options *no arrebatamento da minha mente*, DRB's *no excesso da minha mente*. **Ps 67:28a *in mentis excéssu* (Benjamin) → *em êxtase*** — *fora de si* (draft 1) was heard as fury or madness by all three v1 readers. *fora de mim* stays for 30:23's panic. |
| vituperátio | censura (*ouvi a censura de muitos*) | open | Ps 30:13, only place (ψόγος); heard as 'criticism' (the blind reader: censorship second). Options *difamação*, *vitupério* |
| consiliári | planejar (*planejaram tomar a minha alma*) | open | Ps 30:14, only place (ἐβουλεύσαντο). Draft 1 *deliberaram* was unknown to the blind reader and 'minutes of a meeting' to the stylist. Stays apart from consílium → conselho / desígnio only by root. |

The blind reader heard the soul praising, noticing the build said otherwise. **Ps 63:11 *et laudabúntur omnes recti corde* → *e serão louvados todos os retos de coração*** — the passive held (MS1932 agrees here); heard as 'praised by others'; passed the Latinist twice. **Ps 62:12 *laudabúntur omnes qui jurant in eo* → *serão louvados todos os que juram por ele*** — the passive kept (MS1932 *louvados serão*); the blind reader heard 'praised by others'. |
| perséqui (a good thing) | perseguir (*procura a paz, e persegue-a*) | open | Ps 33:15 (δίωξον); everywhere else of enemies (7:2, 17:38 … grep), which makes the image strong. The stylist's *vai atrás dela* (cadence) is the option. |
| tégere | cobrir (*e cujos pecados foram cobertos*) | open | Ps 31:1 (ἐπεκαλύφθησαν; Rom 4:7). The noun tegmen (35:8) → *abrigo* (new row), not *cobertura*. |

Costs the echo of Rom 4:8 as Portuguese Bibles may render it (unverified); *imputou* is option 1. **Wants a ruling.** |

Draft 2's supplied *Senhor,* was undone at the Latinist's gate (a vocative the Latin lacks; rule 2 allows a pronoun, and MS1932's *(ó Senhor)* is a parenthetical gloss, D28). **Wants a ruling** if the mishearing is to be mended. |

| reveréri / reveréntia | ser desonrado / desonra (*Sejam envergonhados e desonrados*; *Vistam-se de vergonha e de desonra*) | open — **for a ruling** | Ps 34:4, 34:26, 34:26b (ἐντρέπεσθαι / ἐντροπή: to be abashed); always beside confúndi or erubéscere, so it needs a third word of shame (confúndi → *ser envergonhado*, D15; erubéscere → *corar*). Draft 1 *vexados / vexame*: unknown to the blind reader twice. *confundidos* (MS1932) is the word D15 took from confúndi. The Latinist, gate (three minors), wanted *envergonhados / confusão* — refused: they swap confúndi's words onto reveréri. Cost: *desonra* is disgrace, where the Latin is abashment. Other places (grep): 39:15 ×2, 69:3 (both near-twins of 34:4), 68:20 *reveréntiam meam*. **Proposal: *desonrar / desonra*; options *vexar*, *confundir*.** **Ps 39:15, 39:15b (×2) → *desonrados*, copying 34:4.** The Latinist (minor, twice) asked *confundidos* / *se envergonhem*: revereor is inner abashment, *desonrado* outer dishonour. Refused for the row and D15, but his point is evidence for the ruling: the row's word leans outward. **Ps 69:3 = 34:4 → *Sejam envergonhados e desonrados*** — the Latinist asked *confundidos* on both runs (minor); refused for this row (D15 took *confundidos* from confúndi). **Ps 70:13 *pudóre* (ἐντροπή) → *desonra*** — merged by D15's Greek test (*cobertos de vergonha e de desonra* = 34:26b); 70:24 *revériti* → *desonrados*. *pudor* (today modesty) option. |
