Draft 1 *Bem feito* (said of another's misfortune): the stylist and the blind reader heard 'deserved punishment' at 34:25. 34:25 *ánimæ nostræ* → *para a nossa alma*. **39:16 and 69:4 *qui dicunt mihi: Euge, euge* (grep) should copy.** **Ps 39:16 copied: *os que me dizem: Que bom, que bom*.** The stylist ('sounds sincere and cheerful, not jeering') and the blind reader ('the mockery may be missed') both failed it; the stylist asked *Bem feito* again. Held for the row; the recurring complaint is noted for the ruling (69:4 still to copy). **Ps 69:4b copied *os que me dizem: Que bom, que bom* (rule 6, = 39:16) — and all three v1 readers failed it again** (Latinist minor twice, 'weakens the taunt'; stylist 'warm and approving'; ambiguity reader 'the mockery is not audible'): five readers of five across Pss 39 and 69. **Proposal for a ruling: *Bem feito, bem feito* in all four places (34:21, 34:25, 39:16, 69:4b)** — said by mockers *to me* (*qui dicunt mihi*), 'serves you right' is exactly the gloat; the 34:25 mishearing ('deserved punishment') is the same sense seen from outside. The v2 Latinist offered *Muito bem, muito bem*; option in Ps 69's decision `euge`. |
| annúere óculis | fazer sinais com os olhos | open | Ps 34:19 (διανεύοντες ὀφθαλμοῖς); *piscar* heard as blinking, *acenar* (MS1932) as waving the hand. |
| retrórsum (*Avertántur / Convertántur retrórsum*) | voltar para trás (*Voltem para trás*) | open | Ps 34:4b (ἀποστραφήτωσαν εἰς τὰ ὀπίσω); *desviados para trás* is not Portuguese, so neither avértere's *desviar* nor convértere's verb is used alone: the phrase is one Portuguese phrase under either Latin verb (D15's test, one Greek). 39:15, 69:4 (= this colon's start), 128:5 should copy; 9:4, 55:9 *converténtur retrórsum* likely too (grep). **Ps 55:9b *tunc converténtur inimíci mei retrórsum* → *então os meus inimigos voltarão para trás*** — as this row foresaw; heard as retreat. Ps 49:17 *projecísti sermónes meos retrórsum* → *lançaste para trás as minhas palavras*. **Ps 69:4 *Avertántur retrórsum* → *Voltem para trás*** (as 34:4b, 39:15b). **69:4b *Avertántur* without retrórsum → *Voltem-se*** (the verb kept; bare *voltem* is 'come back', D44): retreat heard as likely by the ambiguity reader, conversion possible. The stylist's *Voltem-se para trás* for both refused (turning round, not retreat). |
| æmulári | ter inveja de (*Não tenhas inveja dos malvados*) | open | Ps 36:1, 36:7, 36:8 (παραζηλόω ×3; one phrase three times; 36:8 without an object: *não tenhas inveja, a ponto de fazeres o mal*). L&S's bad sense 'be envious of'. The Hebrew's 'fret not' is not the Latin's. Only here (77:58 has the noun *æmulatiónem*, grep). The Tuesday Matins antiphon *Noli æmulári * in eo qui prosperátur et facit iniquitátem* follows. Passed the Latinist twice; no reader remarked. Options *invejar* (MS1932), *rivalizar com*. |
| zeláre | invejar (*nem invejes os que fazem a iniquidade*) | open | Ps 36:1, beside *æmulári* (the Greek's simple ζήλου beside its compound παραζήλου: one root, so the verb of *inveja*). 72:3 *Quia zelávi super iníquos* should follow (*invejei*). *ter ciúme de* (the cognate) is a lover's jealousy in Brazil; *ter zelo por* the opposite sense. zelus (noun) keeps *zelo* (118:139). **Ps 72:3 *zelávi super iníquos* → *invejei os iníquos*** — applied as the row asked; *super* absorbed by the transitive verb. Passed the Latinist twice, no reader remarked. |
| malignári (the verb) | fazer o mal (*a ponto de fazeres o mal … os que fazem o mal*) | open | Ps 36:8–9 (πονηρεύομαι); the participle as a noun stays *malvados* (D24, 36:1). 104:15 *nolíte malignári* (grep) should take *não façais o mal*. **Recount (grep_latin.py accent fix):** the verb also stands at 73:3 *quanta malignátus est inimícus in sancto* and 82:4 *malignavérunt consílium* (transitive), besides 104:15 — each will decide its own build. |
| fácere iniquitátem / injustítias | fazer a iniquidade / fazer injustiças | open | Ps 36:1, 36:7 (ποιέω); *praticar* stays operári's (5:7a, 6:9). The stylist asked *praticam* at 36:1 — refused for that reason. |
| ólera herbárum | as ervas verdes | open | Ps 36:2 only (λάχανα χλόης; DRB *the green herbs*). |
| páscere (passive) | ser apascentado (*e serás apascentado nas suas riquezas*) | open | Ps 36:3 (ποιμανθήσῃ). **Unknown to the blind reader**; kept for the shepherd's image. *ser alimentado* (DRB *fed*) the option. Other places not yet counted (grep before use). |
| reveláre (a way, to someone) | revelar (*Revela ao Senhor o teu caminho*) | open | Ps 36:5 (ἀποκάλυψον; not the Hebrew's 'commit'); the monastic antiphon *Revéla * Dómino viam meam* and the Alleluia of 01-31 (grep). The glossary's other reveláre builds (118:18 *desvendar* the eyes, 28:9 *desnudar* the woods) stand; options *Descobre*, *Expõe* (MS1932). Passed the Latinist twice. |
| súbditus esse | ser sujeito a (*sê sujeito ao Senhor*) | open | Ps 36:6 (ὑποτάγηθι); the family of *sujeitar* (subdere / subjícere, 17:48). The stylist asked *sê submisso* — option; the blind reader did not stumble. Ps 59:10 *mihi alienígenæ súbditi sunt* → *a mim estão sujeitos os estrangeiros* (the state; *foram sujeitos* option). 107:10 differs (*amíci facti sunt*). |
| observáre (a person, with hostile intent) | espreitar (*O pecador espreitará o justo*) | open | Ps 36:12 (παρατηρήσεται), kept apart from 36:32 *consíderat* → *observa*. **Unknown to the blind reader**; passed the Latinist twice. *observar* (serváre, 30:7) the option. **Ps 55:7 *ipsi calcáneum meum observábunt* → *eles espreitarão o meu calcanhar*** — the row applied; unknown to the blind reader again (the second psalm); passed the Latinist twice. |
| deícere | abater (*Para abater o pobre e o indigente*) | open | Ps 36:14b (καταβαλεῖν); *derrubar* is supplantáre's (17:40, 36:31). |
| trucidáre | massacrar (*para massacrar os retos de coração*) | open | Ps 36:14b only (σφάξαι); draft 1 *trucidar* was unknown to the blind reader. |
| mutuári / commodáre | tomar emprestado / emprestar | open | Ps 36:21 *Mutuábitur peccátor* → *O pecador tomará emprestado*; 36:26 *miserétur et cómmodat* → *tem piedade e empresta* (one Greek verb in two voices, δανείζομαι / δανείζω; the Latin two verbs). 111:5 *qui miserétur et cómmodat* should copy 36:26. |
| disperíre / interíre | perecer de todo / extinguir-se | open | Ps 36:22, 36:38 (ἐξολεθρεύω behind both); disperíre also 82:11, interíre 48:18 (of a man: *morrer* there). The Latin's five verbs of ending in Ps 36 kept apart (see *dispérdere*). |
| collídere (passive) | ser esmagado (*Quando cair, não será esmagado*) | open | Ps 36:24 only (καταραχθήσεται); *quebrar* is confríngere's. |
| mortificáre | dar a morte (*e busca dar-lhe a morte*) | open | Ps 36:32 (θανατῶσαι); 43:22 *mortificámur tota die*, 78:11, 108:17 (grep). The cognate *mortificar* is ascetic self-denial to a Catholic ear. **Ps 43:22b (= Rom 8:36) *somos entregues à morte o dia todo*** — the passive (MS1932); *somos mortos* (DRB) is heard 'dead'; option. |
| superexaltáre | muito exaltar (*Vi o ímpio muito exaltado*) | open | Ps 36:35 only; draft 3 *sumamente exaltado* (MS1932) was bookish to the stylist and unknown to the blind reader. |
| pacíficus (of a man) | de paz (*para o homem de paz*) | open | Ps 36:37 (εἰρηνικῷ); the cognate *pacífico* is proparoxytone at the final (the stylist). 119:7 *eram pacíficus* (grep) will decide its own build. |
| malítia | malícia (*mas não odiou a malícia*) | open | Ps 35:5 (κακία); apart from nequítia → maldade (πονηρία, 7:10). 7 lines (grep: 35:5, 49:19, 51:3, 51:5, 93:23, 106:34, 140:4). The blind reader heard 'intent to harm' first, slyness second, innuendo third; the Latinist passed it twice. Option *maldade*. Ps 51:3 *na malícia*, 51:5 *Amaste a malícia mais que a benignidade*: the blind reader heard slyness / innuendo first again (51:3); kept. Ps 49:19 *Os tuum abundávit malítia* → *A tua boca abundou em malícia*. |
| juménta | animais (*Salvareis os homens e os animais*) | open | Ps 35:7b (κτήνη, the Greek of 8:8 pécora → *animais*; D15's test lets them share). *jumento* is a donkey in Brazil. The Latinist (gate, minor) wanted *os animais domésticos*; refused (a word added, the colon lengthened); option *gado*. 10 lines (grep): 48:13, 48:21, 49:10, 72:23 *Ut juméntum factus sum* (singular: *animal*?), 77:48, 77:50, 103:14, 106:38, 146:9 — check each for the Greek before copying. **Ps 48:13 = 48:21 *comparátus est juméntis insipiéntibus* → *foi comparado aos animais insensatos*** — the row applied (κτήνεσιν). Passed the Latinist twice. **Ps 49:10 *juménta in móntibus* → *os animais nos montes*** — the row applied. The Latinist asked *o gado* (v1; it would say cattle twice beside *os bois*) and *jumentos* (gate; a donkey in Brazil) — refused. **Ps 72:23 singular *Ut juméntum* (κτηνώδης) → *como um jumento*** — a local departure from *animal* in v2, asked by the Latinist and the stylist: one beast of burden, and in Brazil the byword for a dull mind, which is the sense after 72:21 *e não soube*. The plural herds keep *animais*. Gate v2 passed it. |
| quemádmodum | como / assim como | open — **for a ruling** | Ps 35:7b *quemádmodum multiplicásti misericórdiam tuam, Deus* → *como multiplicastes a vossa misericórdia, ó Deus!* — draft 1 *assim como* (the comparison) was the Latinist's major: he reads an exclamation, with the Greek ὡς, DRB 'O how' and MS1932 'Quanto'. Elsewhere quemádmodum is comparative: 32:22 *quemádmodum sperávimus in te* → *assim como esperamos em vós* (Ps 32), 28:6 → *como*, 41:2 *Quemádmodum desíderat cervus … ita* (grep: 7 lines). **Proposal: *assim como* where a *sic / ita* answers or a comparison is plain; *como … !* where it opens an exclamation with nothing compared (35:7b).** |
| tegmen | abrigo (*ao abrigo das vossas asas*) | open | Ps 35:8 (σκέπη, shelter); from tégere → cobrir, but *sob a cobertura* is heard as a roof, an insurance, an icing; *sombra* is umbra's (16:8b). **Ps 60:5 *in velaménto alárum tuárum* → *ao abrigo das vossas asas*** — *velaméntum* has the same Greek (σκέπη), so D15 joins it to this row. The Latinist asked twice (minor) for the concrete *sob o véu* / *sob a cobertura*; held, both options. 62:8b *in velaménto alárum tuárum* should copy. **Ps 62:8b *in velaménto alárum tuárum* → *E ao abrigo das vossas asas exultarei*** — copies 60:5 as foreseen; no reader remarked. |
| ubértas | fartura (*com a fartura da vossa casa*) | open | Ps 35:9 (πιότης, fatness); *abundância* is abundántia's; *fertilidade* of land. 64:12 *campi tui replebúntur ubertáte* is the other place (grep) — fields: *fartura* holds there too. Ps 64:12 *campi tui replebúntur ubertáte* → *os vossos campos serão cheios de fartura* — applied, as the row foresaw; no remark. |
| prætendere | estender (*Estendei a vossa misericórdia*) | open | Ps 35:11 only (grep; παράτεινον, extend, prolong); safe at the vós imperative (past *estendi*). Option *Prolongai*. |
| torrens | torrente | working | Ps 35:9 *torrénte voluptátis tuæ*; listed unknown by the blind reader, kept (a stream is the image of drink). 7 lines (grep): 17:5, 73:15, 77:20, 82:10, 109:7, 123:5. |
| illúsio | escárnio (*os meus lombos estão cheios de escárnios*) | settled (D39) | Ps 37:8; 78:4 *subsannátio et illúsio* the other place (grep). ἐμπαιγμός, mockery. Draft 1 had the cognate *ilusões* (DRB, MS1932, and L&S's 'eccl.' sense citing this verse): **the Latinist marked it MAJOR**, the stylist and the blind reader heard fantasies. *escárnio* is of the family the derídere row gives *escarnecer* (21:8); derísum (43:14 *subsannatiónem et derísum*) is the near twin of 78:4 — they never meet in a verse, but the coordinator may want to keep them apart (*zombaria* is subsannáre's). Option *ilusões* kept in Ps 37. **Ps 43:14 (D39's open question ruled): *derísum* kept apart → *riso*** (*zombaria e riso*): 78:4 is the near-twin that varies exactly this word, and the Greek varies it too (καταγέλως vs χλευασμός); *riso* is the root of de-rídeo and κατα-γέλως. No reader remarked. See the derísum row. |
| lumbi | lombos | settled (D39) | Ps 37:8 (ψύαι), only place in the psalms (grep). The organ named (rule 5), apart from renes → *rins*. The blind reader listed *lombos* unknown and heard the lower back; the Latinist and stylist passed it. Option *entranhas* (MS1932). |
| sánitas | saúde (*não há saúde na minha carne*) | working | Ps 37:4 = 37:8b, the only places (grep). ἴασις. Heard as bodily health. MS1932 *parte sã* is the option. |
| cicatrix | cicatriz | settled (D39) | Ps 37:6, only place. The Latin's word (L&S 'a scar'); DRB *sores*, MS1932 *chagas* make open wounds. The blind reader found rotting scars puzzling; the Latinist passed it twice. Option *chagas*. |
| supergrédi | ultrapassar (*as minhas iniquidades ultrapassaram a minha cabeça*) | working | Ps 37:5, only place. The blind reader heard 'more than I can bear'. *se elevaram acima da* (MS1932, the Greek) the option. |
| redargútio | repreensão (*e que não tem repreensões na sua boca*) | settled (D39) | Ps 37:15, only place (grep); ἐλεγμοί — the noun of ἐλέγχω, which is árguere → *repreender* in 37:2, so the echo is kept. The stylist asked *réplicas* (plainer; refused, option); the blind reader heard 'does not scold others'. |
| detráhere + dative | difamar (*me difamavam*) | settled (D39) | Ps 37:21; 70:13 *detrahéntes ánimæ meæ*, 100:5, 108:4, 108:20, 108:29 (grep, 6 lines). ἐνδιέβαλλον, slandered. *caluniar* is calumniári's. Passed all readers. Options *falar mal de*, *murmurar de* (MS1932). Ps 70:13 *detrahéntes ánimæ meæ* → *os que difamam a minha alma* — applied. |
| magna loqui (*super me*) | falar grandezas (contra mim) | settled (D39) | 11:4 *magníloquam* → *que fala grandezas*, 34:26b, 37:17, 54:13 (grep). **Ps 37:17: the stylist asked *grandes coisas* ('grandezas suggests quantities or splendours') and the blind reader listed *grandezas* (as boasts) unknown** — kept for 11:4 and 34:26b; if it changes, the three change together. |
| substántia (a person's being: *substántia mea*) | o meu ser (*e o meu ser é como nada diante de vós*; *E o meu ser está junto de vós*) | settled (D39) | Ps 38:6, 38:8 (ὑπόστασις both). *a minha substância* (DRB) is chemistry or philosophy in Portuguese; MS1932 *o meu ser* in 38:6 (*todos os meus bens* in 38:8 is property). Passed the Latinist twice; heard 'my being / existence'. **88:48 *quæ mea substántia* and 138:15 can follow; 108:11 (property → *bens*) and 68:3 *non est substántia* (footing) decide locally** (six lines, grep). |
| consístere (a person, *advérsum*) | erguer-se (*quando o pecador se erguia contra mim*) | settled (D39) | Ps 38:2b (συστῆναι … ἐναντίον). Draft 1 *se postava* was unknown to the blind reader and stiff to the stylist; *erguer-se* is also one of astáre's words (D22) — they never meet in a verse. 26:3 (a camp) has *se instalar*. |
| obmutéscere | emudecer (*Emudeci*) | settled (D39) | Ps 38:3, 38:10 — the only two places (grep); one word both times. |
| in imágine (38:7 *in imágine pertránsit homo*) | *como uma imagem* | settled (D39) | ἐν εἰκόνι; DRB 'as an image', MS1932 *como uma sombra*. **The Latinist asked twice (minor) for *em imagem*, which keeps a reading 'in the image (of God)'** — held: not a Portuguese sentence. imágo also 72:20. |
| refrigeráre (passive) | ser reanimado (*para que eu seja reanimado antes de partir*) | settled (D39) | Ps 38:14 (ἀναψύξω); draft 1 *tome alento* (MS1932): *alento* unknown to the blind reader, and the Latinist wanted the passive. Only verb place (grep); the noun refrigérium 65:12 and the refection row (22:2 *refrigério*). |
| esse absolute (*et ámplius non ero*) | existir (*e já não existirei*) | settled (D39) | Ps 38:14; the bare *e não serei mais* hangs in Portuguese (and rhymed with 38:13b *pais*); the stylist's *e já não serei* is the option. **Ps 58:13b *et non erunt* → *e não serão mais*** (the stylist's; DRB *be no more*): *e não existirão* rhymed with the mediant *consumação*, and draft 1's *e deixarão de existir* was paraphrase for the Latinist. The Latinist asked *existirão* on both runs (minor) — option, with the rhyme as the reason. |
| ops, *opem ferre* | socorro (*O Senhor lhe traga socorro*) | settled (D41) | Ps 40:4 only in this build; *auxílio* is adjutórium's. *lhe leve* (draft 1) was heard as the adjective 'light' by the stylist. |
| supplantátio | rasteira (*fez grande contra mim a rasteira*) | settled (D41) | Ps 40:10 (L&S: 'hypocritical deceit', citing this verse; the Greek πτερνισμός is from 'heel'; Jn 13:18 Clementine *levabit contra me calcaneum suum*, fetched). *Rasteira* is the heel-trip and, in Brazil, the figure of a betrayal: image and sense in one word; all three readers heard betrayal. The stylist calls it colloquial; *traição* (MS1932) is the option. |
| susurráre | sussurrar (*Contra mim sussurravam todos os meus inimigos*) | settled (D41) | Ps 40:8 (grep: the only place); *murmurar* is murmuráre's; *cochichar* (MS1932) the option. No reader remarked. |
| fæx (*lutum fæcis*) | borra (*e da lama da borra*) | settled (D41) | Ps 39:3 only (ἰλύος; L&S 'dregs, sediment'). Draft 1 *da lama do lodo*: the Latinist and the stylist heard a tautology, the blind reader a doubled image — *borra* keeps the second noun and its image of foul sediment. *do lodo profundo* (MS1932) option. **Ps 74:9 *fæx ejus non est exinaníta* → *a sua borra não foi esgotada*** — D41's word for dregs of the cup; *exinaníre* → *esgotar* (passive taken from the Latinist; his *esvaziada* option). The ambiguity reader listed *borra* unknown. |
| carmen | canto (*um canto ao nosso Deus*) | settled (D41) | Ps 39:4 only (ὕμνον). Kept one step from *cântico* (cánticum, same verse); *hino* is hymnus's (118:171), though the graduals that adapt this verse (Pent15-0, Quad4-2) read *hymnum*. Passed all readers. |
| insánia (*insániæ falsæ*) | loucura (*as loucuras enganosas*) | settled (D41) | Ps 39:5 only (μανίας ψευδεῖς). Draft 1 *loucuras falsas* failed the stylist ('a madness is not false') and the blind reader (the lie inaudible); *enganosas* (MS1932) taken. The Latinist at the gate asked *falsas* back (minor) — held, option 2. dolus / dolósus keep *engano / enganador*. |
| oblátio | oferenda (*Não quisestes sacrifício nem oferenda*) | settled (D41) | Ps 39:7, 50:21 (grep). *oblação* option (the liturgical cognate). Heb 10:5 Clementine has *Hostiam et oblationem* — another Latin. Ps 50:21 applied: *as oferendas e os holocaustos*. |
| *pro peccáto* (the sin offering, elliptic) | *oferta pelo pecado* (*nem oferta pelo pecado*) | settled (D41) | Ps 39:7b (περὶ ἁμαρτίας). The Latinist asked the ellipsis *nem pelo pecado* twice (minor): refused, heard 'nor because of sin'. DRB 'sin offering'. |
| *in cápite libri* | na cabeça do livro | settled (D41) | Ps 39:8 = Heb 10:7 (Clementine, fetched); the gradual verse of Epi1-0. The image kept as *caput ánguli*; the blind reader heard 'at the beginning of the book'. *No princípio do livro* option. |
| annuntiáre | anunciar | settled (D41) | Ps 9:12, 9:15, 18:2, 21:32, 29:10b, 37:19, 39:6b, 39:10 — uniform; no row till now. |
| mendícus | mendigo (*Eu, porém, sou mendigo e pobre*) | settled (D41) | Ps 39:18, 108:17 (grep); kept apart from pauper *pobre*, egénus *necessitado*, inops *carente* (D38). 69:6 has *egénus* where 39:18 has mendícus. |
| *sollícitus esse* (+ gen.) | ter cuidado de (*o Senhor tem cuidado de mim*) | settled (D41) | Ps 39:18 only (φροντιεῖ). The cognate *solícito* is 'obliging' in Brazil. Passed all readers; the stylist's best line. |
| conféstim | logo (*Carreguem logo a sua vergonha*) | settled (D41) | Ps 39:16 only; *depressa* is cito's. 69:4 has *statim* in the twin line. **Ps 69:4b *statim* (παραυτίκα; only place) → *logo* too** — draft 1 *imediatamente* was 'long, bureaucratic' to the stylist; *logo* (MS1932) taken. conféstim and statim share *logo*: two Greek adverbs of one sense, no distinction for Portuguese to carry. |
| *ferre confusiónem* | carregar a vergonha (*Carreguem logo a sua vergonha*) | settled (D41) | Ps 39:16 (κομισάσθωσαν). *Levem* heard as 'take away'; *Suportem* (DRB) and *Sofram* (MS1932) options. Passed all readers. |
| tardáre (*ne tardáveris*) | tardar (*meu Deus, não tardeis*) | settled (D41) | Ps 39:18b; 69:6b has morári (*ne moréris*) in the twin line → *demorar*, to stay apart. **Ps 69:6b *Dómine, ne moréris* → *Senhor, não demoreis*** (D41's split, morári → *demorar*, intransitive). **Note for review:** the Greek of both lines is one verb, μὴ χρονίσῃς (Rahlfs, `consult/parallels/ps039.md`, `ps069.md`), so by D15's test the split carries no sense and *não tardeis* could serve both; option 3 of Ps 69's decision `moreris`. No reader remarked. |
| auferre | tirar (*para tirá-la*) | settled (D41) | Ps 9:26b, 118:22, 118:43, 39:15 (*ut áuferant eam*, the life). No row till now. **Ps 45:9 *áuferens bella* → *ele tira as guerras*** — the participle made finite: the gerund *tirando* was heard by the blind reader as 'except for the wars'. The Latinist (v2 gate, minor) wanted the gerund back; held. *afastar* (stylist) refused as amovére's. Ps 57:9 *auferéntur* → *serão tirados*. The stylist asked *levados*; refused (*levar* is addúcere's, D42). Ps 75:13 *qui aufert spíritum príncipum* → *ao que tira o espírito dos príncipes* — spíritus kept (the stylist's *alento* refused, an option). |
| *super númerum* | além de todo número | settled (D41) | Ps 39:6b (ὑπὲρ ἀριθμόν). The Latinist asked *além do número* twice (minor): not Portuguese; *todo* is the idiom's. *sem número* option. |
| discérnere (*discérne causam meam*) | separar (*separai a minha causa*) | settled (D42) | Ps 42:1 (MS1932). *distingui* (DRB) and *discerni* are bare -ir *vós* imperatives = 'I distinguished / discerned' (rule 3). **Ps 49:4 *discérnere pópulum suum* → *para separar o seu povo*** (D42 applied; διακρῖναι). The blind reader heard 'set apart'. The Latinist (gate, minor) asked *discernir*: option (heard as 'perceive'); *julgar* (DRB, MS1932) is judicáre's. |
| repéllere (without *a*) | repelir (*por que me repelistes?*) | settled (D42) | Ps 42:2 (ἀπωθέω; MS1932). Beside *amovére / repéllere a* → afastar and reprobáre → rejeitar. Same verb 43:10, 59:3, 59:12, 73:1, 77:60, 77:67, 87:15, 88:39, 107:12 (grep). The blind reader did not know *repelistes* — watch it. **Ps 59:3, 59:12 *repulísti nos* → *vós nos repelistes … que nos repelistes*** — the row applied twice in one psalm. **The blind reader of Ps 59 did not know *repelistes* either — the second psalm to report it (after 42).** *rejeitastes* (reprobáre's), *desamparastes* (MS1932) options. Ps 61:5 → *repelir o meu preço*; unknown to the blind reader again. **Ps 73:1 *Ut quid, Deus, repulísti in finem* → *Por que, Deus, nos repelistes para sempre*** — *nos* supplied (DRB, MS1932); the latinist twice named it a minor and was held; bare *repelistes* is the option. |
| addúcere | levar (*me levaram ao vosso monte santo*) | settled (D42) | Ps 42:3, once in the psalter (MS1932); kept apart from dedúcere → guiar in the same colon. Perfect kept (the Hebrew's future refused). Not once: Ps 44:15b *Adducéntur Regi vírgines* → *Serão levadas ao Rei as virgens*, 44:16 *adducéntur in templum Regis* → *serão levadas ao templo do Rei* (grep), kept apart from *afférre* → trazer in the same verses. **Ps 71:10 *dona addúcent* → *levarão presentes*** (D42) — the Latinist asked *trarão* twice (minor, v1 and gate: movement toward the king); held for D42 and *afférre → trazer*, option. |
| introíre ad | entrar até (*entrarei até o altar de Deus*) | settled (D42) | Ps 42:4. *entrar a* is not Brazilian for going into a place; the Latinist (v2, minor) and the stylist asked *entrarei ao*, held (option). DO's Portuguese Ordo *irei até ao* (pt-PT) also uses *até*. |
| quia, before *quare* | pois (*Pois vós sois, Deus, a minha força*) | settled (D42) | Ps 42:2. *porque* is the default (30:4, 90:9 *Porque vós sois*); where *por que* questions follow, *porque* is heard as one (stylist and blind reader). Local, not a new default. |
| epulári | banquetear-se (*o som de quem se banqueteia*) | settled (D42) | Ps 41:5b, the participle *epulántis* as a relative (*quem*); ἑορτάζοντος 'keeping festival', the Latin's image is the table. Unknown to the blind reader; *o som de quem está em festa* option 2. 67:4 *epuléntur*. |
| abýssus / cataráctæ | abismo / cataratas (*O abismo invoca o abismo, * à voz das vossas cataratas*) | settled (D42) | Ps 41:8; *cataratas* heard as waterfalls (the blind reader), the sense. abýssus recurs 32:7, 35:7, 76:17, 103:6, 106:26, 148:7. |
| fluctus | ondas | settled (D42) | Ps 41:8b *fluctus tui* → *as vossas ondas*; 87:8, 92:4 (*elatiónes maris*), 106:25, 29. |
| excélsa (neuter plural noun) | alturas (*Todas as vossas alturas*) | settled (D42) | Ps 41:8b; 17:34 *super excélsa* → *sobre as alturas*. The Greek μετεωρισμοί are the sea's swells (MS1932 *vagas*); the Latin says heights, kept; *vagas altas* option 2. |
| sitíre ad | ter sede de (*A minha alma teve sede do Deus forte e vivo*) | settled (D42) | Ps 41:3, the perfect kept (*teve*, not MS1932 *tem*); 62:2 *sitívit in te ánima mea*, 142:6. **Ps 62:2b *Sitívit in te ánima mea* → *A minha alma teve sede de vós*** — the row; the second colon verbless as the Latin (*e de quantos modos a minha carne, por vós!*). |
| panes (*lácrimæ panes*) | o meu pão (*As minhas lágrimas foram o meu pão*) | settled (D42) | Ps 41:4; the plural and the dative *mihi* made the Portuguese idiom (D2, D29; 40:10 *comia o meu pão*, 79:6 *pane lacrimárum*, 101:10). The Latinist twice asked *os meus pães* (option 3). |
| módicus | pequeno (*desde o pequeno monte*) | settled (D42) | Ps 41:7 *a monte módico*; 36:16 *módicum justo*, 8:6 *paulo minus*. |
| contristári / incédere | entristecer-se / andar (*por que ando entristecido*) | settled (D42) | Ps 41:10 *contristátus incédo*; 42:2 *tristis incédo* → *ando triste* (the adjective's own word). 37:7 *contristátus ingrediébar* → *andava entristecido*. |
| effúndere ánimam (*effúdi in me ánimam meam*) | derramar a alma (*derramei em mim a minha alma*) | settled (D42) | Ps 41:5; *in me* open between 'within' and 'upon' (ἐπ᾿ ἐμέ), kept *em mim*; *dentro de mim* option 2. The blind reader heard all three senses. 61:9 *effúndite coram illo corda vestra* (the heart), 141:3. Ps 61:9 → *derramai diante dele os vossos corações* (the stylist's best line). |
| ad meípsum | dentro de mim mesmo (after the verb) | settled (D42) | Ps 41:7 *Ad meípsum ánima mea conturbáta est* → *A minha alma está perturbada dentro de mim mesmo* (the stylist's order; *mesmo* for -ipsum kept); kept apart from 41:5 *in me* → *em mim*. 142:4 *in me*. |
| apud (first person: *apud me*) | comigo (*Comigo está a oração*) | open | Ps 41:9b, verbless (copula supplied); the apud row's *junto de* (21:26) is for other persons. |
| Hermóniim / Hermon | Hermon (*e do Hermon*) | settled (D42) | Ps 41:7, the transliterated Hebrew plural heard as a name; *de Hermoniim*, *dos Hermons* options (the Latinist twice asked the plural). 88:13 *Thabor et Hermon*, 132:3 *ros Hermon*. |
| vacáre (*Vacáte, et vidéte*) | parar (*Parai, e vede que eu sou Deus*) | settled (D43) | Ps 45:11 only (grep). L&S 'be free from labour, at leisure'; σχολάσατε 'cease'. MS1932 *Parai*; safe under rule 3 (*parei*). The stylist asked *Aquietai-vos* (DRB 'be still', the Hebrew's 'let go') — kept as option; the blind reader heard 'stop'. |
| transférre | transportar (*e os montes forem transportados para o coração do mar*) | settled (D43) | Ps 45:3. μετατίθεσθαι. *transferir* is an office's word, *trasladar* (MS1932) is for relics; the stylist's *levados* refused (addúcere's, D42). |
| sonáre | ressoar (*Ressoaram, e se perturbaram as águas deles*) | settled (D43) | Ps 45:4 (ἤχησαν). MS1932 *Bramaram*, DRB 'roared' — the roar is the context's, not the word's; options. |
| ímpetus (flúminis) | ímpeto (*O ímpeto do rio alegra a cidade de Deus*) | settled (D43) | Ps 45:5 (ὁρμήματα); sung alone as a versicle (C6-1, missa 02-05, Brasilia/08-30). The stylist's best line; the blind reader listed *ímpeto* as unknown and heard some violence in it. DRB 'stream', MS1932 *rio caudaloso*. |
| inclináre (of kingdoms) | inclinar-se (*e os reinos se inclinaram*) | settled (D43) | Ps 45:7 (ἔκλιναν, 'gave way'). The blind reader heard 'bowed down in submission' first — the Latin's verb allows it. *se curvaram*, *vacilaram*, MS1932 *se humilharam* options. |
| *non commovébitur* with no subject named | *não se abalará* (pronominal, no gender) | settled (D43) | Ps 45:6 *Deus in médio ejus, non commovébitur*: the city or God; a participle (*abalada / abalado*) would choose. Sung alone in the Commons of Virgins and of the BVM. 20:8 (subject named) keeps *não será abalado*. |
| jubiláre / jubilátio / júbilus | aclamar / aclamação (*aclamai a Deus com voz de exultação*; *Subiu Deus entre aclamações*) | settled (D43) | Ps 46:2, 46:6. One Latin family (13 lines: 65:1, 80:2, 88:15, 94:1–2 the Invitatory, 97:4, 97:6, 99:2a, 150:5) and here one Greek (ἀλαλάζω / ἀλαλαγμός — the same noun as 32:3 *in vociferatióne* → *com aclamação*). *jubilai* is 'retire / be expelled' in Brazil; *júbilo* is wanted by the gáudium row; *exultar* is exsultáre's. The Latinist (v1, v2, minor) asked the singular *com júbilo* in 46:6 — held for the root pair; option. The Ascension liturgy's *in jubilatióne* takes the same word. Ps 65:1 *Jubiláte Deo, omnis terra* → *Aclamai a Deus, toda a terra* — applied; no remark. |
| terríbilis | terrível (*o Senhor é excelso, terrível*) | settled (D43) | Ps 46:3 (φοβερός); DRB, MS1932. The blind reader heard awe, but said the everyday 'awful' grates; *temível* the option. 65:3, 75:8, 88:8, 95:4, 98:3, 110:9 *sanctum et terríbile nomen ejus*. Ps 65:3 *Quam terribília sunt ópera tua* → *Como são terríveis as vossas obras*, 65:5 *terríbilis in consíliis* → *terrível nos desígnios* (verbless, as the Latin; the stylist's *ele é terrível* the option); the ambiguity reader heard 'fearsome', the everyday 'horrible' intruding a little. Ps 75:8 *Tu terríbilis es* → *Vós sois terrível*; 75:13 *Terríbili … terríbili* → *Ao terrível … ao terrível* (the repetition kept). The ambiguity reader noted the colloquial 'awful' and heard it rightly in context. |
| excélsus (adjective) | excelso (*o Senhor é excelso*) | settled (D43) | Ps 46:3; kept apart from Altíssimus though the Greek is ὕψιστος. Unknown to the Ps 46 blind reader; kept. **Ps 72:8, 72:11 *in excélso* → *no alto* (both)** — one wording for the repeated phrase (the Greek differs: εἰς τὸ ὕψος, ἐν τῷ ὑψίστῳ); heard as 'loftily' at 72:8 and 'up above' at 72:11. Passed the Latinist twice. |
| spécies (καλλονή, beauty) | beleza (*a beleza de Jacó, que ele amou*) | settled (D43) | Ps 46:5; decor is *esplendor* (20:6, 25:8, 29:8). **For Ps 44:5a** *Spécie tua et pulchritúdine tua* (ὡραιότης / κάλλος): pulchritúdo → *beleza* and spécies → *formosura* there would keep the two apart and keep *beleza* with the κάλλος family. 49:1 *spécies decóris* decides locally. **Ps 44 applies it:** 44:3 *Speciósus forma* → *mais formoso de aspecto*, 44:5a *Spécie tua et pulchritúdine tua* → *Com a tua formosura e a tua beleza* (decor 44:12 *esplendor*). **Ps 49:1b *Ex Sion spécies decóris ejus* → *de Sião, a beleza do seu esplendor*** — both rows applied, two nouns for the Latin's two (ἡ εὐπρέπεια τῆς ὡραιότητος). The Latinist (gate, minor) asked *a formosura da sua beleza*: refused, it gives each Latin noun the other's word; MS1932's order *o esplendor da sua beleza* an option. |
| congregári (people gathering) | reunir-se (*Os príncipes dos povos se reuniram*) | settled (D43) | Ps 46:10 (συνήχθησαν) = 2:2 *os príncipes se reuniram* (conveníre, the same Greek). 34:15 *juntaram-se* only because conveníre stands in the same verse; 15:4b active *congregar*. **Ps 47:5 *reges terræ congregáti sunt: convenérunt in unum* → *se congregaram: reuniram-se juntos*** — the same Greek (συνήχθησαν), but conveníre → *reunir-se* follows in the verse, and *juntar-se* (34:15) would clash with *juntos*; so 15:4b's *congregar*. The ambiguity reader heard a neutral or church gathering; kept. |
| subjícere | sujeitar (*Sujeitou a nós os povos*) | settled (D43) | 8:8, 17:48 (subdere), 46:4. The enclitic *Sujeitou-nos os povos* is heard first as 'he subjected us'; the stylist found *os povos a nós* stiff — v2 puts the dative before the object. |
| sapiénter | sabiamente (*entoai salmos sabiamente*) | settled (D43) | Ps 46:8 (συνετῶς); MS1932, DRB; *com sabedoria* (sapiéntia's noun) the option. Ps 57:6 *venéfici incantántis sapiénter* → *do feiticeiro que encanta sabiamente*; the blind reader heard praise of the sorcerer — the Latin's own irony. |
| plaudere mánibus | bater palmas (*Todas as nações, batei palmas*) | settled (D43) | Ps 46:2; *aplaudi* is a bare -ir imperative (rule 3). 97:8 *Flúmina plaudent manu* decides locally. |
| derísum | riso (*zombaria e riso para os que estão ao nosso redor*) | settled (D43) | Ps 43:14 (καταγέλως); rules D39's question: kept apart from illúsio → *escárnio* (78:4, the near-twin, has illúsio, and the Greek differs too). Options *escárnio*, *irrisão*, *chacota*. Cost: *escarnecer* (derídere, 21:8) is another family in Portuguese. |
| virtútes nostræ (plural, the people's hosts) | forças (*e não saireis, Deus, com as nossas forças*) | settled (D43) | Ps 43:10; **59:12b and 107:12b are the same colon and should copy it**. Holds strength and armed forces as the Latin plural does; *exércitos* (DRB, MS1932) decides. The singular virtus stays *poder*, *Dóminus virtútum* *Senhor dos poderes*; cost: *fortitúdo → força* (17:2) is the singular of the same word. Ps 59:12b copies 43:10b (with the question mark); the blind reader heard strength first, troops second — the Latin's range. |
| ventiláre (cornu) | lançar ao ar (*com o chifre, lançaremos ao ar os nossos inimigos*) | settled (D43) | Ps 43:6 (κερατιοῦμεν); L&S 'toss in the air, winnow'. Options *sacudir*, *derrubar* (DRB; supplantáre's). The stylist moved *com o chifre* forward (v2). |
| diripere | saquear (*saqueavam para si*) | settled (D43) | Ps 43:11; 88:42 *diripuérunt eum omnes transeúntes* (grep) should follow. |
| commutátio | troca (*nas trocas deles*) | settled (D43) | Ps 43:13; 88:52 *commutatiónem Christi tui* is another sense (grep) and will want its own look. **Ps 54:20b *Non enim est illis commutátio* → *Pois para eles não há mudança*** — the local sense D43 allows (DRB 'change', MS1932 *mudança*; ἀντάλλαγμα is 'exchange'); *troca* the option. Passed the Latinist twice. |
| obloqui | insultar (*do que me afronta e do que me insulta*) | settled (D43) | Ps 43:17 (παραλαλέω; L&S cites this verse); once in the psalter. *difamar* is detráhere's (D41), *censura* vituperátio's. Two persons (article repeated, v2, the Latinist); the supplied *me* held as grammar against him (twice, minor). |
| verecúndia | desonra (*a minha desonra está diante de mim*) | settled (D43) | Ps 43:16 (ἐντροπή, the Greek of reveréntia at 34:26b → *desonra*); confúsio beside it → *vergonha* (D41). 68:8, 68:20 (grep) should follow. |
| similitúdo (παραβολή, a byword) | termo de comparação (*Fizestes de nós um termo de comparação para as nações*) | settled (D43) | Ps 43:15 (v2, the stylist; draft 1 *uma comparação*). The Latinist at the gate called it paraphrase and asked *semelhança* (minor, refused: no sentence in Portuguese). 68:12 *factus sum illis in parábolam* is another word. Ps 68:12 *factus sum illis in parábolam* (same Greek): draft 1 *uma parábola* was heard as a Gospel parable; v2 took this row's *termo de comparação*; the gate Latinist found it neutral, losing the scorn, and asked *um provérbio* as the v1 stylist had — taken in v3. 43:15 keeps its settled wording; Gustavo may want one word for both. |
| commótio cápitis | um abanar de cabeça | settled (D43) | Ps 43:15 (v2; draft 1 *meneio*, unknown to the blind reader); *abanar a cabeça* is the sideways shake, not the nod. 21:8 *movérunt caput* is movére (*moveram a cabeça*). |
| conglutináre | colar (*o nosso ventre está colado à terra*) | settled (D43) | Ps 43:25 (ἐκολλήθη, the Greek of adhærére at 21:16 *colou-se*); once in the psalter. The Sexagesima Introit quotes it with *adhǽsit*. |
| post (after avértere retrórsum) | após (*Fizestes-nos voltar para trás após os nossos inimigos*) | settled (D43) | Ps 43:11 (v2, the Latinist: draft 1 *diante dos* followed the Greek παρά). *atrás dos* stacks with *para trás*. |
| operári (opus) | operar (*a obra que operastes*) | settled (D43) | Ps 43:2b; the noun-verb echo of the Latin and Greek. *operastes* was unknown to the blind reader; option *fizestes*. *operári iniquitátem → praticar* is untouched. 73:12 *operátus est salútem*, 89:16–17 *ópera … opus mánuum* (grep) will meet it. |
| æstimári | ser tido (*fomos tidos como ovelhas de matança*) | settled (D43) | Ps 43:22b (= Rom 8:36; v2, the stylist; draft 1 *considerados*). 87:5 *æstimátus sum* (grep) should follow. |
| possidére | possuir (*não possuíram a terra*) | open | Ps 43:4; many verses (24:13, 36:9 …, grep) — check their renderings before settling. |
| látera Aquilónis; aquilo | *os lados do norte* (bare apposition to *o monte Sião*); aquilo → *norte* | settled (D43) | Ps 47:3 (τὰ πλευρὰ τοῦ βορρᾶ). *aquilão* and *setentrião* (MS1932) need a footnote; 88:12 *aquilónem et mare* and 106:3 *ab aquilóne et mari* should follow (grep). The ambiguity reader could not place the phrase (a crux in the Latin too); *nos lados do norte* is the option |
| admirári | admirar-se (*assim se admiraram*) | settled (D43) | Ps 47:6 only (grep); θαυμάζω; keeps admirábilis's root; *espantar-se* the option. The ambiguity reader heard 'astonished' first |
| apprehéndere (a fear seizing) | apoderar-se de (*o tremor se apoderou deles*) | settled (D43) | Ps 47:6; MS1932. The verb has four places and four Portuguese verbs by object: 2:12 *abraçar* (discipline), 34:2 *empunhar* (arms), 34:8 *apanhar* (a trap) |
| vehemens (of wind) | impetuoso (*com vento impetuoso*) | settled (D43) | Ps 47:7 (πνεῦμα βίαιον); MS1932. veheménter the adverb stays *muito* (D22) |
| navis / Tharsis | navio / Társis | settled (D43) | Ps 47:7 *os navios de Társis*; *naus* (MS1932) the option; 71:10 *Reges Tharsis* follows; 103:26, 106:23 *naves* (grep before use) |
| parturiens (*dolóres ut parturiéntis*) | *dores como de quem está de parto* (verbless, as the Latin) | settled (D43) | Ps 47:7; MS1932. Beside the row parturíre / párere; *parturiente* (clinical) the option |
| complécti | abraçar (*Cercai Sião, e abraçai-a*) | settled (D43) | Ps 47:13 only (grep); L&S 'clasp, embrace', then 'encircle'; kept apart from circumdáre → *cercar* in the same colon. *rodeai-a* (DRB 'encompass') the option |
| distribúere (imperative) | partilhar (*partilhai as suas casas*) | settled (D43) | Ps 47:14; **rule-3 trap: *distribuí*, *reparti* (and *dividi*) are first-person pasts**. The ambiguity reader heard hospitality; the Latin is as open. 77:54 has the noun *distributiónis* |
| progénies | geração (*a outra geração*) | settled (D43) | Ps 47:14 *in progénie áltera* → *a outra geração* (the Greek's εἰς; MS1932); 48:12 *in progénie et progénie* should be the generátio formula *de geração em geração*; 48:20 *in progénies patrum suórum* |
| in sǽcula (alone) | pelos séculos (*ele nos regerá pelos séculos*) | settled (D43) | Ps 47:15; εἰς τοὺς αἰῶνας. Kept apart from in ætérnum's *para sempre* (D23), which stands in the same verse. Seven places (grep): 47:15, 60:5, 71:17, 77:69, 80:16, 83:5 (*in sǽcula sæculórum* → *pelos séculos dos séculos*), 145:10 Ps 60:5 applied (*pelos séculos*, moved before *na vossa tenda* for the cadence). |
| secúndum … sic (correlative) | *Segundo … assim também* | settled (D43) | Ps 47:11 *Secúndum nomen tuum, Deus, sic et laus tua* — the Latinist's gate (minor) asked for *Segundo* against drafts 1–2's *Como* (MS1932): the praise measured by the name, not merely like it. *Como* the option |
| hic est (pronoun) | este é (*Porque este é Deus*) | settled (D43) | Ps 47:15 (οὗτός ἐστιν); MS1932's adverb *aqui está* refused |
| terrígenæ | nascidos da terra (*os nascidos da terra e os filhos dos homens*) | settled (D43) | Ps 48:3 only (grep); γηγενεῖς, DRB *earthborn*. The image kept; the Hebrew's 'low and high' (MS1932) not followed. The correlative *-que … et* as *tanto … como* was draft 1 — the stylist heard a prose aside, the Latinist (v2, minor) wants it back; option. |
| propósitio | *o que proponho* (the root as a clause, D16's move) | settled (D43) | Ps 48:5 *apériam … propositiónem meam* → *abrirei com o saltério o que proponho*. πρόβλημα. *proposição* (DRB) was bookish to the stylist and unknown to the blind reader; the Latinist (v2, minor) wants the noun back (option); *o meu enigma* is the Greek's sense. **77:2 *loquar propositiónes ab inítio*** will test it (a plural, after *loqui*). |
| calcáneum | calcanhar (*a iniquidade do meu calcanhar me cercará*) | settled (D43) | Ps 48:6; πτέρνη. The heel kept, the crux left open (my own slips / those at my heels); MS1932 *perseguidores* and *passos* are options. Passed the Latinist twice. Cf. D41 *supplantátio → rasteira*. |
| placátio | propiciação (*não dará a Deus a sua propiciação*) | settled (D43) | Ps 48:8 only (grep); ἐξίλασμα. **Unknown to the blind reader** — for Gustavo: the plain option is the clause *o que o aplaque* (MS1932's, the root placáre → aplacar, but *suam* lost). 129:4 *propitiátio* (ἱλασμός) would share *propiciação*. Passed the Latinist twice. |
| stultus | tolo (*o insensato e o tolo*) | settled (D43) | Ps 48:11; 91:7 (*insípiens … stultus*, grep). ἄνους; kept apart from insípiens → insensato. *estulto*, *néscio* options. |
| vocáre nómina in terris | *chamaram os seus nomes nas suas terras* | settled (D43) | Ps 48:12 — the Latinist's MAJOR on draft 1's DRB build (*chamaram as suas terras pelos seus nomes*, which makes *terras* the object and explains). Taken; the DRB and MS1932 (*deram os seus nomes às suas terras*) builds are options. |
| depáscere | pastar (*a morte os pastará*) | settled (D43) | Ps 48:15 only; L&S cites it under 'consume'; the Greek ποιμαίνει is the shepherd. *pastar* keeps the pasture root; the blind reader heard 'death their shepherd' first, the devouring second — both are the Latin's. *apascentar*, MS1932 *fará deles o seu pasto* options. |
| a glória eórum (48:15b) | *desde a glória deles* | settled (D43) | Ps 48:15b. **The two Latinist runs contradict:** v1 asked *desde* against *longe da*; v2 asks *longe da* back ('a' separative). *desde* holds 'from' and 'since' (MS1932 *passada a sua glória*); for Gustavo's ear. *deles*, not *sua*: two readers heard *sua* as God's. |
| interíre (of a man) | morrer (*quando morrer*) | settled (D43) | Ps 48:18; the Greek here is ἀποθνῄσκειν, 48:11 *moriéntes*'s verb, so D15's test gives *morrer*; *perecer* (períre) option; 36:38 *interíbunt* (of a remnant) → *se extinguirá*. |
| *non … ómnia* (Hebraism, 'nothing') | *nada* (*nada levará*) | settled (D43) | Ps 48:18 *non sumet ómnia*; οὐκ … τὰ πάντα; DRB *nothing*, MS1932 *nada*. The Latinist asked *não levará tudo* twice (minor): refused — heard in Portuguese as 'not all of it', a wrong sense. The stylist's best line. Other *non … omnis* places decide locally (e.g. 142:2 *non justificábitur … omnis vivens*). |
| in vita ipsíus | em vida (*será bendita em vida*) | settled (D43) | Ps 48:19; the stylist: *na sua vida* after *a sua alma* is heard as '(never) in his life'. The Latinist (v2, minor) wants *ipsíus* shown; *na sua vida* option. |
| tibi when God is not addressed in the psalm | *tu* (*ele te dará graças quando lhe fizeres bem*) | settled (D43) | Ps 48:19 — the one second singular of Ps 48 is the hearer of 48:17 *Ne timúeris*; *vós* would impose a sudden apostrophe to God (and be heard as the nations of 48:2). MS1932 *te*; the Latinist found it coherent twice. *vos* option. |
| address — the psalmist to the Messiah-king (Ps 44) | *tu* (*O teu trono, ó Deus*), the bride *tu* too | settled (D43) | Ps 44; D17 left it to this psalm. Rule 3 (a single human addressee); one song speaks to groom and bride with one address, as MS1932. Cost: 44:7–8 *ó Deus* in the *tu* form — the blind reader heard 44:7 as a turn to God. The whole *vós* text is option 2 (decision `address`; *Cingi-vos* at 44:4 because bare *Cingi* is also 'I girded'). Wants a ruling: 71 and 109:1–4 are near cousins |
| diffúndere | derramar (*derramou-se a graça nos teus lábios*) | settled (D43) | Ps 44:3, the only place (grep); ἐξεχύθη, *effúndere*'s Greek, so D15 lets them share *derramar*. Verb-first kept so that the antiphon *Diffúsa est grátia* is sayable alone; the stylist's *nos teus lábios se derramou a graça* is the option |
| femur | coxa (*Cinge a tua espada sobre a tua coxa*) | settled (D43) | Ps 44:4 (μηρός); the concrete image kept; MS1932 *ao teu lado* explains it; *cintura* option. v2: *Cinge a tua espada* (the stylist: *cingir* takes the sword as object) |
| gutta (the spice) | gota (*Mirra, e gota, e cássia*) | settled (D43) | Ps 44:9; στακτή (DRB *stacte*). The Latin names the drop; the stylist asked *resina*, the blind reader found *gota* puzzling (drop or gout) — held, *resina* / *estacte* options |
| vestítus / vestiménta | veste / vestes | settled (D43) | Ps 44:9 *a vestiméntis tuis* → *das tuas vestes*; 44:10b *in vestítu deauráto* → *em veste dourada* (the stylist: *vestido* is a modern dress). *vestítus* is ἱματισμός, as *vestis* at 21:19 (*veste*), so D15 lets them share. 101:27 *sicut opertórium … sicut vestiméntum* will need checking |
| várietas (of cloth) | cores variadas (*cercada de cores variadas*) | settled (D43) | Ps 44:10b, 44:14 (*varietátibus*) — πεποικιλμένη; *variedade* failed the stylist and the blind reader ('assortment', vague); the plural *cores* also answers the number of 44:14. The gate Latinist (minor) wanted *variedade(s)* back: held, option |
| ab intus | de dentro (*Toda a glória da filha do Rei é de dentro*) | settled (D43) | Ps 44:14 (ἔσωθεν); keeps *ab*, supplies only the copula; heard as inward, spiritual glory. *está no interior* (MS1932), *vem de dentro* (stylist) options |
| consórtes | companheiros (*acima dos teus companheiros*) | settled (D43) | Ps 44:8 (μέτοχοι); *consortes* is a spouse in Portuguese. *præ* → *acima de* there (v2, stylist), *mais … que* at 44:3 |
| próspere (procédere) | próspero (*avança próspero*) | settled (D43) | Ps 44:5a; the adjective for the adverb (the stylist; *com prosperidade* 'a finance report'); the gate Latinist preferred *prosperamente* (minor), option. 117:25 *prosperáre* → prosperar |
| placére + dative (*qui homínibus placent*) | agradar a (*dos que agradam aos homens*) | settled (D44) | Ps 52:6b (ἀνθρωπαρέσκων, 'men-pleasers'); 114:9 *Placébo Dómino* should take *agradarei ao Senhor*. The Latin's openness (pleasing to men / courting them) kept; MS1932's *procuram agradar* closes it (option). The blind reader heard people-pleasers first. |
| benígnitas | benignidade (*Amaste a malícia mais que a benignidade*) | settled (D44) | Ps 51:5 (ἀγαθωσύνη); 64:12, 84:13 (grep, 3 lines). Kept apart from bónitas → bondade. The blind reader listed it as unknown; *bondade* is the option. The rhyme with 51:5b *equidade* is the Latin's own (-tátem / -tátem). Ps 64:12 *corónæ anni benignitátis tuæ* → *a coroa do ano da vossa benignidade* — held under D44 against the stylist's *bondade*; unknown to the blind reader again. |
| novácula; acútus (of a blade) | navalha; afiado (*como navalha afiada*) | settled (D44) | Ps 51:4, only place of novácula. acútus is 44:6's word (*setas agudas*, the same Greek ἠκονημένος): *agudo* for a point, *afiado* for an edge — Portuguese idiom; *aguda* (MS1932) the option. |
| præcipitátio | ruína (*todas as palavras de ruína*) | settled (D44) | Ps 51:6 (καταποντισμός); DRB, MS1932. *precipitação* is haste or rainfall; the headlong image is lost. The Latinist's *de precipício* refused (a place, not an act). 54:10 *Præcípita* (the verb) decides for itself. |
| emigráre | desalojar (transitive: *te desalojará da tua tenda*) | settled (D44) | Ps 51:7 (μεταναστεύσαι σε). Draft 1 *te fará emigrar* was heard as going abroad by the stylist and the blind reader. **61:7 *non emigrábo*** (intransitive, the only other place, grep) decides for itself; *não serei desalojado* is available. Options *te fará sair* (MS1932), *te desterrará*. **Ps 61:7 *non emigrábo* → *não partirei*** (intransitive, active). Draft 1 *não serei desalojado* drew the Latinist's major (voice: a passive with an agent the Latin lacks), the stylist ('bureaucratic', evictions) and the blind reader (unknown word, eviction); the stylist's *partir* taken. Cost: 38:14 *antes de partir* (abeam) is dying. *não emigrarei* (Latinist) option; the transitive keeps *desalojar*. Passed the v2 gate. |
| radix | raiz | settled (D44) | Ps 51:7 *et radícem tuam de terra vivéntium* → *e a tua raiz, da terra dos vivos* (the Latin's ellipsis kept, with a comma); 79:10 *plantásti radíces ejus* (grep). |
| olíva fructífera | oliveira frutífera (*como oliveira frutífera na casa de Deus*) | settled (D44) | Ps 51:10 (κατάκαρπος); an Alleluia verse (missa/Sancti/10-09). fructíferus also 106:34, 148:9 (*ligna fructífera*); olíva also 127:3b *novéllæ olivárum*. |
| bellári | guerrear (*os que guerreiam contra mim*) | settled (D44) | Ps 55:3 only (grep); πολεμέω, the same Greek as 55:2 *impúgnans* → *combater*, but the Latin varies and *guerrear* keeps *bellum* (17:40 → *guerra*); *lutar*, *combater*, *pelejam* (MS1932) options. No reader remarked. |
| exsecrári | detestar (*detestavam as minhas palavras*) | settled (D44) | Ps 55:6 (the noun *exsecrátio* 58:13 — grep). No reader remarked; 58:13's noun decides for itself. **Ps 58:13b the noun *exsecrátio* → *maldição*** (ἀρά, the Greek of 9:28 *maledictióne* → *maldição*: D15's test); *execração* the option. No reader remarked. |
| promíssio | promessa | settled (D44) | Ps 55:9b only (grep; ἐπαγγελία). *Assim como também na vossa promessa* — the ellipsis kept; the blind reader found it incomplete, as the Latin is. |
| lapsus | queda (*os meus pés da queda*) | settled (D44) | Ps 55:13, 114:8 (grep; ὀλίσθημα, a slip). MS1932. Heard as falling into sin first; 114:8 should copy. |
| pro níhilo (55:8 *pro níhilo salvos fácies illos*) | por nada | settled (D44) | Ps 55:8 (ὑπὲρ τοῦ μηθενὸς); DRB 'for nothing'. The blind reader heard it split between 'in no way' and 'freely' — the Latin's openness; MS1932 *de nenhum modo* decides (option). 80:15, 89:5, 105:24 are other builds (*pro níhilo habére*) and decide locally. |
| abscóndere without an object (55:7 *Inhabitábunt et abscóndent*) | *esconderão* (bare) | settled (D44) | Ps 55:7 (κατακρύψουσιν, no object). Draft 1's reflexive *se esconderão* (DRB, MS1932) was marked by the Latinist (minor) as changing the voice; the bare verb taken, and he passed it at the gate. *inhabitáre → habitar* (36:29). |
| placére coram (55:13 *ut pláceam coram Deo*) | agradar diante de (*para que eu agrade diante de Deus*) | settled (D44) | Ps 55:13 (εὐαρεστῆσαι ἐνώπιον); keeps *coram*; MS1932 *seja agradável a Deus* drops it (option *seja agradável*). Cf. the row placére + dative (52:6b, 114:9). No reader remarked. |

The v2 gate (minor) asked back *eis que conheci que vós sois* — held (option): he called both defensible. |

MS1932 *convocou … Chamará*, DRB one verb: options. |
| maniféste | abertamente | settled (D44) | Ps 49:3 *Deus maniféste véniet* → *Deus virá abertamente* (ἐμφανῶς). Draft 1 *manifestamente* (MS1932) was unknown to the blind reader and heavy to the stylist; *às claras* option. |
| válidus | violento (of a storm) | settled (D44) | Ps 49:3b *tempéstas válida* → *uma tempestade violenta* (MS1932); *forte* is fortis's (7:12), *poderosa* the virtus family's. Check each other place before copying. |

| immoláre | imolar | settled (D44) | Ps 49:14 (*Imola a Deus um sacrifício de louvor*); keeps the sacrificial verb after the refused bulls and goats. Unknown to the blind reader; kept. MS1932 *Oferece* option. 26:6, 105:37 (grep). |
| honorificáre | honrar | settled (D44) | Ps 49:15 *et honorificábis me* → *e tu me honrarás*; 49:23 *Sacrifícium laudis honorificábit me* → *O sacrifício de louvor me honrará* (MS1932); 36:20 *honrados*; 138:17 the other place (grep). Kept apart from glorificáre (DRB *glorify*). |
| pórtio pónere | pôr a porção (*com os adúlteros punhas a tua porção*) | settled (D44) | Ps 49:18; see pórtio. |

MS1932 *urdia* option. 57:3 should follow. **Ps 57:3 *injustítias manus vestræ concínnant* → *as vossas mãos tramam injustiças*** — applied, as this row foresaw (συμπλέκουσιν). |

| principális (*spíritu principáli*) | soberano (*e firmai-me com um espírito soberano*) | settled (D44) | Ps 50:14 (ἡγεμονικῷ, 'ruling'); *principal* means only 'main'; options *de príncipe*, *magnânimo* (MS1932), *generoso* (the Hebrew, refused). The ambiguity reader noted the everyday *atribulado* = 'hectic'; kept. |

| ámplius (degree: *Ámplius lava me*) | ainda mais (*Lavai-me ainda mais da minha iniquidade*) | settled (D44) | Ps 50:4 (ἐπὶ πλεῖον; DRB 'yet more'); *mais e mais* (MS1932) option; kept apart from *ámplius non* → *já não* (38:14). |

| impónere super | pôr sobre (*então sobre o vosso altar porão bezerros*) | settled (D44) | Ps 50:21; the place first so *porão* is not heard as the noun 'hold, basement'; *colocarão* option. **Ps 65:11 *posuísti tribulatiónes … imposuísti hómines super cápita nostra* → *pusestes … pusestes homens sobre as nossas cabeças*** — the v2 Latinist asked for *impusestes* (the compound); held: *impor* is imposing a rule, and the Latin's two verbs share their root. |

| hyssópus | hissopo (*Vós me aspergireis com o hissopo*) | settled (D44) | Ps 50:9 (MS1932 *hissope*, older spelling); one blind reader listed *hissopo* and *aspergireis* as unknown — the Asperges words, kept. |
| delectári (deponent: *holocáustis non delectáberis*) | deleitar-se (*com holocaustos não vos deleitareis*) | settled (D44) | Ps 50:18 (εὐδοκήσεις), as 36:4 *Deleita-te no Senhor*; the causative delectáre at 29:2 went to *alegrar* (εὐφραίνω). Ps 64:8 the causative *delectábis* → *alegrareis*, as 29:2 (the Greek differs, τέρψεις; the Latin verb and voice are the same); *deleitareis* the option. |

Ps 58:17 *exsultábo mane misericórdiam tuam* → *de manhã exultarei na vossa misericórdia* — the ruling held against the v2 Latinist (minor; *celebrarei com júbilo a vossa misericórdia*, option). | spíritus sanctus tuus (50:13) | o vosso espírito santo (lower case) | settled (D44) | Ps 50:13; lower case as the DO Latin prints it, beside *espírito reto* (12) and *soberano* (14); the Person is heard anyway (the ambiguity reader); *Espírito Santo* option. |

| solitúdo | solidão (*e permaneci na solidão*) | settled (D44) | Ps 54:8; the place and the loneliness, as the Portuguese; the blind reader heard loneliness. *deserto* (MS1932) is desértum's. 101:7 *pellicáno solitúdinis*, 106:4 *Erravérunt in solitúdine* will test it. |
| pusillanímitas | desânimo (*do desânimo de espírito*) | settled (D44) | Ps 54:9 only (grep); ὀλιγοψυχία; the cognate *pusilanimidade* long and bookish; MS1932 *abatimento*. |

No reader remarked. Proposal: *maldizer* wherever *benedícere* stands beside it (108:28 *Maledícent illi, et tu benedíces*). |

| jáculum | dardo (*e elas mesmas são dardos*) | settled (D44) | Ps 54:22; kept apart from sagítta → seta. |

| dimidiáre | chegar à metade (*não chegarão à metade dos seus dias*) | settled (D44) | Ps 54:24b only (grep); ἡμισεύσωσιν; MS1932's words; the blind reader heard 'die young'. |
| habitáculum | morada (*Porque há maldades nas moradas deles*) | settled (D44) | Ps 54:16b; 32:14's word. |
| turris | torre (*torre de força*) | open | Ps 60:4; 47:13, 121:7 *in túrribus* (grep) should follow. |

Heard rightly by the blind reader ('who has been good to me'). *me fez o bem*, MS1932 *tanto bem me tem feito* options |
| incurváre | curvar (*e curvaram a minha alma*) | open | Ps 56:7 (κατέκαμψαν; L&S 'bend, bow; trop. cast down'); 68:24 *dorsum eórum semper incúrva* is the only other place (grep) → *curvai*. *fizeram curvar* (MS1932), *abateram* options |
| *dare in oppróbrium* | entregar à afronta (*entregou à afronta os que me calcam aos pés*) | open | Ps 56:4 (ἔδωκεν εἰς ὄνειδος); keeps *dare* as a giving-over, the oppróbrium row's *afronta*. 38:9 (*oppróbrium … dedísti me*, the person made the reproach) is another build. *cobriu de afronta* (MS1932's build) option. Heard as 'put to shame' by the blind reader; Latinist clean twice |
| *in umbra* (locative, not the object of *speráre*) | à sombra de (*E à sombra das vossas asas esperarei*) | open | Ps 56:2b; 16:8b *sub umbra* → *Sob a sombra* (another preposition), 35:8 *in tégmine* → *ao abrigo*. The stylist's best line |

| *recta judicáre* | julgar retamente (*julgai retamente, filhos dos homens*) | open | Ps 57:2 (εὐθεῖα κρίνετε). The adverb of *reto*: *com retidão* is diréctio's, *o que é reto* was heard as 'assess'. Cf. 74:3 *justítias judicábo* (grep). |
| vulva | ventre (*Desde o ventre*) | open | Ps 57:4 only (μήτρα), beside *útero → seio materno*; see the row *venter / úbera / úterus*. |

| mola (tooth) | molar (*os molares dos leões*) | open | Ps 57:7 (μύλας; L&S cites the verse: 'jawbone, or the teeth'); parallel to *dentes*. *queixadas* (MS1932) option. |
| decúrrere / flúere | correr / escorrer (*como a água que corre*; *como a cera que escorre*) | open | Ps 57:8–9, two verbs two verses apart; the Latinist (v2, minor) wanted *escorre* for *decúrrens* — held so the two stay two. *que se derrete* (MS1932, the Greek τακείς) option for 57:9. 67:3 *Sicut fluit cera a fácie ignis* should follow (grep). |
| supercádere | cair sobre (*caiu fogo sobre eles*) | open | Ps 57:9 (ἐπέπεσε). The Latinist asked v1 for 'fell upon' (against draft 1 *de cima*), then v2 for *de cima* back (against the supplied *eles*); held on the v1 remark. |
| rhamnus | espinheiro | open | Ps 57:10 only (L&S 'buckthorn'); *sarça* (MS1932) is the Burning Bush's word; *ramno* unknown. The blind reader listed *espinheiro* as unknown. |
| absorbére | engolir (*assim na ira os engole*) | open | Ps 57:10 (καταπίεται; present). *tragar* is exact but *traga* is also trazer's subjunctive; *devorar* (MS1932) is another image. 68:16 and 123:3b (grep) should test it. |
| similitúdo (ὁμοίωσις, likeness) | semelhante (*O furor deles é semelhante ao da serpente*) | open | Ps 57:5; the noun made adjective at the stylist's request (*é à semelhança* a calque). The byword sense (παραβολή, 43:15) keeps *termo de comparação*. |
| compúnctio (*vinum compunctiónis*) | compunção (*destes-nos a beber vinho de compunção*) | open | Ps 59:5 only (grep the psalter before ruling); the noun of 4:5 *compungi-vos*; the Greek κατάνυξις is stupor. | olla / lebes (Moab's pot) | panela (*Moab é a panela da minha esperança*) | open | Ps 59:9b *olla*; 107:10 has *lebes* for the same Greek (λέβης) — by D15's test it may copy *panela*, or take *caldeirão* to show the Latin's variation. | calceaméntum | calçado (*estenderei o meu calçado*) | open | Ps 59:10 = 107:10. MS1932; *sandália* option. The blind reader found the gesture obscure (the Latin's image). |
| alienígenæ | estrangeiros | open | Ps 59:10 = 107:10 (ἀλλόφυλοι); kept apart from *aliénus → estranho* (17:46 *filhos estranhos*). 82:8, 86:4 (grep) should follow. |
| partíri / divídere (land) | repartir (*e repartirei Siquém*) | open | Ps 59:8 *partíbor*; 107:8 *dívidam* is the same Greek (διαμεριῶ) and should copy; = 21:18 *repartiram*. Rule 3 applies only at the imperative (*reparti*). |

| accédere | aproximar-se de (*O homem se aproximará de um coração profundo*) | open | Ps 63:7b, the Latinist's fix (approach, not arrival), = 33:6 *Accédite ad eum → Aproximai-vos dele*; but 90:10 *Non accédet ad te malum → O mal não chegará a ti* has *chegar*. | altus (*cor altum*) | profundo (*um coração profundo*) | open | Ps 63:7b (καρδία βαθεῖα; DRB 'deep'). *Alto* would keep the echo with *exaltábitur* but a 'high heart' is a proud one in Portuguese. The ambiguity reader found the line obscure, as the Latin is. |
| *res amára* | coisa amarga (apposition, between commas) | open | Ps 63:4 *intendérunt arcum rem amáram* (πρᾶγμα πικρόν); what is bitter is left open. MS1932 *arco envenenado* refused. |
| *narráre ut* + subjunctive | narrar que (*Narraram que esconderiam laços*) | open | Ps 63:6b (διηγήσαντο τοῦ κρύψαι). The two Latinist runs contradicted each other (*como* → *que*, then *que* → *como*); held at *que* (DRB 'talked of hiding'); *como* the option. For Gustavo. |

| partes (a share) | porção (*serão a porção das raposas*) | open | Ps 62:10 (μερίδες); collective singular; *presa* (MS1932) interprets. Blind reader heard 'eaten by foxes' |
| obstrúere (*os*) | fechar (*foi fechada a boca*) | open | Ps 62:12 only (grep); *tapar a boca* is a hand over it in Brazil (the stylist asked *tapada*, refused; option) |

| irrúere in | lançar-se sobre (*lançaram-se sobre mim os fortes*) | open | Ps 58:4 (ἐπέθεντο). No reader remarked. **Ps 61:4 *Quoúsque irrúitis in hóminem?* → *Até quando vos lançais sobre um homem?*** (= 58:4); the stylist asked *vos atirais* (option); the blind reader heard 'attack'. |

| convértere in (a thing into another) | converter em (*Ele converte o mar em terra seca*) | open | Ps 65:6; apart from the convértere row (*voltar*, to turn back): here a change of nature. 104:29 *convértit aquas eórum in sánguinem*, 106:33, 113:8. |
