| parábola (a byword: *factus sum illis in parábolam*) | provérbio (*e tornei-me para eles um provérbio*) | open | Ps 68:12 (v3; stylist and gate Latinist). 48:5 *Inclinábo in parábolam* (a riddle-saying) keeps *parábola*; 43:15 *similitúdo*, same Greek, *termo de comparação* (D43). |
| segregáre | reservar (*Reservareis, Deus, para a vossa herança uma chuva voluntária*) | open | Ps 67:10 (ἀφοριεῖς). *separar* is *discérnere*'s (D42; 67:15 five verses later); *Separareis* the option. No remark. |
| cleros (κλῆροι) | partilhas (*Se dormirdes no meio das partilhas*) | open | Ps 67:14 only. *sortes* heard as luck, *lotes* as building plots, *heranças* collides with *heréditas* (67:10). Listed unknown by the ambiguity reader; passed the Latinist twice. |
| ingréssus (πορεῖαι) | passos (*Viram os vossos passos, Deus*) | open | Ps 67:25 (twice), only here. *entradas* (draft 1) heard as tickets / starters by the stylist and the ambiguity reader. Meets *gressus* (16:5 *passos*), its root — accepted. *marchas* option. |
| evangelizáre | evangelizar (*aos que evangelizam*) | open | Ps 67:12. *anunciar a boa-nova* (DRB, MS1932) +7 syllables; the cognate is the Latin's length and the verse's traditional reading. Check 39:10, 95:2 *annuntiáte* vs *bene nuntiáte*. |
| pluvia voluntária | uma chuva voluntária | open | Ps 67:10 (ἑκούσιον); strange as in Latin; *generosa* option. Ambiguity reader: 'freely given', 'sounds odd'. |
| dux | guia (*Os príncipes de Judá, os seus guias*) | open | Ps 67:28b, as 54:14 *dux meus → meu guia* (D44). |
| fit reverentia (rubric in DO's text) | *(faz-se reverência)*, in parentheses | open | Ps 67:5a before *Dóminus nomen illi*; also 110:9b, 112:2 (grep). Kept as a translated rubric; the ambiguity reader noted it would be heard if read aloud. **For the coordinator / Gustavo: keep or drop rubrics?** |
| juvéncula tympanístria | moças que tocavam tamborins | open | Ps 67:26. |
| cantátio | canto (*Em vós está sempre o meu canto*) | open | Ps 70:7, only place (grep); ὕμνησις. The plain noun of *cantar*; *cântico* is cánticum's. The copula supplied; DRB and MS1932 paraphrase ('Of thee shall I sing'). |
| litteratúra | as letras (*Porque não conheço as letras*) | open | Ps 70:16, only place; γραμματεία. Keeps the root and both senses (literacy, learning); the Hebrew's 'numbers' not followed; DRB *learning*, MS1932 *a ciência*. *cognóvi* present by D44 (the Latinist asked *conheci* at v1; option). The ambiguity reader heard 'I cannot read / I am unlearned'. |
| sénium | a idade extrema (*E até a velhice e a idade extrema*) | open | Ps 70:18 beside *senécta* → velhice. Draft 1 *a decrepitude* (L&S's 'feebleness of age') was heard as clinical by the stylist and unknown by the ambiguity reader; *os cabelos brancos* (DRB, MS1932) is the Hebrew's image. |
| magnália | as grandes coisas (*as grandes coisas que fizestes*) | open | Ps 70:19; μεγαλεῖα; DRB *great things*. Apart from magnitúdo → grandeza and mirabília → maravilhas. 105:21 *qui fecit magnália in Ægýpto* should copy. *grandes obras* option. |
| *usque in altíssima* | até as alturas | open | Ps 70:19 (ἕως ὑψίστων), neuter plural; the superlative not heard (*até o mais alto* option). Apart from excélsa → alturas (D42) only by Latin; they do not meet in a psalm. *até o Altíssimo* would be heard as God's name. |
| redúcere | trazer (*de novo me trouxestes*) | open | Ps 70:20 *íterum reduxísti me*; draft 1 *reconduzistes* was heard as bureaucratic (the stylist); *de novo* carries the return. Apart from edúcere → tirar (30:5). |
| pronuntiáre | pronunciar | open | Ps 118:13, 118:172 (applied there without a row), 70:17 *pronuntiábo mirabília tua* → *pronunciarei as vossas maravilhas*. Apart from annuntiáre → anunciar (D41), though the Greek at 70:17 is ἀπαγγελῶ, annuntiáre's verb (so D15's test would allow a merge). The Ps 70 stylist found *pronunciar maravilhas* technical and asked *proclamar* — for Gustavo; option in Ps 70. |
| *mala mihi* (quǽrere / inquírere) | *males contra mim* (*os que buscam males contra mim*) | working | 37:13 *procuravam males contra mim*; Ps 70:13 = 70:24 *qui quærunt mala mihi* → *os que buscam males contra mim*. The Ps 70 stylist found *buscar males contra* stiff and asked *os que me buscam males* (refused for 37:13's wording). 40:6 *dixérunt mala mihi* → *disseram mal de mim* and 40:8 are other builds. |
| *in auxílium meum réspice* | *olhai em meu auxílio* | open | Ps 70:12 (πρόσχες). Draft 1 built it as 21:20 (*olhai para …*); Latinist, stylist and the ambiguity reader heard 'look at my help'; the purpose idiom *em meu auxílio* (D40's phrase) taken. |
| *Sed et* | Mas também | open | Ps 70:24 (ἔτι δὲ καί); *E também* option. |
| liquefácere (*liquefácta est terra*) | derreter-se (*Derreteu-se a terra*) | open | Ps 74:4 (ἐτάκη; 21:15b *cera que se derrete* is liquéscere). Concrete, as the Greek's melting; MS1932 *dissolveu-se* option. The ambiguity reader heard it literally, 'a strange image' — the Latin's. |
| merum / mistum (*vini meri plenus misto*) | vinho puro / mistura (*um cálice de vinho puro, cheio de mistura*) | open | Ps 74:8 (οἴνου ἀκράτου πλῆρες κεράσματος; DRB 'strong wine full of mixture'). The paradox kept unexplained. |
| exinaníre | esgotar (of dregs: *a sua borra não foi esgotada*) | open | Ps 74:9 (ἐξεκενώθη). MS1932's verb; *esvaziar* (the root sense, the Latinist's) option. 136:7 *Exinaníte, exinaníte* (razing a city) decides for itself — *esvaziar* / *arrasar* there. |
| hic … hic (distributive: *Hunc humíliat, et hunc exáltat*; *ex hoc in hoc*) | este … aquele (*A este humilha, e àquele exalta*; *deste para aquele*) | open | Ps 74:8–9 (τοῦτον … τοῦτον). *a este … a este* is not the Portuguese idiom and can be heard as one man; MS1932 has *este … aquele*. The literal repetition and DRB's *um … outro* are options. No reader remarked. |
| desértus (adj., *desértis móntibus*) | deserto (*dos montes desertos*) | open | Ps 74:7 (ἐρήμων ὀρέων; DRB 'desert hills'; the Hebrew's 'desert of the mountains' not followed). The noun desértum stays *deserto* (28:7). |
| oríri | nascer (*Nascerá nos seus dias a justiça*) | open | Ps 71:7 *Oriétur in diébus ejus justítia* (ἀνατελεῖ, of a rising star or a sprouting plant); the Advent use (Adv1-1, Adv2-0, Adv3-0, Adv3-3 — grep) hears the birth. *Surgirá*, *Aparecerá* options. Other places (grep before use): 96:11 *Lux orta est justo*, 111:4 *Exórtum est* (exoríri), 84:12 *Véritas de terra orta est*. No reader remarked. |
| prócidere | prostrar-se (*Os etíopes se prostrarão diante dele*) | open | Ps 71:9 (προπεσοῦνται); 94:6 *Veníte, adorémus, et procidámus ante Deum* — the Invitatory — must be weighed with it (grep). v2 order subject first (the stylist: *etíopes* proparoxytone at the mediant). |
| vellus | lã (*Descerá como a chuva sobre a lã*) | open | Ps 71:6, only place (grep); πόκος, the fleece (Gideon, Jdg 6:37). Draft 1 *o velo*: unknown to the ambiguity reader and misheard (*véu*, *velho*); v2 *a lã* (the stylist's). **The Latinist asked *o velo* back on the gate (minor) — held; for Gustavo:** the concrete fleece vs a first hearing that goes wrong. |
| fænum | feno (36:2, *como o feno, depressa secarão*) / erva (71:16, *florescerão como a erva da terra*) | open | χόρτος both times, grass. Split by what the verse does: at 36:2 it dries (hay is right); at 71:16 it flowers — the stylist ('hay does not flower') and the ambiguity reader (heard dry hay) both refused *feno* there; *erva* (MS1932) taken in Ps 71 v2 and passed the gate. Other places: grep before use. If one word is wanted everywhere, *erva* is the candidate. |
| donum (the kings' gifts) | presentes (*os reis dos árabes e de Sabá levarão presentes*) | open | Ps 71:10b; kept apart from múnera → dádivas in the same verse. 67:19 *accepísti dona in homínibus* — check its wording (grep). |
| honorábilis | digno de honra (*e o nome deles será digno de honra diante dele*) | open | Ps 71:14b, only place (grep); τίμιον. Draft 1's participle *honrado* — the Latinist: the adjective is 'worthy of honour'; taken in v2; the copula's future supplied (DRB *shall be*). |
| adoráre de ipso | adorar por (*e sempre adorarão por ele*) | open | Ps 71:15 (προσεύξονται περὶ αὐτοῦ — 'pray for him'); the Latin's *adoráre* kept, *de* kept open as *por*; the ambiguity reader heard 'adore him' first, 'pray for / through him' next. Options *e o adorarão sempre*, *e sempre orarão por ele* (the Greek). |
| lingere terram | lamber a terra (*e os seus inimigos lamberão a terra*) | open | Ps 71:9b (λείξουσιν χοῦν — 'dust', but the Latin says *terram*); the Latin's image kept against *beijarão*; *lamberão o pó* (the Greek) the option. No reader remarked. |
| contribuláre (a thing crushed) | esmagar (*esmagastes as cabeças dos dragões nas águas*) | open | Ps 73:13 (συνέτριψας), beside 73:14 *confregísti → quebrastes*; *atribulado* belongs to 50:19's spirit and cannot crush a head. No reader remarked. |
| pollúere | macular (*na terra macularam a tenda do vosso nome*) | open | Ps 73:7; also 78:1 *polluérunt templum sanctum tuum* (grep). One family with 17:31 *impollúta → imaculado*; *profanar* is kept for profanáre (88:32, 35), *contaminar* for contamináre (54:21; the same Greek βεβηλόω, the option). |
| fabricári | formar (*vós formastes a aurora e o sol*) | open | Ps 73:16 (κατηρτίσω); v1 *fabricastes* — the stylist heard factories; *formastes* taken in v2. Kept apart from *fazer* (73:17 *fecísti*) and *moldar* (plasmáre, 73:17). Known cost: *formar* is also formáre's word. |
| áscia | enxó (*com machado e enxó a derrubaram*) | open | Ps 73:6, only place; the carpenter's hewing axe (L&S cite this verse); *secúris → machado*. The ambiguity reader did not know *enxó*; kept as a known cost; *machadinha* (DRB *hatchet*) the option. |
| cognátio | parentela (*os da parentela deles*) | open | Ps 73:8; the collective subject made plural for the plural verb. The ambiguity reader listed *parentela* unknown; kept. |
| solémnitas / dies festi | solenidade / dias de festa | open | Ps 73:4 *in médio solemnitátis tuæ* → *no meio da vossa solenidade*; 73:8 *omnes dies festos Dei* → *todos os dias de festa de Deus*. One Greek (ἑορτή), two Latin words kept two. Ps 75:11 *diem festum agent tibi* → *celebrará para vós um dia de festa* (see the *diem festum ágere* row). |
| Æthíopes / Ethan | Etiópia / Etã | open | Ps 73:14 *pópulis Æthíopum* → *aos povos da Etiópia* (v1 *dos etíopes*: the stylist heard a clipped proparoxytone close; MS1932's land); 73:15 *flúvios Ethan* → *os rios de Etã* (a proper name in the Latin and the Greek). |
| sinus (the hand in the bosom) | seio (*do meio do vosso seio*) | open | Ps 73:11 = 34:13b's word; the ambiguity reader found the image obscure; kept, as the Latin. |
| respéctus (*non est respéctus morti eórum*) | respeito a (*não há respeito à morte deles*) | open | Ps 72:4 (ἀνάνευσις, a crux). Whose regard is missing is left open, as the Latin; the ambiguity reader heard 'they do not fear death' first. The stylist asked *respeito para a*, refused; *consideração pela* and MS1932's *temor da* are options. Passed the Latinist twice. |
| firmaméntum (of the wicked: *firmaméntum in plaga eórum*) | firmeza | open | Ps 72:4 (στερέωμα; DRB 'strength'), the local place D31 left: neither *esteio* (God) nor *firmamento* (sky). The colon stays verbless — v1 supplied *há*, which the Latinist ruled a major (it settles whether *non est* carries over). Heard as 'strong even in affliction'. *plaga* → *flagelo* (D39) against the Latinist's *chaga*: the Greek repeats μάστιξ / μαστιγόω in 72:4–5. |
| tenére (*ténuit eos supérbia … Tenuísti manum déxteram meam*) | tomar (*a soberba os tomou … Tomastes a minha mão direita*) | open | Ps 72:6, 72:24: one verb (ἐκράτησεν / ἐκράτησας) for pride seizing the wicked and God holding the psalmist — the psalm's turn. The Latinist asked 'held' three times (*reteve*, *prendeu*, *Segurastes*); held, since his fixes split the pair. *segurar* for both is the option — **for Gustavo**. |
| afféctus (*transiérunt in afféctum cordis*) | aos afetos do coração | open | Ps 72:7 (διάθεσιν); the plural keeps the older sense (movements of the heart), the singular *afeto* is heard as fondness. The Latinist asked the singular twice (option); the ambiguity reader listed *afetos* unknown. *às paixões* (MS1932) option. |
| justificáre cor (*justificávi cor meum*) | tornar justo (*sem causa tornei justo o meu coração*) | open | Ps 72:13 (ἐδικαίωσα). Draft 1's cognate *justifiquei* was heard as 'I made excuses for' by the ambiguity reader; v2 *tornei justo*, passed the gate. *justificar* stays for justificatiónes (the statutes) and for God as subject. |
| nátio (*natiónem filiórum tuórum*) | nação | open | Ps 72:15 (γενεά); *geração* (DRB) is generátio's. Collides only with *gentes → nações*; they do not meet here. *reprobávi* kept indicative → *rejeitei* (the reprobáre row). Passed the Latinist twice. |
| novíssima (*in novíssimis eórum*) | o fim deles | open | Ps 72:17 (τὰ ἔσχατα); DRB, MS1932. The Latinist asked the plural *as últimas coisas deles* twice (option); *os novíssimos* is the catechism's word. Heard rightly as 'their final destiny'. |
| pónere with no object (*propter dolos posuísti eis*) | pusestes para eles | open | Ps 72:18 (ἔθου αὐτοῖς). Draft 1 supplied DRB's *o*; no reader could place it. Brazilian leaves the object of *pôr* unspoken, so v2 follows the Latin; gate passed. *vós lhes pusestes laços* and MS1932's *prosperidade enganosa* interpret. |
| surgéntes (*sómnium surgéntium*) | os que se levantam | open | Ps 72:20 (ἐξεγειρομένου, one awaking). The ambiguity reader heard 'those who rise up / rebel' as possible; *sonho* beside it points to waking. *despertam* (DRB, MS1932) option. |
| fornicári abs te | prostituir-se longe de vós | open | Ps 72:27 (πορνεύοντα ἀπὸ σοῦ), the prophets' image of idolatry kept; *fornicar* is heard coarse or as a sexual term; MS1932 *infiéis* explains. The ambiguity reader heard literal prostitution first. 105:39 *fornicáti sunt in adinventiónibus suis* decides with it. |
| effúndi (*effúsi sunt gressus mei*) | derramar-se (*quase se derramaram os meus passos*) | open | Ps 72:2 (ἐξεχύθη), the image kept (D42's verb); the ambiguity reader found it puzzling but heard 'nearly failing', the sense. *escorregaram* (DRB) option. |
| habitátio (God's dwelling) | habitação (*e a sua habitação em Sião*) | open | Ps 75:3 (τὸ κατοικητήριον; 68:26 *habitátio eórum* → *habitação* the precedent). Kept apart from habitáculum → *morada*. The Holy Saturday versicle. |
| quiéscere (*terra trémuit et quiévit*) | aquietar-se (*a terra tremeu e se aquietou*) | open | Ps 75:9 (ἡσύχασεν); the Easter Offertory. Kept apart from requiéscere → *repousar*; MS1932 *ficou em sossego*, *silenciou* options. The stylist named 75:9 the best line. |
| diem festum ágere | celebrar um dia de festa (*celebrará para vós um dia de festa*) | open | Ps 75:11 (ἑορτάσει σοι). *para vós* after the verb (the stylist heard *vos celebrará* as 'celebrates you'); MS1932 *vos fará festa* option. |
| vovére (*Vovéte, et réddite*) | fazer votos (*Fazei votos, e pagai-os*) | open | Ps 75:12 (εὔξασθε καὶ ἀπόδοτε; the Offertory of Pentecost XVII). The noun *votos* as with *vota réddere* (réddere row); *votai* (a vote) avoided. |
| ex tunc (*ex tunc ira tua*) | desde então (*desde então, a vossa ira*) | open | Ps 75:8b (ἀπὸ τότε), verbless as the Latin; the ambiguity reader heard a fragment, as the Latin is; *desde o tempo da vossa ira* and MS1932 *no momento da vossa ira* options. |
| apud (of persons, not *apud te / me*: *terríbili apud reges terræ*) | diante de (*ao terrível diante dos reis da terra*) | open | Ps 75:13 (παρά). The stylist heard MS1932's *para os reis* as 'in the kings' opinion'; *diante* taken in draft 2; *para os*, *junto dos*, *entre os* options. |
| viri divitiárum | os homens de riquezas (*todos os homens de riquezas nada encontraram nas suas mãos*) | open | Ps 75:6 (ἄνδρες τοῦ πλούτου); the Hebraism kept (*os homens ricos* option). Subject before *nada encontraram*: with the Latin's order *nas suas mãos* joins the riches (the stylist's order an option). |
| mirabíliter | maravilhosamente | open | Ps 44:5b, 75:5 (θαυμαστῶς). The stylist asked *admiravelmente* at 75:5 (lighter) — refused, admirábilis's word; an option. |
| ascéndere equos | montar cavalos (*os que montaram cavalos*) | open | Ps 75:7 (οἱ ἐπιβεβηκότες τοὺς ἵππους). The perfect, as the v1 Latinist asked (MS1932 *montavam*); *equos* kept as the object. |
| a móntibus ætérnis | dos montes eternos | open | Ps 75:5 (ἀπὸ ὀρέων αἰωνίων). Draft 1's *desde os montes* was heard as temporal; the stylist's *dos* taken. MS1932 *do alto dos* option. |

## Formulas (identical wherever the Latin is identical)

| Latin | Portuguese | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| *Quóniam in sǽculum misericórdia ejus* | *porque é para sempre a sua misericórdia* — proposed by Ps 117 in place of *porque para sempre é a sua misericórdia* | settled (D14; D23 gives Ps 135's *in ætérnum* refrain the same words) — Ps 117 said the old form four times running (117:1–4): *porque para* stutters and *sempre é a sua* piles three vowels. The new form has the same words with the copula moved up, still closes on *misericórdia*, and passed the stylist without remark; it gives Ps 135 a twin of the same build (*porque é … a sua misericórdia*). Options in Ps 117's decision `refrain` (one slot, five verses); natural order *porque a sua misericórdia é para sempre* is the third |
| *Dicat nunc Israël quóniam bonus: quóniam…* (117:2–4; *dicat nunc Israël* also 123:1, 128:1 → *diga agora Israel*) | *Diga agora Israel: porque ele é bom, porque…* — the refrain quoted with its conjunction, identical in all five verses | open — DRB and MS1932 read *quóniam* as 'that' (*que ele é bom: que…*), which is the option; Ps 117's decision `dicat` |
| *et factus est / es mihi in salútem* (117:14, 117:21, 117:28b; the canticles with ids 15:2, 12:3) | *e se fez salvação para mim* / *e vos fizestes salvação para mim* | open (hangs on *fíeri*; word order from the stylist) |
| *et in nómine Dómini quia ultus sum in eos* (117:10–12) | *e foi em nome do Senhor que me vinguei deles* | working — a cleft, so that *quia* is heard as its *que*; *deles* follows the Latin's *eos* (after *gentes*) and avoids *delas* falling after *abelhas* in 117:12 |
| *Benedíctus qui venit in nómine Dómini* (117:26a) | *Bendito o que vem em nome do Senhor* | working — the wording of MS1932 and DM1962 alike |
| *Hæc est dies, quam fecit Dóminus: exsultémus, et lætémur in ea* (117:24) | *Este é o dia que o Senhor fez: exultemos e alegremo-nos nele* | working — word for word DM1962 |
| *Confitémini Dómino quóniam bonus* | *Dai graças ao Senhor, porque ele é bom* (117:1, 117:29; D5) — until the 2026-09-21 review this cell said *Louvai o Senhor, porque ele é bom*, the pre-D5 verb | settled (D5); was: open (hangs on *confitéri*; see that row) |
| *confitébor nómini tuo* (53:8, 137:1b–2a) | *darei graças ao vosso nome* | settled (D5); was: open (hangs on *confitéri*); in 53:8 the vocative is moved — *darei graças, Senhor, ao vosso nome: porque é bom* — so that the neuter *bonum* is not heard as "the Lord is good" |
| *Deus, exáudi oratiónem meam* (53:4; cf. *Dómine, exáudi…*) | *Deus, escutai a minha oração* | working — the *Senhor* formula with the Latin's vocative |
| *Dómine, exáudi oratiónem meam* | *Senhor, escutai a minha oração* | working |
| *Miserére mei* | *Tende piedade de mim* | working Ps 50:3a *Tende piedade de mim, Deus* (bare vocative, as the psalter; *ó Deus* kept as option). |
| *Fílius meus es tu, ego hódie génui te* (2:7) | *Tu és meu Filho, eu hoje te gerei* | settled (D17); was: open — Ps 2: God speaks to the Son as *tu* (row in "Address and grammar"); natural order, with MS1932 and DM1962; the Latin's order *Meu Filho és tu* is the option; the stylist's best line |
| *Exsúrge, Dómine, salvum me fac, Deus meus* (3:7) | *levantai-vos, Senhor, salvai-me, meu Deus* | working — Ps 3 |
| *Dómini est salus* (3:9) | *Do Senhor é a salvação* | working — Ps 3; the Latin's order, which is also good Portuguese |
| *et Dóminus suscépit me* (117:13) = *quia Dóminus suscépit me* (3:6) | *(porque) o Senhor me amparou* | working — identical in both psalms |
| *secúndum verbum tuum* (118:25, 65, 107; Nunc dimittis) | *segundo a vossa palavra* | working — Ps 118:25 |
| *doce me justificatiónes tuas* (118:12, 26; reordered in 118:64, 68, 124, 135) | *ensinai-me os vossos preceitos* | working (hangs on *justificatiónes*) — Ps 118 |
| *judícia justítiæ tuæ* (118:7, 106, 160, 164) | *os juízos da vossa justiça* | working — Ps 118:7; **not** 118:62 *judícia justificatiónis tuæ*, a different Latin |
| *in toto corde (meo)* (118:2, 10, 34, 58, 69, 145) | *de todo o (meu) coração* | working — Ps 118 |
| *qui fecit cælum et terram* (113:23, 120:2, 123:8, 133:3, 145:5–6) | *que fez o céu e a terra* | working — Ps 133:3 puts *ele* before it only because *Sião* would otherwise be the antecedent |
| *Qui statis in domo Dómini, in átriis domus Dei nostri* (133:1b = 134:2) | *Que estais de pé na casa do Senhor, nos átrios da casa do nosso Deus* | working (hangs on *stare*) |
| *Benedícat te / tibi Dóminus ex Sion* (133:3, 127:5) | *O Senhor te bendiga de Sião* | open (hangs on *benedícere*, God → man) |
| *secúndum elóquium tuum* (118:41, 58, 76; 116, 133, 170 to come; 118:154 has *propter*, 118:169 *juxta*) | ***segundo o que dissestes*** — settled (D16), replacing *segundo o vosso dito* | The clause held on a second blind stylist pass (four of six places passed outright, two mended by word order). History: *segundo o vosso dito* was **tested in Ps 118:33–80 and refused by the stylist in all six places where the singular stands** ("a quoted phrase", "bookish", "guardar um dito is to keep a phrase in memory"; he asks for *palavra*). The blind reader understood it everywhere; the Latinist passed it. It stays in the text under D15 — for the main session to revisit; fall-backs *segundo a vossa promessa* (fails 118:67) or the merge into *palavra* (collapses this formula into *secúndum verbum tuum*, 7 verses away at 118:58 / 65). **118:116:** the formula drew no remark from the stylist (his own proposed line keeps it) nor from the blind reader; the Latinist (draft 10, minor) notes that *dissestes* dates an utterance the noun leaves timeless — D16's named cost **118:133, 170** (the formula) and **118:169** (*juxta* → *conforme o que dissestes*; the Latin varies the preposition and Portuguese has the plain pair *conforme / segundo*): no remark from the stylist on either of two readings, understood by the blind reader twice; the Latinist's minor (past tense) on drafts 13–14, silence on 15–16. **118:154 *propter elóquium tuum* → *pelo que dissestes*** (not *por causa do*, which echoed *a minha causa* in the same verse). |

| **Ps 13 / Ps 52, the doublet — what is shared word for word** (grep and side by side: `consult/parallels/ps013.md`, `ps052.md`; Clementine fetched `consult/bolls-VULG-19-13.json`, `-19-52.json`) | Ps 13 draft 2 wording, for Ps 52 to copy: **13:1a = 52:1** *Disse o insensato no seu coração: * Não há Deus.* · **13:3a = 52:4** *Todos se desviaram, juntos se tornaram inúteis: * não há quem faça o bem, não há nem sequer um.* · **13:5b = 52:6b** *ali tremeram de temor, onde não havia temor.* · **13:7a = 52:7a** *Quem dará de Sião a salvação de Israel?* · **13:7 end = 52:7 end** *… o cativeiro do seu povo, Jacó exultará, e Israel se alegrará.* **Shared in part:** 13:1b / 52:2 *Corromperam-se, e tornaram-se abomináveis … * não há quem faça o bem* (52: *nas suas iniquidades*, and no *não há nem sequer um*); 13:2 / 52:3 *Deus olhou do céu para os filhos dos homens, * para ver se há quem entenda, ou quem procure a Deus* (only Dóminus / Deus differ); 13:4 / 52:5 *Não hão de saber todos os que praticam a iniquidade, * os que devoram o meu povo como quem come pão?* (cognóscent / scient, sicut escam / ut cibum — one Greek each, so one Portuguese); 13:5a / 52:6a *Ao Senhor não invocaram* → *A Deus não invocaram*. **Not shared:** 13:3b–3d (not in Ps 52, nor in the Hebrew; in the Rahlfs LXX on disk and in Rom 3:13–18, fetched); 13:6 against 52:6b; *cum avérterit Dóminus* (→ *afastar*) against *cum convérterit Deus* (row *convértere*) | working — Ps 13; **Ps 52 copies** **Ps 52 done (draft 2):** every shared wording copied from Ps 13 draft 3 (checked against D1–D43: none ruled against since); decided locally only where the Latin differs — 52:2 *nas iniquidades*, 52:6 *A Deus não invocaram* (13:5's order kept for the twin), 52:6b whole, 52:7 *quando Deus fizer voltar o cativeiro*. Reader remarks on shared words (52:2 *fizeram-se*, 52:5 *alimento de pão* / *como se come o pão*, 52:7 the -ará pair) were refused to keep the twins identical: if Gustavo takes one, it goes into both psalms. |

D40 stays open. **Ps 69:2 translated with 37:23's wording held: *Deus, atendei em meu auxílio: * Senhor, apressai-vos para auxiliar-me*** — D40 still unruled, so the holding ruling keeps the two psalms one. The v1 stylist failed *atender em* again ('not Portuguese', asked *voltai-vos em*, refused: convértere's verb); the ambiguity reader understood a plea to come and help; the Latinist passed it twice. Options in Ps 69's decision `intende`: *acudi*, *vinde* (DM1962, the line Brazilians know), *atendei ao*, *voltai-vos*. **D40 is now the one open question in Ps 69.** |

Exsúrge … memor esto oppróbrii servórum tuórum* also in Quad4-4) | *Olhai, Senhor, para a vossa aliança, e não abandoneis para sempre as almas dos vossos pobres: levantai-vos, Senhor, e julgai a vossa causa, e não esqueçais as vozes dos que vos buscam* | open — Ps 73; the Missal's *derelínquas* (the derelínquere row) and *quæréntium te* are not the psalter's *obliviscáris* and *inimicórum tuórum* |
| *Quam bonus Israël Deus, his, qui recto sunt corde!* (72:1; Graduals 02-27, 03-27, 06-04 and the Tract of Palm Sunday, Quad6-0, with 72:2–3) · *Tenuísti manum déxteram meam: et in voluntáte tua deduxísti me: et cum glória assumpsísti me* (72:24; Gradual 03-27, Quad6-0) | *Como Deus é bom para Israel, * para os que são retos de coração!* · *Tomastes a minha mão direita: e pela vossa vontade me guiastes, * e com glória me acolhestes* — the Missal's *assumpsísti* would need its own verb | open — Ps 72 |
| *Deus cordis mei, et pars mea Deus in ætérnum* (72:26b; Alleluia 06-04, 10-17; responsory SanctiM Pent02-6b) · *Mihi autem adhærére Deo bonum est: pónere in Dómino Deo spem meam* (72:28; versicles 05-13, Urbis 07-17, Pent02-6b) | *Deus do meu coração, * e a minha parte é Deus para sempre* · *Mas para mim é bom apegar-me a Deus: * pôr no Senhor Deus a minha esperança* | open — Ps 72; the copula is supplied only in the second member of 72:26b |

## Doublets

13 / 52 · 39:14–18 / 69 · 56:8–12 + 59:7–14 / 107. Whoever translates the second copies the first wherever the Latin is identical, and says so in `audit`.
